# Feature Specification: Cryptographic Architecture and phyco-v3 Design

> This feature makes no security claim. No PhycoWeb-PRNG model, `phyco-v3` included, may be used
> to generate secrets; use an established cryptographic random-number generator for keys,
> nonces, tokens and salts. Passing statistical tests does not show cryptographic security.

**Feature Branch**: `004-crypto-architecture-v3`

**Created**: 2026-11-01

**Status**: Spec and plan approved (2026-11-01); checklist choices of the first run, the re-run and
the second re-run answered and applied (2026-11-01); checklist choices of the third re-run answered
and applied (2026-11-02); checklist choices of the fourth re-run answered in advance and applied
(2026-11-02); checklist choices of the fifth re-run answered in advance and applied (2026-11-02);
checklist choices of the sixth re-run answered in advance and applied (2026-11-02); checklist
choices of the seventh re-run answered in advance and applied (2026-11-02); checklist choices of the
eighth re-run answered in advance and applied (2026-11-02); checklist choices of the ninth re-run
answered in advance (2026-11-02) and applied (2026-11-03); checklist choices of the tenth re-run
answered in advance (2026-11-02) and applied (2026-11-03), the last round of the loop by the owner's

**Input**: User description (verbatim):

> Cryptographic redesign of PhycoWeb-PRNG, part 1: the security target, a permutation-based
> architecture and the phyco-v3 design, up to a frozen design. This feature makes no security
> claim; it prepares the evidence a later claim would need (constitution 1.2.0, Security Policy).
> It aims at claim level L1 and starts L2 (security.md section 15).
>
> Why: phyco-v2 cannot support any cryptographic claim. Its 64-bit seed caps security at 64 bits
> (security.md S1-S3), its step merges states (S13), its veins are confined and biased (S12,
> S14), a 2x2 ring that keeps its vein update cannot reach the 2^256 secret states FUT-SEC-2
> requires for a 128-bit claim (S15), and its mixing came from added terms rather than a designed
> component (S11).
>
> Scope, in order:
>
> 1. Reproduce phyco-v2's 64-bit seed bound on a reduced seed domain before any new key
>    interface is implemented (FUT-SEC-8).
> 2. Specify the security target as requirements, not claims: the properties sought
>    (indistinguishability and backtracking resistance; prediction resistance only with a
>    validated entropy source, otherwise out of scope), a 128-bit target strength, key and nonce
>    sizes, output limits per key and per key and nonce, a threat model extended for keys,
>    nonces, multiple users and state compromise, and the generic bounds every parameter must
>    respect.
> 3. Specify a keyless, bio-inspired permutation on a parameterised grid (FUT-GRID-1 to
>    FUT-GRID-5): bijective by construction with a specified inverse; fixed operations only (no
>    data-dependent control or shift amounts); full-range state words; round constants and
>    rotations generated by stated rules that break grid symmetry and never repeat inside a
>    call; at most one or two 32-bit carry chains per pipeline stage; and a round count fixed by
>    a pre-registered rule from diffusion, proven trail bounds and a margin. Document the
>    slime-mould mapping, but rest no security argument on it.
> 4. Specify a mode with a published security proof that reduces the generator to the
>    permutation (keyed duplex recommended: instantiate, generate, forget, optional reseed),
>    with padding and domain separation, and state which grid sizes are claimable, deciding the
>    FUT-SEC-2 and FUT-GRID-2 conflict at 2x2 (security.md section 20.1); 2x2 is expected to
>    become a reduced research variant.
> 5. Define reduced variants (word widths 8, 16 and 32, fewer rounds, small grids) from the same
>    generics, which the production interface refuses.
> 6. Deliver: a design document with its design rationale (security.md section 8); a Python
>    reference model of the permutation, its inverse and the mode; golden vectors and
>    known-answer tests per size; structural evidence (inverse tests, exhaustive bijectivity on
>    the smallest variant, exact diffusion in both directions, per-word bias, symmetry checks on
>    the constants); and first proven or solver-computed trail bounds on reduced rounds, from
>    tools first validated on known results, including re-finding S12-S15 on phyco-v2.
> 7. Use development keys only, log every candidate design, archive phyco-v2 in baseline/
>    (Principle V), and end with an owner design review, an external design review if one can
>    be arranged (security.md section 7), and the design freeze.
>
> Carry FUT-SEC-1 to FUT-SEC-9 as requirements traced to tasks and evidence (Security Policy);
> FUT-SEC-7's held-out statistical programme is delivered by a later feature and stays open in
> this feature's report. Re-decide every carried-over feature-002 requirement (FR-001 to FR-009),
> including the FR-007 rule on standard primitives and standard modes. Out of scope: RTL, the
> held-out statistical programme, the full cryptanalysis campaign, external review, publication
> and any security claim. Inputs: research/security/crypto-redesign-strategy.md, security.md 2.9
> (sections 3, 5, 8, 9.2, 12, 15, 20.1 and 21), and research/security/seed-entropy-bound.md
> section 5 with the section 5.3 checklist groups A to G.

**Update input** (2026-11-01), user description (verbatim):

> consider all text and items in crypto-redesign-strategy.md, security.md, seed-entropy-bound.md,
> and vhdl-genai.md to develop feature 004 specs

This revision takes every section and item of those four documents into account. Each is a
requirement below or has a recorded disposition: a later feature, the owner, or out of scope
(section "Input coverage").

## Context

`phyco-v2` (feature 002; `design.md` 1.0, SHA-256 `a1109bf1…`; `phycoweb/model_v2.py`, SHA-256
`1260142d…`) has passed every statistical attempt it was given: SP 800-22 attempt 1 in feature
002 and battery attempt 1 (PractRand and TestU01) in feature 003. It can pass a test and still
not keep a secret. security.md §9.2 records why it cannot support any cryptographic claim:

- **S1–S3**: its seed is 64 bits, so no security level can exceed 64 bits; trying every seed
  costs about 2⁷² generator steps.
- **S13**: its step is not one-to-one. States merge through the vein decay and through a zero
  mode of the transport.
- **S12, S14**: every vein stays in 0…2³¹, and the top bits of each vein are strongly biased.
- **S15**: that bound holds from every starting state, so a 2×2 ring that keeps the design-1.0
  vein update reaches at most about 2²⁵² secret states, short of the 2²⁵⁶ that FUT-SEC-2 requires
  for a 128-bit claim.
- **S11**: its statistical quality came from two added ARX terms, not from a designed component.

This feature starts the route the strategy note recommends (option A2,
[`crypto-redesign-strategy.md`](../../research/security/crypto-redesign-strategy.md) §4): a new
keyless, bio-inspired permutation Π on a parameterised grid, used in a mode whose published
security proof reduces the generator to Π. The default mode is a keyed duplex. The feature ends
with a frozen design, `phyco-v3`, or with a recorded decision to stop (option C, security.md
§21).

**This feature makes no security claim.** Under the constitution's Security Policy, no
cryptographic claim may be made, and no evaluation meant to support one pursued, until FUT-SEC-1
to FUT-SEC-9 are met. Design work and the search for weaknesses are allowed, and their results
must be reported (security.md §15). Everything this spec asks of the design (the security
target, its parameters, its bounds, the mode) is a requirement the design must meet, not a
statement that it does. In this feature `phyco-v3` can reach claim level L1 (security target
specified); L2 is started but not reached, because the internal attack campaign and the
reduced-round statistical margin belong to feature 005.

**This feature changes no existing generator.** `phyco-v2`, `lut-v1`, harness `sp80022` 1.0.0,
the feature-002 and feature-003 protocols, their seed sets and every frozen file stay as they
are. `phyco-v2` is copied byte for byte into `baseline/` (Principle V). The live files are not
moved, because the freeze records of features 002 and 003 hash them.

**External review.** An external design review before the freeze is in scope if one can be
arranged (security.md §7). The independent review of claim level L5 belongs to feature 009 and
is out of scope. The review asks for comments on a design that makes no claim. It is not an
evaluation intended to support a claim, which the Security Policy defers, and is never cited as
one; the review package (FR-063) carries the disclaimer and says so.

**The rules that select the design are fixed in this spec before any design result exists**
(section "Pre-registered Design Protocol"). After approval they may only become stricter
(Principle IV).

**Inputs.** The four documents this spec answers to are the strategy note
([`crypto-redesign-strategy.md`](../../research/security/crypto-redesign-strategy.md)),
[`security.md`](../../security.md) 2.9, the seed-entropy note
([`seed-entropy-bound.md`](../../research/security/seed-entropy-bound.md)) and the hardware
note ([`vhdl-genai.md`](../../research/hardware/vhdl-genai.md)). The hardware note's rules for
AI-assisted RTL bind features 006 and 008, but three of them reach back into this feature: the
Python model is the only oracle, the oracle and its corner-case vectors exist before any RTL,
and the design document leaves an implementer, human or model, nothing to guess.

- Q: Commit ffcee2b said that a run ended by a reboot, a power loss or an update takes no re-run,
  and the eighteenth `/speckit-analyze` report of 2026-11-03 found that one such end during the
  FUT-SEC-8 sweep or a uniform control can then end the whole feature in a stop, and one during a
  validation leaves that tool unvalidated. May such a run go again, and where do its charged
  hours stop? → A: Any run may, and its hours stop at the reboot (owner, 2026-11-03): "Yes, any
  run (Recommended)" and "At the reboot (Recommended)". Commit 57763b5 applies the answers: such a
  run is no tool defect and gives no result, its partial outputs are discarded unread, its hours
  stand up to the boot, and it may run again on the same inputs, which spends no re-run, its
  row's hours bounding how often; a FUT-SEC-8 repeat still needs the owner's request (research
  R7). Under the reading that a run may be repeated only after a tool defect, one such end could
  leave every candidate without a conclusive control and the feature with no demonstration, so
  the first entry made from that commit's places marks that reading `set_aside`, with that case,
  for the owner's decision in the Review & Approval row "Readings set aside", which now names
  nineteen such entries (checklist-choices.md, beside the tenth re-run's list; PRO-063, PRO-079).

| Final result review (phase report) | _pending_ | _pending_ | _pending_ | The owner's commit that records this row's decision ends FR-043's window, a stop that the final report recommends (FR-052) ending it at the same commit: inside the window every commit of a file of this feature is the owner's, security.md, README, `.gitignore`, `.gitattributes`, `requirements.txt` and `protocol/claims.json` included, and the standing authorization covers them again from the next commit, for the features that follow (PRO-080) |

A repair of a broken log chain (research R16) needs a row of its own in this table, "Log repair
<n>", added when the break is found: it names the log, the `seq` of the break and the date, and the
owner sets it to Approved and commits it. `scripts/repair_log.py --approval` names that row, and the
script refuses the repair unless the row reads Approved at HEAD and names the same log and position.

An environment record (research R1) and a value the owner provides for a tool's validation (research
R19) each need a row of their own too, "Environment record <n>" or "Owner-provided value <n>", added
when the owner writes the record or the entry with `scripts/owner_record.py`, which
prints its approval hash, kind `canonical`, the SHA-256 of its canonical
JSON without the approval fields (data-model introduction; REP-083): the row
names the platform or the known result, that hash and the date, and the owner
sets it to Approved and commits it. The evidence scripts' environment guard accepts a record in
force only while its row reads Approved at HEAD with that hash, and `validate_tool.py`'s source
guard accepts an `owner-provided` entry only while its row does, as `repair_log.py` reads "Log
repair <n>" (cli contract, "Environment", `validate_tool.py`).

A file that the row "Tool acquisition" does not cover (research R19), the source of a refused set
that no listed file gives (research R15) or another copy of a paper among them, needs a row of its
own too, "Download <n>", added when the owner gives the permission for that file: it names the
file, under a name that no entry of the downloads manifest holds (research R19), its source, its
size and its licence, the listed file it stands in for, if any, and the date, and the owner sets
it to Approved and commits it. `scripts/downloads.py approve` reads that row at HEAD and writes
the file's `approved`
entry in the downloads manifest only while the row reads Approved and names the file with those
four values, as `repair_log.py` reads "Log repair <n>" (FR-046; cli contract, `downloads.py`).

Two lists of rows are set by the owner and committed, as "Log repair <n>" is. The first holds the
rows a guard reads at HEAD, each with its guard: the FUT-SEC-8 result review and
each "FUT-SEC-8 result review <k>", read by the evidence scripts' guard (cli
contract, "Order of US1"); the D4 tie, read by `round_rule.py --select`, which
exits 2 after a D4 tie until the row reads Approved with the choice record's
SHA-256, where the tie-breaks reached the owner's choice, or "Not needed",
naming the tie record and its tie-break, where one decided it; the Selection tie and each "Selection
tie <k>" of a later selection record, read the same way by `design_freeze.py create` and by
`round_rule.py --decide`, which exits 2 while the latest selection record has a
tie and no choice whose row reads Approved with the choice record's SHA-256
(PRO-074); the Security-target review and each "Security-target review <k>", the
Owner design review and each "Owner design review <k>", and the External design review and each
"External design review <k>", read by `design_freeze.py create` in the rows held for the candidate
that the latest selection record selects, the Owner design review's rows also read by
`candidate_log.py outcome` in the row with the highest k, which exits 2 on an outcome that row does
not record (ANA-083); the rows that a review entry of the provenance log names, the Security-target
review's and the Owner design review's families among them, read by `provenance.py review`, which
refuses an entry whose `row` does not read, with its `reviewed_sha256`, the decision the entry
records (RAT-077; PRO-074); L1 recorded and each "L1 recorded <k>", read by `check_claims.py` in
the row with the highest k, which fails, exit 1, while the register's level for `phyco-v3` differs
from the one those rows give, L1 while that row reads Approved and names the highest-k row of
each review family, both Approved, with every hash those review rows name
matching the file at HEAD (SEC-079), and L0 otherwise; each "Log repair <n>",
read by `repair_log.py`; each "Environment record <n>", read by the evidence scripts' environment
guard (cli contract, "Environment"); each "Owner-provided value <n>", read by `validate_tool.py`'s
source guard; each "Download <n>", read by `downloads.py approve`; the Place check and each
"Place check <k>", read by `protocol_change.py create` in the row with the highest k (FR-044); the
Readings set aside, read by
`protocol_change.py create`, which exits 2 while a first entry of the protocol change log
holds a reading marked `set_aside` that the row does not decide (FR-044;
PRO-079); and the Second transcription go-ahead, read by the test of the
transcription's provenance. The second holds the rows that record an owner's own act that no guard
reads: the Design freeze. A test checks that the first list, its numbered families included,
and the contracts' guards agree both ways. Any other approval is written
by the agent only on the owner's explicit word for that row, quoted with
its date in the row's notes. Every guard that reads this table reads it as
committed at HEAD and exits 2 while the working tree's table differs from HEAD's (cli contract,
"Approvals"). The agent commits under the owner's standing authorization (constitution 1.3.0,
Principle IX), and its commits carry no AI co-author line, so the history cannot show who wrote or
committed a row; the owner's review at each ⛔ gate therefore lists the approval commits since the
last gate, and the `allow_list` entries of the claim register added since, each with its reason,
which `check_claims.py` prints, an entry the owner declines being removed in the next commit
(FR-069; SEC-087). From the first commit that records an owner design review's
approval to the feature's end, plan phase 9 included, which the owner's commit
that records the decision of the row "Final result review" ends (FR-043;
PRO-080), every commit of a file of this feature is the owner's, a later
row "Owner design review <k>" and the change it reviews included; where the owner commits a row on
its own between that approval and the freeze's step (1), "Environment record <n>" or "Log repair
<n>", the owner design review is held again in "Owner design review <k>", naming the same versions
where nothing else changed, and step (1) follows that row's approval (FR-043; SEC-084; PRO-072). The
agent writes the owner design review's review entries right after the commit that records that
review's approval, before the owner sets any row in the working tree for step (1), the external
review's row among them, leaving them in the working tree, since `provenance.py review` exits 2
while the working tree's table differs from HEAD's, and the owner sets step (1)'s rows only once
those entries stand, so that step (1)'s commit holds both (FR-043; PRO-075). The standing
authorization covers the files of this feature again from the commit after the one that ends the
window, for the features that follow (PRO-080).

A decline is logged with its reasons and ends, at each gate, in one of these outcomes. At the
FUT-SEC-8 result review: a corrected demonstration report, as a new version reviewed in a row of its
own, with a defect fixed and the sweep repeated under `--repeat-after-defect`, on the
owner's request, where the review finds one; or a stop; key loading stays
refused (cli contract, "Order of US1") until such a row reads Approved. At the
security-target review: a revised chapter, as a new version reviewed in a row of
its own, its security-target parameters changing only in their stricter direction (FR-044); or a
stop; the owner design review does not start, and `design_freeze.py create` exits 2, while the
security-target review is not Approved. At the owner design review: the outcomes its row and FR-043
state, each logged in a `review-outcome` entry of the candidate log, which `candidate_log.py
outcome` writes after the commit that records the row's decision (data-model §3; ANA-083). At any
other gate: a revision of what it reviews, reviewed again in a row of its own, or a
stop.

A review held again, after a decline, for the changed parts after an approval, or for another
candidate that a later selection record selects, is a row of its own, added when it is held:
"FUT-SEC-8 result review <k>", "Security-target review <k>" or "Owner design review <k>", with
k = 2, 3, … in order, the unnumbered row being the first. It names what it reviews by path and
SHA-256: the demonstration record, the chapter's version, or the design document's version with the
selection record and `rationale.md`, whose SHA-256 `design_freeze.py create` compares with the file
at HEAD, exiting 2 while they differ, so that a change to `rationale.md` after the review's
approval counts only once an "Owner design review <k>" naming the changed
file reads Approved, as a change to the design document does (RAT-083). The
chapter's version is a set, which the security-target review's row names and
`design_freeze.py create` compares item by item: the SHA-256 of the text of each chapter the review
covers, the design document's chapters "Security target" and "Mode" (FR-034) and `rationale.md`'s
chapters of those names, which the review also reads, though their review entries are the owner
design review's (RAT-086), each from its heading line to the next heading of the
same or a higher level, or to the file's end, over its raw bytes (research R16), and the SHA-256 of
`security-target/parameters.json` and `limits.json`, the `verified` and met marks among them, with
the design document's version the review read, from its line "Version: 0.<k>". A change to any of
these hashes after the review, a mark included, counts only once a "Security-target review <k>"
naming the changed set reads Approved: until then `create` exits 2, since the row no longer names
what the record freezes, while a change elsewhere in the design document or in `rationale.md` leaves
that review standing. "L1 recorded" and "L1 recorded <k>" name the same set (SEC-075). Each
guard reads the row of its family with the highest k at HEAD, which must
review what the guard's run cites (cli contract, "Approvals"). A row's Decision holds one of the
values data-model §17 lists for it, and each guard opens only on the values that section names for
it. L1 rests on two of these families: "L1 recorded" names the FUT-SEC-8 result review's and the
security-target review's rows it rests on, by family and k, and once a later row of either family is
added, L1 is recorded again in a row of its own, "L1 recorded <k>", k = 2, 3, … in order, when the
later rows read Approved, naming them; until then the register keeps `phyco-v3` at L0 (FR-012,
FR-069). The agent writes the register's change, `phyco-v3`'s entry in `protocol/claims.json` and
its line in security.md §15, in a commit of its own right after the owner's commit that sets "L1
recorded" or "L1 recorded <k>" to Approved, or that adds a later row of either review family, which
makes L1 lapse, and never before that commit, save inside FR-043's window, where the owner's commit
holds it (SEC-084), and `check_claims.py` fails while the register's level differs from the one the
rows give, in either direction (SEC-076). L1 holds at HEAD only while what
its rows name holds there: `check_claims.py` compares with the files at HEAD each hash named by the
review rows that the "L1 recorded" row with the highest k names, the chapter's version item by item
and the demonstration record with its report, and gives L0 while any differs, so that a change to
the reviewed set makes L1 lapse from the commit that makes it, as a later row does; the agent
writes the register's return to L0 in that commit, or, where an owner's commit
makes the change, in a commit of its own right after it, `history` keeping
the earlier L1 with its dates, its rows and the version reviewed, until "L1 recorded <k>" records L1
again (SEC-079). FR-043's window, in which the agent commits no file of this feature,
`protocol/claims.json` and security.md among them, runs from the first commit that records an owner
design review's approval to the feature's end; inside it the agent writes each of these changes in
the working tree, once the owner has set the row or made the change there,
and the owner's commit holds it, so that at every commit of the window the
register gives the rows' level (SEC-084). Once the external design review
is done, its row names the review package's manifest it read by SHA-256, and the owner may have
later changes reviewed in a row of its own, "External design review <k>", which names its manifest
the same way (FR-043, FR-063; SEC-077). Each row of the external review's family names the selection
record it applies to, a done row through its manifest, which carries that record, and a "not
arranged" row by naming the latest selection record when it is written; `design_freeze.py create`
reads the highest-k row that names the latest selection record and exits 2 while none does, so that
a new selection record needs a row of its own, done or "not arranged", for the candidate it selects,
and a review done for another candidate stays done in its row and is cited, in the freeze record's
"External review" and in the final report, for the candidate and the version it read only (SC-013;
SEC-078).

This table is the source of the feature's status. The Status line at the top of this spec names
every round of checklist choices the table holds, with its state, and the Status of
checklist-choices.md follows the table too; each is updated in the same commit as the table row or
the answered round it reports, and a test compares the three.

## Decisions Taken as Defaults

The strategy note (§8.4) leaves nine decisions to the owner. This spec adopted the recommended
option of each as its default, so that every requirement below is definite. D10 records which of
the note's further ideas (§10) the spec adopts. On 2026-11-01 the owner confirmed D1–D9 as
written, and D10 with its stop rule changed to a budget of three candidates per family (section
"Clarifications"). A later change is made in this spec before any result it affects.

| ID | Decision | Choice | Alternative | Used in | Status |
|----|----------|--------|-------------|---------|--------|
| D1 | Mode | Keyed duplex: instantiate, generate, forget, optional reseed | Counter mode with feed-forward and fast key erasure | FR-013 | Confirmed 2026-11-01 |
| D2 | Key and nonce | 256-bit key; 128-bit nonce, used once per key | 128-bit key with a 128-bit nonce | FR-006 | Confirmed 2026-11-01 |
| D3 | Claimable sizes (FUT-SEC-2 and FUT-GRID-2 at 2×2) | Claimable from 4×4; 2×2 and 3×3 are reduced research variants outside every claim | Wider nodes, for example four 32-bit lanes, so that 2×2 holds 512 bits | FR-017, FR-018 | Confirmed 2026-11-01 |
| D4 | Nonlinearity | ARX and Boolean both explored on reduced widths with development keys; one chosen by the pre-registered choice rule | ARX only, or Boolean only | FR-022 | Confirmed 2026-11-01 |
| D5 | Frustrated Foraging | Removed from Π; the food forcing becomes round-constant injection at food nodes | Kept as a fixed, not data-triggered, schedule and analysed | FR-031 | Confirmed 2026-11-01 |
| D6 | Feature-002 FR-007 | Standard modes allowed; published primitives, their components and their constants forbidden | Feature-002 FR-007 unchanged | FR-047 | Confirmed 2026-11-01 |
| D7 | Prediction resistance | Not sought until a validated entropy source exists | A separate entropy-source feature | FR-004, FR-016 | Confirmed 2026-11-01 |
| D8 | Certification route (FUT-SEC-9) | Independent academic cryptanalysis plus an AIS 20/31-style evaluation; the functionality class confirmed after AIS 20/31 version 3.0 (2024) is read | Another named scheme | FR-011 | Confirmed 2026-11-01 |
| D9 | External expertise | The owner design review is required; the owner seeks an external design review, recorded as done or not arranged; the L5 reviewers are named in feature 009 | Reviewers named now | FR-043 | Confirmed 2026-11-01 |
| D10 | Ideas of strategy note §10 adopted here | The claim and evidence register with its test (FR-069), a start-up self-test defined for the RTL (FR-059), a 64-bit ARX-box among the ARX candidates (FR-022) and a pre-registered stop rule with a budget of three candidates per family; the other ideas go to later features or stay out of scope (section "Input coverage") | Any subset of these | FR-022, FR-059, FR-069 | Confirmed 2026-11-01; the stop rule changed to a candidate budget |

## User Scenarios & Testing *(mandatory)*

Users: (a) the repository owner, who decides whether `phyco-v3` goes on to cryptanalysis
(feature 005) or the route stops; (b) reviewers, the owner now and an external cryptographer if
one can be arranged, who need a complete design rationale and a review package; (c) the later
implementers of the C stream and the VHDL (features 006 and 008), people or AI drafting tools
whose drafts deterministic tools judge, who need a design with nothing left to guess and an
oracle to check against; (d) researchers, who must be able to reproduce every figure.

### User Story 1 - The 64-bit seed bound of phyco-v2, reproduced (Priority: P1)

The owner sees `phyco-v2`'s 64-bit bound demonstrated as an executed attack rather than a
counting argument. With two of the four seed words given to the attacker, all 2³² candidates
for the other two are tried, the seed is recovered from three output words, and the cost per
candidate is measured and scaled to the full 2⁶⁴ seeds. This happens before any key interface
for `phyco-v3` is implemented, and it is the first validation of the analysis tooling on a
known result.

**Why this priority**: FUT-SEC-8 puts it first. It needs no new design, it turns S3 from a
generic argument into a reproduced result, and it validates the search tool before any negative
result from the tooling is allowed to count.

**Independent Test**: Run the pre-registered demonstration on its development targets. Check
that each recovered seed equals its target, that the false matches agree with the random
baseline, and that the cost is recorded with its commands and hashes. Agreement is tested on the
one-word false-match count of each target, against a Poisson distribution of mean
(2³² − 1)/2³², and on the three targets pooled, against three times that mean, two-sided at level
0.01; any false match after two or three words is reported as a deviation. A disagreement is a
finding (FR-060), analysed as the protocol says, and never a reason to sweep again.

**Acceptance Scenarios**:

1. **Given** `phyco-v2` (`phycoweb/model_v2.py`, SHA-256 `1260142d…`) and a development target
   seed whose first two words are revealed, **When** every one of the 2³² candidates for the
   last two words is tested against the first three output words, **Then** the target is among
   the matches and every match is listed.
2. **Given** the sweep, **When** its record is read, **Then** it gives the matches after one, two
   and three words beside their expected numbers, the cost per candidate in generator steps and
   in time, the total cost, and the extrapolation to all 2⁶⁴ seeds.
3. **Given** the search tool, **When** it is checked before the sweep, **Then** its output
   matches the `phyco-v2` golden vectors word for word.
4. **Given** the demonstration report, **When** the owner has reviewed it, **Then** the review is
   recorded before any `phyco-v3` instantiation (key-loading) code exists.

---

### User Story 2 - A security target the design must meet (Priority: P2)

The owner and any reviewer get the security target as requirements: the properties sought, the
128-bit target strength, the key, nonce and output limits, the threat model extended to keys,
nonces, many users and state compromise, the mode and the theorem it relies on, and a check of
every parameter against every generic bound at every supported grid size. Each FUT-SEC
requirement is traced to where it is met, or to the feature that will meet it. None of it is a
claim.

**Why this priority**: L1 comes before everything after it. The target fixes what the design must
achieve before the design exists, so the design cannot be judged against a target chosen after
the fact.

**Independent Test**: Without running any code, a reviewer checks FUT-SEC-1 to FUT-SEC-9,
threats T1–T11 and the generic bounds of security.md §12.2 against the parameter table and the
mode chapter of the design document.

**Acceptance Scenarios**:

1. **Given** the security target, **When** FUT-SEC-1 to FUT-SEC-6 and FUT-SEC-9 are checked,
   **Then** each is met by specification, with the section that meets it cited; FUT-SEC-8 points
   to User Story 1, and FUT-SEC-7 points to feature 007 and is open.
2. **Given** each supported size, **When** the parameter table is read, **Then** it states the
   key entropy, the secret state reachable from the key, the capacity, the rate, the round count and
   the resulting bound, and every size that fails a generic bound is a research variant; the table
   and the chapter's output limits restate `parameters.json` and `limits.json`, and a test compares
   them value by value (FR-034; RAT-085).
3. **Given** threats T1–T11, **When** the threat model is read, **Then** each is adopted for
   `phyco-v3` or excluded with a reason.
4. **Given** the mode, **When** the theorem it relies on is checked, **Then** every precondition
   of the theorem is listed with the design feature that meets it, and the output limits follow
   from the theorem's bound.

---

### User Story 3 - The permutation, its inverse and the mode, specified and modelled (Priority: P3)

Implementers and reviewers get a design document for the keyless permutation Π on an n×n grid,
its inverse Π⁻¹ and the mode. It states the width, signedness and shift kind of every operation,
documents the slime-mould mapping, and generates every constant by a committed rule. A bit-exact
Python reference model implements Π, Π⁻¹ and the mode, with golden vectors and known-answer
tests for every supported size and for every reduced variant used as evidence, and with
constructed-state vectors for every edge state the design document lists. A second
transcription, written from the design document alone, checks that the document reads only one
way. The reduced variants come from the same generics, and the production interface refuses
them.

**Why this priority**: This is the design itself. It takes its parameters from the security
target (User Story 2), and the analysis (User Story 4) needs it as its object.

**Independent Test**: Regenerate every vector and known-answer test from the model on two
platforms and compare them; regenerate every constant and every constructed-state vector from
the committed scripts; compare the second transcription with the model step by step; request
every reduced variant from the production interface and check that each is refused.

**Acceptance Scenarios**:

1. **Given** the model and a supported size, **When** the known-answer tests run, **Then** Π, Π⁻¹
   and the mode reproduce every committed vector on Windows and on Linux.
2. **Given** the committed constant-generation script, **When** it runs, **Then** it reproduces
   every round constant and rotation amount of every supported size and word width.
3. **Given** the production interface, **When** a reduced variant (a smaller width, fewer
   rounds, a research grid size or a reduced capacity) is requested, **Then** the request is
   refused with an error.
4. **Given** the design document, **When** an implementer looks up any operation, **Then** its
   width, signedness, wrap rule, shift kind and place in the update order are stated, and the
   edge states of the model are listed.
5. **Given** the edge states the design document lists, **When** the constructed-state script
   runs, **Then** it reproduces a committed vector for every one of them, and those vectors are
   frozen with the others.
6. **Given** a second transcription written from the design document alone, **When** it and the
   model run on the vectors step by step, **Then** they agree at every step, and the common-mode
   caveat is stated beside the result.

---

### User Story 4 - Structural evidence and first trail bounds from validated tools (Priority: P4)

The owner gets the first evidence about `phyco-v3` that owes nothing to statistics: Π⁻¹ undoes
Π, the smallest variant is a bijection over every input, diffusion is computed exactly in both
directions, the bias of every state word is measured, the constants have no grid symmetry, and
the first proven or solver-computed trail bounds hold on reduced rounds. Every tool whose
results count has first re-found known results: S12–S15 and the FUT-SEC-8 recovery on
`phyco-v2`, and a published optimal trail. The round count follows from the pre-registered
rule.

**Why this priority**: These are the structural proofs and first bounds of L2, and they fix the
round count. They need the design (User Story 3).

**Independent Test**: Run the re-check of FR-065, which recomputes every verdict from the saved
outputs, and check that each tool's validation record is older than its first counted result.
Full re-runs are reproductions, listed per kind with their cost: each kind is reproduced, in a run
that was light by research R7's test, by its script's `--reproduce` mode, which runs the recorded
command, from a worktree of the original's commit, into a directory of its own, appends nothing
to the candidate log and compares as FR-053 defines (deterministic kinds
byte for byte, searches and solver runs by their values and verdicts), the kinds that
FR-061 leaves out excepted; a heavy run is reproduced only on the owner's
request, quoted with its date in the reproduction record, one at a time, as a
heavy run charged to research R7's feature-wide row, and the agent never starts one on its own.

**Acceptance Scenarios**:

1. **Given** every supported size and variant, **When** Π⁻¹(Π(x)) and Π(Π⁻¹(x)) are computed for
   the fixed and the development test inputs, **Then** both equal x in every case.
2. **Given** the smallest variant, **When** Π is applied to all of its inputs, **Then** no two
   inputs give the same output.
3. **Given** every supported size, **When** the exact bit-dependency matrices of Π and Π⁻¹ are
   computed round by round, **Then** R_f and R_b are reported.
4. **Given** an analysis tool, **When** its first counted result is recorded, **Then** its
   validation on a known result of the same kind is already recorded, with its version and
   licence.
5. **Given** the evidence at the freeze, **When** the round rule is applied, **Then** it gives
   the round count R of every claimable size, with every input of the rule cited.
6. **Given** any finding of this feature, **When** its record is read, **Then** it carries one of
   the four classes of security.md §9.1, and a negative result is worded as limited to its
   scope.

---

### User Story 5 - Design review and freeze (Priority: P5)

The owner reviews a complete design rationale, every candidate design that was considered with
the reason it was kept or dropped, and the evidence. An external cryptographer reviews it too,
if one can be arranged, from a review package prepared either way; no AI review stands in for
that review. The design is then frozen with its hashes, so that features 005 to 007 analyse
exactly one fixed object. `phyco-v2` is archived in `baseline/`, and the final report traces
FUT-SEC-1 to FUT-SEC-9 to their evidence, with FUT-SEC-7's statistical programme open.

**Why this priority**: It closes the feature, and it needs all of the evidence before it.

**Independent Test**: Check the rationale against every item of security.md §8 and the review
package against §14; compare the candidate log with the model's variant IDs; recompute the
freeze hashes and the baseline manifest; run the claim-register test.

**Acceptance Scenarios**:

1. **Given** the design rationale, **When** it is checked against security.md §8, **Then** every
   item is covered, and the slime-mould mapping is stated to carry no security argument. The
   owner design review records "no security statement rests on the mapping" as one of its
   items, and the claim-register test finds no security wording in the mapping chapter
   (FR-069).
2. **Given** the candidate log, **When** it is compared with the model's variant IDs and the
   evidence records, **Then** every candidate design is in the log, whether or not it produced
   evidence, with its decision and reason; every record a log entry cites exists with the cited
   SHA-256; and the latest `selected` entry, which cites the latest selection record with its tie
   choice where there is one, maps the selected candidate's variant IDs to those of `phyco-v3`,
   through which `phyco-v3` reproduces the candidate's outputs, an earlier `selected` entry standing
   as a decision that a later selection record superseded (FR-041, FR-043; ANA-081).
3. **Given** the archive, **When** its manifest is checked, **Then** every archived `phyco-v2`
   file matches its recorded SHA-256 and the live file it was copied from.
4. **Given** the owner design review and the external review record (done, or not arranged),
   **When** the freeze is approved, **Then** the freeze record holds the hashes of the design
   document, the model, the vectors, constructed-state vectors included, and the scripts that
   generate the constants and the vectors, and R with its inputs.
5. **Given** the final report, **When** it is read, **Then** each of FUT-SEC-1 to FUT-SEC-9 maps
   to evidence or is listed as open, FUT-SEC-7 is open for feature 007, and no claim is made.
6. **Given** the review package, **When** it is checked against security.md §14, **Then** it
   holds every item listed there with exact revisions, whether or not a reviewer was arranged, each
   item by the files that stand for it in the manifest's group for it, and the check fails an item
   with no file (FR-063; RAT-089).
7. **Given** the claim and evidence register, **When** its test runs, **Then** README,
   security.md and the disclaimers agree with it, and no forbidden wording is found outside the
   rules that forbid it.

### Edge Cases

- **The demonstration misses its target**: the search tool is defective and fails its
  validation. The defect is reported and fixed, and the sweep is repeated under
  `--repeat-after-defect`, on the owner's request and charged to research R7's feature-wide row
  (Pre-registered Design Protocol, "FUT-SEC-8 demonstration"), its record written beside the
  first, which stays, and naming the defect, the record it supersedes and the
  owner's request with its date (data-model §4); every run is reported. The
  demonstration uses development seeds and decides nothing about a design, so a
  repeat is not re-seeding until passing.
- **More matches than expected**: any extra match after three words is listed and separated with
  a fourth word, and the count is reported against the baseline.
- **No published theorem covers the mode exactly, or a precondition cannot be met**: the mode is
  changed to one that a theorem covers, or the feature stops. A mode without a matching theorem
  is never adopted.
- **A size fails a generic bound**: it becomes a research variant, outside every claim, and the
  production interface refuses it.
- **Odd grid sizes**: a red-black update needs an even torus. An odd size uses a three-colour
  schedule from the same generics, or is listed as unsupported with the reason, as an open item
  against FUT-GRID-1. The schedule is a function of n's parity, computed at elaboration from one
  stated rule (two colours for even n, three for odd n), so an odd n is the same core with
  another generic value (FR-018, FR-019). The rule must give a proper colouring of the torus,
  wrap-around links included, at every odd n from 3 to 15, which a committed test checks link by
  link; (i + j) mod 3 does not, at n = 7 and 13 (FR-018).
- **A candidate fails an inverse or bijectivity test**: the failure is investigated first. A
  recorded tool defect gets the one re-run the protocol allows, inside the budget; otherwise the
  candidate is rejected and logged with the failure, never deleted, and it counts towards the
  stop rule's budget. An operation list not made of the two allowed step forms (research R11) is
  refused before any test.
- **A candidate is to be dropped for a reason the rules do not name**: it cannot be. `rejected`
  and `ineligible` are recorded only for the rules' reasons: a failed inverse or bijectivity
  test, a failed eligibility item or a spent budget. A candidate may be withdrawn before its
  first tool run for any recorded reason, and it still counts towards the stop rule; the log
  writer refuses any other decision (FR-041).
- **A run is suspected of a tool defect**: a tool defect is one of a failed validation or
  re-validation; a failed cross-check between kinds (FR-039); a disagreement
  between avalanche counts and dependency matrices; a disagreement between two
  evaluators of one operation list (scalar, batch, C, model); a crash or an
  undocumented exit status; a reproduction that differs from its original on a field that enters a
  verdict, in the environment that made the original (FR-053); a disagreement of the re-check under
  the rules in force at a record's entry, or a re-check's reproduction that differs, wherever it ran
  (FR-065); or a bug in the tool's code, shown by the faulty line and by a test that fails before
  the fix and passes after. It is recorded with that evidence before any
  re-run. A disliked result is none of these, nor is the end of a run by a reboot, a power loss
  or an update, which no fault of the tool brought: such a run gives no result, its partial
  outputs are discarded unread, its `inconclusive` record and its hours up to the boot stand,
  and it may run again on the same inputs, which spends no re-run, its row's hours bounding how
  often (research R7).
- **A tool fails its validation**: none of its results count, no bound from it enters the round
  rule, and the failure is recorded. The defect is recorded with its cause and fixed, and the tool
  is validated again on every known result it has, the first and the second at each of
  its widths, all fixed before its first validation, so that no use at any
  width counts again on a fix tuned to one value (a tool with no second at
  one of its widths stays unvalidated, its results `not evaluated`; FR-039;
  ANA-076); attempts are not limited, but each is recorded. Results of the failed
  version never count; results already counted from an earlier validated version stand, since
  they cite its hash. A trail or distinguisher found by a tool not yet validated is never
  dropped: it is recorded as an unverified hypothesis (FR-060) and blocks the candidate's
  eligibility until a validated tool, or a direct evaluation of the trail with the validated
  exact models, settles it (FR-039).
- **A validation target's published value cannot be obtained**: the tool stays unvalidated and
  its results are `not evaluated`; the owner may provide the value with its source (research
  R19). If no trail route is validated, no candidate can establish R_bound, none is eligible,
  and the feature recommends stopping (stop rule); the decisions this brings log their `replacement`
  entries closed at once, with the reason "cause shared" (Eligibility and selection; ANA-073).
- **A paper that a mode theorem rests on cannot be obtained or read**: its theorem stays
  `unverified`, and `downloads.json` records the file as `not obtained` with the reason (research
  R19). Where another theorem of research R10's list covers the same call and its paper is on disk
  and read, the mode chapter uses it, in a new design-document version reviewed again; where none
  does, no published theorem's preconditions can be shown met, and the feature recommends stopping
  (stop rule). Another copy of the paper, from any source, is a new download that needs the owner's
  permission for that file (FR-046; research R19).
- **A refused set's only source file cannot be had**: the refused list waits for the set's file,
  permitted in research R19's tables or, for a file they do not list, in a row "Download <n>"
  (research R15, R19), and a file recorded `not obtained` waits for another copy, permitted in a
  row "Download <n>" under a name of its own. Where none can be had, the list stays incomplete,
  no candidate can be scored, none can be eligible (FR-023), and the feature recommends stopping
  (stop rule).
- **No proven or validated trail bound reaches 2⁻¹²⁸ at a claimable size**: R_bound is not
  established for that candidate within its analysis budget, and it cannot be frozen. The gap is
  reported, and the candidate is `ineligible`; another candidate is entered in its place, before the
  set closes, while its family has fewer than three evaluated candidates and the budget allows (each
  of the replacement's rows holding more than zero hours and at least the recorded need of each run
  the rules require of every candidate at that size; stop rule; ANA-078), as the candidate log's
  `replacement` entry records (section "Pre-registered Design Protocol",
  "Eligibility and selection"); if no candidate ends eligible, the feature recommends stopping.
- **The round rule gives an expensive R**: rounds are never lowered for throughput. The cost is
  reported, and an expensive R is never a reason to enter or select another candidate: a further
  candidate is entered only in place of one `rejected` or `ineligible`, within the stop rule's
  budget, and selection follows the rules (section "Pre-registered Design Protocol", "Eligibility
  and selection").
- **A distinguisher on more rounds turns up before the freeze**: R rises by the round rule, and
  every measurement the raised R needs runs as a new measurement of its kind in the same budget row
  (research R7). If a row cannot hold it, that evidence is inconclusive and the candidate
  `ineligible` at that size for a spent budget, and selection proceeds among the others within the
  stop rule; if a selection record exists, `round_rule.py --select` runs again over the whole set
  and writes the next one, and a newly selected candidate gets the security-target review and every
  later review, each in a row of its own (section "Pre-registered Design Protocol", "Eligibility and
  selection"). A raised R is never by itself a reason to drop a candidate. A finding that breaks the
  target on the full rounds is recorded at once
  (FR-051). If it comes after the owner design review, the design document gets a new version,
  the rationale is revised, the review is repeated for the changed parts and recorded in a row of
  its own, "Owner design review <k>" (the notes under the Review & Approval table), and the review
  package is rebuilt (FR-043).
- **A change is needed after the freeze** (to a frozen file, a raise of R by feature 005, a new
  claimable size): it is a new model ID with its own vectors and freeze (FR-028), and the frozen
  files are never edited. Before the freeze, a change is a new design-document version and a
  log entry.
- **The frozen design document and the frozen model disagree**: the frozen model and vectors are
  the expected values (FR-056), and the design document is the specification. A disagreement
  found after the freeze is a finding; the frozen files stay unchanged, and the correction is a
  new model ID with its own design-document version, model, vectors and freeze (FR-028), even
  where the intended design is the same. If it bears on any claim, it is recorded at once
  (FR-051).
- **Both nonlinearity families score the same**: the pre-registered tie-break decides.
- **No external reviewer can be arranged**: the freeze proceeds, and the review is recorded as
  not arranged (security.md §7).
- **A request exceeds an output limit, or production is asked for a reduced variant**: the
  interface refuses with an error. It never wraps, truncates or carries on silently.
- **Nonce reuse**: the same key, nonce and personalization string give the same output; more
  generally, under one key and nonce, two instantiations' states agree until the first call or input
  word that differs takes effect (the personalization string, a request's length, a reseed's input
  or the order of calls), and the output blocks given while they agree repeat, the design document's
  chapter "Mode" naming, for each case, which blocks repeat, as it derives them from the mode's
  calls; the mode's bound covers a repeated nonce only through the collision term of the nonces
  FR-006 calls "random 128-bit nonces" (research R9), and a nonce the caller
  repeats by error or by force lies outside it, so that no goal of T7 is
  claimed for those instantiations (FR-006; SEC-083). This is stated behaviour
  (T7), not detected, within one object or across objects. No bounded record
  could cover the per-key instantiation limit, other objects or other devices, and the RTL keeps
  no nonce history, so a partial check would make the model differ from the hardware and
  suggest a protection that does not exist. A test shows the stated behaviour (FR-058).
- **Reseed without a validated entropy source**: the interface allows it, but it gives no
  prediction resistance, and its input is treated as chosen by the attacker (T9).
- **The Python model is slow at large grids**: the vectors keep the same lengths at every size,
  as the plan fixes them for FR-036, with no shortening at 8×8 or 16×16; the length and the
  measured speed are stated.
- **Large evidence records** (for example dependency matrices at 16×16): the plan decides what is
  committed, compressed or regenerated on demand, and every record's hash is kept. An output kept
  only locally must come from a deterministic command, so that its committed SHA-256 can be
  checked by regenerating it, and its record states that command's cost (heavy or not, hours
  projected). An output that is not deterministic, such as a solver log with timings, is never
  local-only: it is committed, compressed, or its verdict rests on a committed deterministic
  summary. Local-only outputs are written under `data/local/004/<record_id>/`, which `.gitignore`
  ignores, never under the reports directory, and a committed output above 10 MB is refused
  (research R16).
- **An edge state that no key reaches**: it still gets a constructed-state vector, and the record
  says that no key reaches it, as no seed reaches class 3 in `phyco-v2` (vhdl-genai.md §5.2).
- **The second transcription disagrees with the model**: the disagreement is a finding. Its cause
  is found and recorded, and the design document, the model or the transcription is corrected
  before the freeze; a correction to the design document is a new version of it.
- **Evidence is missing for a result**: the status is "not evaluated", "inconclusive" or
  "blocked" (security.md §16.3). A generated report is not proof that a test ran.
- **The bias rule fires on its uniform control**: the bias evidence of that size is inconclusive for
  every candidate, since the control's one record per test and size serves them all, so none is
  eligible at that size and the feature recommends stopping (stop rule), citing
  the control record, and the decisions this brings log their `replacement`
  entries closed at once, with the reason "cause shared", since a replacement
  would meet the same control record (Eligibility and selection; ANA-073).
  The control inputs are never redrawn or replaced, and the rule is not loosened (Principle IV).
- **Property-based testing is not approved**: the boundary and fixed tests stand alone, and the
  gap is recorded as an open item (FR-058).
- **An AI session offers a review**: it is recorded as an AI review, never as the external design
  review or as independent cryptanalysis (FR-064).

## Requirements *(mandatory)*

### Functional Requirements

**Demonstration first (FUT-SEC-8)**

- **FR-001**: Before any `phyco-v3` instantiation (key-loading) code exists, `phyco-v2`'s 64-bit
  seed bound MUST be reproduced on a reduced seed domain as pre-registered below: for each
  target, the first two seed words are revealed, all 2³² candidates for the last two are
  tested, and the seed is recovered from the first three output words. Instantiation code is
  code that loads a key into a state of `phyco-v3` or of any candidate: the mode's instantiate
  in the model, the research duplex, and any script that runs instantiate, such as the
  instantiation states of research R4. It does not include the development-input loader, which
  derives keys but loads none, keyless operation lists of Π, or the text of the design document.
  Writing instantiation code waits for the "FUT-SEC-8 result review" row, which the owner sets to
  Approved and commits (FR-003).
- **FR-002**: The demonstration record MUST give every match after one, two and three words
  beside the expected number, the cost per candidate in generator steps and in time, the total
  cost, the extrapolation to all 2⁶⁴ seeds, the machine and commands, and the SHA-256 of the
  model and of the search tool. Before the sweep, the search tool MUST reproduce the `phyco-v2`
  golden vectors. The demonstration report MUST meet security.md §12.4, with T1 as the attacker
  and its knowledge of the position stated (seed-entropy note §2, item 8).
- **FR-003**: The owner MUST review the result (⛔ gate) before any instantiation code (FR-001)
  is written, for `phyco-v3` or for any candidate. The result is the search tool's validation
  record (security.md §12.5), and the best-attack register row for exhaustive seed search
  (security.md §12.6) MUST cite it. The review is recorded when the owner sets the "FUT-SEC-8 result
  review" row to Approved and commits it (the note under the Review & Approval table). The evidence
  scripts' guard reads that row as committed at HEAD and refuses to run instantiation code unless it
  reads Approved there, or while the working tree's table differs from HEAD's; a test checks the
  guard.

**Security target (L1; FUT-SEC-1 to FUT-SEC-6, FUT-SEC-9)**

- **FR-004**: The security target MUST state each property sought as a bound on an adversary's
  advantage, in the definitions of security.md §3: keystream security for nonces never repeated
  under a key, multi-user security for u keys, and backtracking resistance across each forget
  call. Prediction resistance MUST be stated as not sought (D7; FUT-SEC-4). Every bound MUST be
  stated relative to Π as a random permutation and limited to attacks below the target
  strength, as in a flat sponge claim (strategy note §2.1), and the one assumption it rests on,
  that Π has no structural distinguisher below 2¹²⁸, MUST be named as what the cryptanalysis
  attacks. Backtracking resistance means that a state compromised between calls reveals nothing
  about output delivered before the last forget call, and that a state compromised within a
  request reveals, through Π⁻¹, only that request's earlier blocks back to its last forget call,
  at most the per-request cap of 2¹⁹ bits. Instantiate ends with a forget call (FR-013), so no
  such walk reaches the key. Every cost in the security target, in FR-005 and in the round
  rule's R_dist is counted in one unit, evaluations of Π or Π⁻¹ (research R9's N), with every
  data block observed counted as at least one evaluation, and an attack's data is also bounded
  by the output limits (FR-007).
- **FR-005**: The target strength MUST be s = 128 bits at every claimable size, and no parameter
  MAY admit a generic attack that costs less than 2¹²⁸ evaluations of Π or Π⁻¹ (FR-004;
  FUT-SEC-1).
- **FR-006**: For the production parameter sets (the claimable sizes, w = 32, c = 384), the key
  MUST be 256 bits (D2). The key is assumed to have full entropy, to be uniform and secret, and
  to come from an established cryptographic random-number generator, never from a PhycoWeb-PRNG
  model. The generator cannot check this, a key with less entropy caps the strength at that
  entropy (FR-010), and the security target lists the assumption among the caller's
  obligations. The key is the entropy input at instantiation, so its entropy exceeds the target
  strength, as SP 800-90A requires of entropy input (seed-entropy note §3). The nonce MUST be
  128 bits, and a nonce MUST be used at most once per key (FUT-SEC-3), in one of two ways: a
  counter per key, never repeated or wrapped, kept by the caller across objects and devices; or
  random 128-bit nonces, whose collision probability, at most q²/2¹²⁹ for q instantiations, is
  a term of the mode's bound, with a per-key limit of at most 2³² instantiations so that the
  term stays within ε (FR-007). The generator cannot tell a random nonce from a counter value, so
  this limit applies to every key whatever its nonces: one instantiation counter per object, and
  across objects a usage rule (research R9). An optional personalization string MUST have a stated
  maximum length and MUST NOT replace the nonce. The behaviour on a repeated nonce MUST be stated
  (T7) for each case: under one key and nonce, two instantiations' states agree until the first call
  or input word that differs takes effect (the personalization string, a request's length, a
  reseed's input or the order of calls), and the output blocks given while they agree repeat, the
  design document's chapter "Mode" naming, for each case, which blocks repeat, as it derives them
  from the mode's calls; the mode's bound covers a repeated nonce only through the collision term of
  the nonces this requirement calls "random 128-bit nonces" (research R9),
  and a nonce the caller repeats by error or by force lies outside it, so
  that no goal of T7 is claimed for those instantiations (SEC-083). A keyed
  reduced variant takes a c-bit key and a c/2-bit nonce (research R14), and its size-profile row
  states its `bound_bits` as a bound on that variant only, with its checks made at s = c/2, status
  `research` and "no claim" (FR-032); s = c/2 is the level of those checks, not a field of the row
  and not its bound, and `bound_bits`, one of whose terms is c/2, is never above it (FR-010;
  SEC-096).
- **FR-007**: The security target MUST specify the maximum output per instantiation (key and
  nonce), per generate request and per key across all nonces, the maximum number of keys u
  that it covers, and every other online quantity the theorem's bound counts (calls of Π,
  instantiations per key, held at the random-nonce limit whatever the nonces (FR-006), and reseeds
  per instantiation), each with a limit and a counter, and each derived from the mode's theorem at
  s = 128 with the target advantage stated (FUT-SEC-6). Every limit is stated in the theorem's
  unit, calls of Π, and also in bits at every claimable size: a request of n bits takes ⌈n/r⌉
  output calls plus one forget call, and the calls instantiate takes are stated. The production
  interface MUST refuse any request beyond them. Across objects, processes and devices, and
  across resets of a device (FR-025), the per-key limits and u are usage rules that the caller
  keeps; the model enforces them only within one object. The security target lists them with
  the caller's other obligations (the key source and nonce uniqueness, FR-006).
- **FR-008**: The threat model MUST adopt each of T1–T11 (security.md §5) for `phyco-v3` or
  exclude it with a reason, and MUST list the assets of a keyed design: at least the key; the
  capacity part at every moment; the whole state within a request, whose earlier blocks Π⁻¹
  recovers back to the last forget call; every output block delivered to the caller; the
  integrity of the limit counters; and the caller's nonce-uniqueness record. The design
  document may add more. T1 MUST be restated for `phyco-v3`, with the attacker knowing the
  position of every output block it observes. T4 is adopted as constant time by construction
  (FR-020); power, electromagnetic and fault-injection attacks and the behaviour on reset and
  clock glitches are deferred to feature 008, with the risk stated: a state read by a probe, or
  changed by a fault, in mid-request exposes that request back to its last forget call, and any of
  the self-test's selectors, the one at the key chain's input, the one at the nonce chain's input or
  the one at the absorption interface, held on its constants by a design error or a fault, alone or
  with the others, would run every instantiate on the self-test's key, a public development input,
  in place of the host's, on nonce 0, or on the self-test's input words in place of the host's at
  the absorption interface, with no sign at the ports, a risk that feature 008's tests cover by
  matching the output, block by block, with the model's after a passed self-test, a key load and an
  instantiate with the host's key, nonce and personalization string, each other than the
  self-test's, and after a reseed with an input other than the self-test's and a second request, so
  that each selector held alone, as well as all of them, gives an output that
  differs from the model's (FR-059; SEC-082, SEC-088; RAT-079). The 128-bit
  target is a classical one, so against T11 this feature covers only single-target key search, k/2 ≥
  s, which k = 256 meets with equality and no margin at s = 128 (FR-010). Multi-target quantum key
  search, which in some cost models costs about 2^((k − log₂ u)/2) and so falls below 2^s for every
  u > 1, quantum attacks on the capacity through queries to the public Π, and every other quantum
  generic attack are excluded for that reason and listed open for feature 005.
- **FR-009**: For every supported size, the specification MUST state the key entropy, the secret
  state reachable from the key, and the bound these place on any security level (Security
  Policy; FUT-GRID-2). The secret state reachable from the key is the set of state bits that no
  public input sets and no output reveals, at every call boundary: after instantiate, after each
  generate with its forget call, and after each reseed. For the duplex as designed it is the
  capacity part, c bits. It is counted from the specified layout (which lanes form the capacity
  part, and that no call writes or outputs them) and recorded per boundary as "by
  specification", not measured. Public parts of the state and of the inputs (constants,
  domain-separation bits, counters, the nonce, the personalization string, reseed inputs and
  every output) MUST NOT be counted as secret; a reseed input is never secret state, even from
  a secret source (FR-016). A research variant's bound is labelled "research variant, no claim"
  and is never compared with s = 128 or reported as a strength (FR-032). That bound is the size
  profile's `bound_bits`, the least of the figures FR-010's checks give, as FR-010 states (SEC-090).
- **FR-010**: Every claimable size MUST be shown to respect every generic bound of security.md
  §12.2: strength at most the key entropy; reachable secret state at least 2s at every call
  boundary (FR-009); output volume stated, with every limit at most what the theorem allows at
  ε (the doubling check of research R9); multi-target search, k − log₂ u ≥ s within the stated
  u; key plus nonce at least 2s; capacity c ≥ 2s; quantum search (T11), single-target only (FR-008),
  k/2 ≥ s, which holds with equality and no margin at k = 256 and s = 128 and is recorded in the
  size profile as met with equality; behaviour on nonce reuse stated (edge case "Nonce
  reuse"), with its test. Each is a check in the size profile (`parameters.json`) that can fail,
  computed by code: `mode_limits_v3.py` writes the profile beside `limits.json` and exits 1 on any
  violation at a claimable size (FR-065), reading the variant table, the layout and the limits from
  the design document's tables that FR-034 "Chapters" fixes, and the chapter "Security target"'s
  parameter table and output limits restate the profile and `limits.json`, which a test compares
  value by value (RAT-085). The profile's `bound_bits`, the bound FR-009 states, is
  per supported size the least of the figures these checks give, each the cost of a generic attack
  in bits: k, the key entropy; half the reachable secret state at the call boundary where it is
  least; k − log₂ u at the u that `limits.json` states; half of the key and nonce bits together;
  c/2; and k/2, T11 being single-target key search only (FR-008); a term below zero counts as zero,
  and the checks of output volume and of nonce reuse give no figure and stay checks that can fail.
  `mode_limits_v3.py` writes beside it each term and the term that sets it, as `bound_terms`,
  computed by code, and a test recomputes the least from the terms and each term from the row's
  other fields. A keyed reduced variant, such as `n2-w16-R8-c64`, takes the same rule at its own k,
  nonce bits and c, its value labelled "research variant, no claim" and never compared with s, and a
  keyless variant, with k = 0, takes `none`. The least is the most conservative reading, since each
  check caps every level (SEC-090).
- **FR-011**: The certification route MUST be named (FUT-SEC-9; D8); naming it meets FUT-SEC-9
  by specification. The functionality class within AIS 20/31 version 3.0 stays an open item,
  written "class unconfirmed" beside the route, until the download the owner allowed (research R19)
  is read and the class is confirmed or changed in the design document, at the security-target
  review, the first step of plan phase 8 (FR-012). No artifact MAY describe `phyco-v3`, or any
  model, as FIPS 140-3 approved or as an
  SP 800-90A DRBG. A value taken from a standard is cited as that standard's value (for example
  "the value SP 800-90A uses for its largest request"), never as compliance, and the design
  document states once that `phyco-v3` conforms to no standard; the claim-register test
  (FR-069) flags "compliant", "conforms" and "approved" near a standard's name, that is in the same
  sentence or table row, and that statement, like every flagged line, passes only through an
  allow-list entry with its reason.
- **FR-012**: The claim template of security.md §3 MUST be filled in for `phyco-v3` as a target
  and marked "target, not a claim". The claim register (security.md §15) MUST record `phyco-v3`
  at L0, then at L1 once its gate has passed and FUT-SEC-8 and the design document's chapters
  "Security target" and "Mode" (FR-034) have been reviewed, and with "no claim" at every level. Both
  chapters are drafted in plan phase 4 and recorded as under review, in the provenance log and in
  the security-target review's Review & Approval row, until that review in
  phase 8, and `rationale.md`'s chapters of those names, which explain them,
  are drafted with them, the security-target review reading them and the
  owner design review adding their review entries (RAT-086); neither carries
  a mark in `design.md`, whose approval status stays outside the file (FR-034), so
  that the text the review approves is the text `design_freeze.py create`
  compares (RAT-068, RAT-069). Their review, the security-target review, has a
  Review & Approval row of its own and is the first step of phase 8, after the selection and before
  the owner design review: it cites the selected candidate's records of FR-029's diffusion through
  instantiation and of research R4's first-order differences, which exist only after phase 5, and
  closes FR-011's functionality class. The owner design review does not start while the
  security-target review is not Approved, and a decline there ends in a revised chapter, reviewed in
  a row of its own, or a stop (the note under the Review & Approval table). The security-target
  review may read Approved while a theorem the mode chapter uses is "unverified"; the owner design
  review and the freeze need every such theorem `verified` in `limits.json`, with each precondition
  marked met there, beside the section of the mode chapter that meets it, which must be one of that
  chapter's sections in the version of the design document read, `design_freeze.py create` exiting 2
  otherwise (FR-043; research R10). Each theorem's entry in `limits.json` cites the
  paper's `fetched` entry in `downloads.json` by SHA-256, with the theorem's number and page;
  `mode_limits_v3.py` refuses `verified` without one, or when the file on disk has another SHA-256,
  and the agent's `verified` and met marks count only once the security-target review row, which
  reviews the chapter by its version, reads Approved: until then `design_freeze.py create` reads
  them as `unverified`. The chapter's version is the set the notes under the Review & Approval table
  define, the SHA-256 of the design document's chapters "Security target" and "Mode", of
  `rationale.md`'s chapters of those names (RAT-086) and of `parameters.json` and `limits.json`,
  with the design document's version read, so that a mark set after the review
  counts only once a "Security-target review <k>" naming the changed set reads
  Approved (SEC-075). A paper that cannot be obtained or read ends as the
  edge case on it states: another theorem of research R10's list, in a new
  version reviewed again, or a stop. The L1 gate of security.md §15 reads
  "FUT-SEC-8 result review and security-target review" (version 2.13), and L1 is recorded in a row
  of its own, "L1 recorded", once both reviews read Approved, naming the two review rows by family
  and k with what each reviewed, a row the owner sets and the claim test reads at HEAD (FR-069); a
  later row of either review family makes it lapse, and L1 is then recorded again
  in a row "L1 recorded <k>" once the later rows read Approved (the notes under
  the Review & Approval table); each such row reads "blocked", naming the
  missing paper, while any theorem the mode chapter uses is "unverified" (research R10). The spec
  approval of 2026-11-01 started the design work and recorded no level, so the register keeps
  `phyco-v3` at L0 until "L1 recorded" reads Approved. For L1, FUT-SEC-4 is met by statement, since
  it applies only if prediction resistance is claimed and D7 does not seek it, and FUT-SEC-5 is met
  by specification for key loading and by evidence for diffusion through instantiation (FR-029) and
  research R4's first-order related-key and related-nonce differences, whose records the
  security-target review cites, so that nothing remains to measure, and stays open for feature 005's
  analysis (FR-049); the register records the same split. The register's `claim_level` holds the
  level whose gate holds at HEAD (L0 or L1), so that it returns to L0 while a review held again is
  pending, or while a hash its review rows name differs from the file at
  HEAD (SEC-079), with each earlier L1 and its dates kept in the entry's
  `history`, and a separate `in_progress` field, `none` before the first counted trail-bound record,
  names L2 with its start date, that record's date, and "not reached", written by
  the agent in the commit that writes that record and checked by the claim
  test against the records at HEAD (SEC-080); the claim-register test checks that no document states
  a level above `claim_level`, flagging every name of a level above it in every scanned line as a
  whole word, as FR-069's rule states (SEC-085). Plan phase 1 creates `phyco-v3`'s entry
  in `protocol/claims.json` at L0, with "no claim", `in_progress` `none`,
  `bound` `none` and `evidence` empty, and its line at L0 in security.md §15's register, with its
  row in §1.1, "L0; no claim" under "Claim level (section 15)", in the same security.md version
  (SEC-081; SEC-089; SEC-095). The agent writes each change that the rows
  bring to the entry's `claim_level`, `updated` and `history`, and to that line and that row of §1.1
  (SEC-095), in a commit of its own right after the owner's commit that sets "L1
  recorded" or "L1 recorded <k>" to Approved, or that adds a later row of either review family,
  which makes L1 lapse, and never before that commit, save inside FR-043's window, where the owner's
  commit holds it (SEC-084); the claim test checks the register against those rows both ways
  (FR-069; SEC-076). L1 holds at HEAD only while what its rows name holds
  there: `check_claims.py` compares with the files at HEAD each hash named by the review rows that
  the "L1 recorded" row with the highest k names, the chapter's version item by item and the
  demonstration record with its report, and gives L0 while any differs, so that a change to the
  reviewed set makes L1 lapse from the commit that makes it, as a later row does; the agent writes
  the register's return to L0 in that commit, or, where an owner's commit makes the change, in a
  commit of its own right after it, `history` keeping the earlier L1 with its dates, its rows and
  the version reviewed, until "L1 recorded <k>" records L1 again (SEC-079). FR-043's window, in
  which the agent commits no file of this feature, `protocol/claims.json` and security.md among
  them, runs from the first commit that records an owner design review's approval to the feature's
  end, plan phase 9 included; inside it the agent writes each change of `phyco-v3`'s entry and of
  its line in security.md §15 that SEC-076 and SEC-079 name in the working tree, once the owner has
  set the row or made the change there and before the owner's commit, which holds it, so that at
  every commit of the window the register gives the rows' level, and `design_freeze.py create` exits
  2 while `check_claims.py` reports a finding at HEAD (SEC-084).

**Mode (D1)**

- **FR-013**: The generator MUST be a keyed duplex over Π with the calls instantiate (key, nonce,
  personalization string), generate (n bits), forget and an optional reseed (input), and forget
  MUST follow every generate request and end every instantiate, in the form the theorem covers
  (research R10), so that no state compromise reaches the key (FR-004). The reseed call is part of
  the design, of the model and of the core, D1's "optional reseed" meaning only that a host
  need not call it, so that the start-up self-test and feature 008's tests
  always run a reseed (FR-008, FR-059; SEC-088). Its security bound MUST
  come from a named, published theorem for exactly this mode (for example the keyed-duplex
  bounds of Daemen, Mennink and Van Assche, 2017, or the sponge-based generator of Bertoni et
  al., 2010). Every precondition of the theorem, including how the key enters the state, MUST be
  listed with the design feature that meets it. The key and the nonce are placed in the initial
  state at stated positions, the rest of it set to stated constants, as the full-state keyed
  duplex initializes; later inputs (the personalization string and reseed inputs) are absorbed
  over the rate only (FR-018), and the mode chapter MUST show that this is a special case of the
  theorem's absorption. Both are preconditions, "unverified" until the paper is read; if the
  paper's form differs, the design takes the paper's form, or the feature stops. The reachable
  secret state MUST stay at least 2s at every call boundary (FR-009), and a reseed never resets
  a counter. In the model the key is given once, when the generator object is constructed, and
  held until zeroization: each instantiate takes the nonce and the personalization string and
  loads the stored key into the state, so no key is ever compared with another. In hardware the key
  is loaded once, along a registered shift chain, into key registers at the nodes whose lanes hold
  key bits, and zeroized with the state; at every instantiate the host gives the nonce on the
  absorption interface, and in one cycle a selector at every lane sets the lane from one of four
  sources: a key register, the nonce chain, the nonce register of a rate lane that holds nonce bits,
  or its stated constant (FR-025 "Key loading and reset").
- **FR-014**: Padding and domain separation MUST be specified so that every absorbed input (key,
