  nonce, personalization string, reseed input) and every call type can be decoded uniquely from
  the absorbed blocks and is separated from every other, and this injectivity MUST be shown in
  two ways: a proof in the design document that the encoding of every (call type, input) pair
  into absorbed blocks is injective, with the padding rule, the rule for partial blocks and the
  domain bits stated; and a committed check over every input length from 0 to each input's
  maximum and every call type, showing that distinct (call type, length) pairs give distinct
  block layouts, with a test that distinct development inputs give distinct block sequences.
  Acceptance is 0 collisions and the proof accepted at the owner design review. Where call
  types use distinct constant streams, the proof says whether the streams or the domain bits
  carry the separation. Padding constants and domain separators are constants of the mode, with
  their rule and reason stated (FR-034). In hardware a personalization string or a reseed input
  arrives after one 32-bit word that gives its length in bytes, so the core knows the length before
  it pads the input, and the unused bytes of a last partial word must be zero (FR-025 "Flow
  control").
- **FR-015**: The capacity MUST be c ≥ 384 bits at every claimable size, with rate r = b − c.
  Output MUST come only from the outer (rate) part, after a full call of Π. One canonical
  serialization of the output (word width, byte order, bit order) MUST be stated for every tool
  and for the hardware export. A request whose length is not a multiple of r keeps the first
  bits of its last block in that order and discards the rest (FR-027). Every digest of output,
  such as the long-run digest of the vectors, is taken over this serialization as raw bytes
  (4·2²⁰ bytes for 2²⁰ words of 32 bits), never over a text rendering of it.
- **FR-016**: The reseed input MUST be treated as chosen by the attacker (T9), and reseeding MUST
  NOT weaken any property sought. This feature has no validated entropy source and seeks no
  prediction resistance (D7), so no source is excepted; a later feature that brings a validated
  entropy source and seeks prediction resistance states its own rule under its own protocol. To make
  that verifiable, the mode chapter MUST map reseed to the theorem's absorption call with
  attacker-chosen input and list that mapping as a precondition, "unverified" until the paper
  is read (research R10); the reachable secret state after each reseed is at least 2s (FR-009);
  and a reseed never resets a counter. A reseed input's length is public even when its content
  is secret (FR-020).
- **FR-017**: The claimable sizes MUST be decided, which resolves the FUT-SEC-2 and FUT-GRID-2
  conflict at 2×2 (security.md §20.1). By default (D3) they start at 4×4, and 2×2 and 3×3 are
  reduced research variants outside every claim. The decision MUST be recorded in security.md
  §20.1 with the reason it weakens neither requirement: FUT-SEC-1 and FUT-SEC-2 bound a claimed
  strength, no claim covers a research size, each research row of the size profile states its key
  entropy, its `bound_bits` and the status `research`, a keyless row stating a key entropy of 0
  and `bound_bits` `none` (SEC-096), and the production interface refuses
  every research variant (FR-033).

**Permutation Π (FUT-GRID-1 to FUT-GRID-5)**

- **FR-018**: Π MUST be a keyless permutation of the whole state of an n×n torus grid, with grid
  size n and word width w as generics covering every n from 2 to 16 (FUT-GRID-1). Each node
  holds two w-bit lanes (pressure and vein), so the state has b = 2w·n² bits (D3). Data MUST
  flow only between direct neighbours, with at most four channels per node: a channel is one
  w-bit word passed from a node to a neighbour in one phase, and a node receives at most four
  per phase (one from each neighbour, or two from each of two), counted from the operation list
  with the linear layer between boxes included. At 2×2 a node's opposite neighbours coincide, so its
  four links join two distinct nodes, two channels from each, and a translation by one node is its
  own inverse; the size profile states this, and 2×2 evidence counts only for properties that do not
  depend on the neighbourhood (FR-032, FR-052). Round constants MUST enter at local food nodes,
  placed by a stated rule (FUT-GRID-4) whose placement has no symmetry of the torus that moves a
  node at any claimable size: one food node is fixed by a rotation about it and two by the half-turn
  about their midpoint, so the rule takes at least three food nodes in an asymmetric arrangement,
  such as (0,0), (0,1) and (1,2), which has none at 4×4, 8×8 and 16×16 (computed on 2026-11-01), and
  research R13's symmetry check stays as written there. At 2×2 and 3×3, where every placement has
  such a symmetry (computed the same day), the placement is checked with the constants it carries as
  one pattern, the `symmetry` record says so, the size profile marks the variant, and no claim
  covers it (D3; research R13). Absorption and output MUST use registered trees over the
  rate lanes only, and the key and the nonce reach their lanes by the key path of FR-025 ("Key
  loading and reset"). The torus MUST be realisable as a folded torus, so that every link, the
  wrap-around links included, joins neighbouring nodes of the layout (Principle III; strategy
  note §5.4): logical neighbours sit at most two positions apart in each dimension of the
  layout, for odd n as well as even, with at most one node between a link's ends, and the
  design document gives the folding rule and the longest link per size. The colour schedule is
  a function of n's parity, computed at elaboration from one stated rule (two colours for even
  n, three for odd n), so an odd n is the same core with another generic value. For odd n the rule
  MUST give a proper colouring of the torus, wrap-around links included, at every odd n from 3 to
  15, and a committed test checks every link at each odd n, since a rule as simple as (i + j) mod 3
  gives two neighbours across the wrap the same colour at n = 7 and 13; a rule that holds is c(i, j)
  = (f(i) + f(j)) mod 3, with f a proper 3-colouring of the n-cycle such as f(k) = k mod 2 for k < n
  − 1 and f(n − 1) = 2, since two neighbours differ in one coordinate, where f differs by 1 or 2.
  The design document states its rule and the test's result, and FUT-GRID-1's odd sizes cite them;
  without such a rule, odd sizes are unsupported with the reason (research R14). The supported sizes
  MUST be listed with b, c, r, R and their status (claimable or research variant), and any size from
  2×2 to 16×16 that is not supported MUST be listed with the reason as an open item against
  FUT-GRID-1. Supported, wherever this feature's documents use the word, means these listed sizes:
  2×2, 3×3 where the design document specifies its three-colour schedule, 4×4, 8×8 and 16×16. Any
  other n from 2 to 16 is for exploration only, from a table that
  `gen_constants_v3.py --exploration N W` writes for that (n, w), before or after the freeze, and
  checks as for a supported size (FR-023), kept under `research/004/exploration/`, never frozen and
  never evidence; an exploration variant's ID carries its R (model contract, "Variant IDs"). The
  list names each size's keyed variants: the production variant at every
  claimable size, and at 2×2, and at a supported 3×3, at least one keyed reduced variant, such
  as `n2-w16-R8-c64` with an 8-byte key and a 4-byte nonce; keyless variants are listed as
  permutation-only. A keyed variant's r MUST be a multiple of 32, as its c is a multiple of 64
  (research R14), a variant whose r is not a multiple of 32 being keyless: since b = 2w·n² and c is
  a multiple of 64, r is a multiple of 32 at w ≥ 16 and at every even n, so that at an odd n a keyed
  variant needs w ≥ 16, as a keyed 2×2 does, and a supported 3×3's keyed variant is at w = 16 or 32,
  such as one with c = 64 and r = 224 at w = 16; every block of the absorption and output interfaces
  is then a whole number of 32-bit words (FR-025 "Flow control"; HW-093). Per claimable size and per
  keyed research variant it gives the start-up self-test's expected output (FR-059), and a
  core is built only at a keyed variant, its generics refusing a keyless
  variant at elaboration (FR-025 "Key loading and reset"; HW-095). For every
  supported size the design document states the number of food nodes, their
  positions by the rule and the reason for them (for example the number of rounds until every
  lane depends on a constant injected in round 1, from the dependency propagation of research
  R13); the ROM depth at each food node (R entries per distinct constant
  stream, t·R for FR-023's t call types, the stream selected by the call
  type that travels with the start signal of each call of Π; FR-025; HW-079)
  and the word width, with the total bits reported, which the cost model does
  not score (research R5); and each tree's fan-in per stage, depth, registered stages and
  latency in cycles. The trees' latency enters the instantiate latency (FR-025) and
  the throughput (FR-026), and the timing justification Principle III asks for is an argument
  in the design document (a register at every stage, a fan-in bounded by a stated number),
  referenced from the plan's Complexity Tracking. The frozen `phycoweb/constants_v3.py` holds
  one constant table per supported (n, w), and the constant script also writes each as JSON
  beside the vectors, with the count of words its reading order skipped and every repeat of a class
  (FR-023), frozen with them; feature 006 turns them into packages and the RTL selects
  one by its generics, while the LFSR that generates them stays in the script. A size without a
  table cannot be built until the same script generates one. For eligibility, decided for every
  candidate before selection, the folding rule, the longest link, the food nodes with their reason
  and each tree's figures come from the candidate's operation list and its `parameters` entry
  (section "Pre-registered Design Protocol"); the self-test's expected output is generated from the
  selected candidate's vectors, so that item cannot fail.
- **FR-019**: Π MUST be bijective by construction, with every round a composition of steps that
  are invertible whatever their inputs, and Π⁻¹ MUST be specified step by step and modelled.
  The colour schedule MUST follow the rules of research R11: within one phase no update reads a
  lane that another update of that phase writes, every round runs every phase in a fixed order (a
  round may visit a colour more than once, and P counts its phases; FR-025),
  and an odd n uses a three-colour schedule from the same generics, a proper colouring of the torus
  at every odd n, or is unsupported with the reason (FR-018). The operation-list loader checks the
  phase rule at every supported size, from the declared reads and writes after the neighbourhood is
  resolved, and refuses a list that breaks it, exit 2, before any test; a committed test runs the
  check on every list at every size, with a stated list that breaks the rule at one size only, which
  it must refuse. The interpreters apply a phase as the hardware does, each update
  reading the values at the phase's start, which equals list order where the
  rule holds, checked on the vectors, so bijectivity by construction holds wherever the check passes
  (research R11). Since each update adds at most one carry chain and the phase rule keeps any path
  within a phase from passing through two updates, a stage holds at most one carry chain from the
  round's updates on any path (FR-025 "Stages"). Π⁻¹ is written out at the same level
  of detail as Π (the phases in reverse order, the constants in reverse, each operation's width,
  signedness and place in the update order) and checked against the inverse generated from the
  operation list. Its roles are stated: R_b, attacks in the inverse direction, and the exposure
  within a request (FR-004). It is research-only and outside the production interface (FR-033).
- **FR-020**: Π and the mode MUST use fixed operations only: no control flow, table index, shift
  amount, rotation amount, selection or wiring between nodes may depend on secret data, so that
  the number of operations, and the cycle count in hardware, is constant by construction. This
  excludes data-dependent rotations or shifts and key-dependent topology (strategy note §10,
  rejected ideas). Public values are the variant, each command, the call type of
  each call of Π (FR-023; HW-079) and the lengths of the personalization
  string, of the reseed input and of every request; a reseed input's length is
  public even when its content is secret, as the threat model states. A cycle count may depend
  on public values and on nothing else, and the design document gives, per call (key loading,
  counted for a finished load; instantiate, ending with its forget call complete and the core
  `instantiated` and ready for a command, the nonce's transfer, the length
  check, the copy and every block of the personalization string with its
  calls of Π included, of which FR-025's two instantiate figures are parts
  (HW-075, HW-083); generate of n bits with its forget; reseed of m bytes; a
  refused request that changes nothing, a command word refused between calls or an instantiate or a
  reseed refused at its length word, each from its command word to the cycle in which its code is
  raised, a word refused while a call runs having no count of its own and leaving the running call's
  as it would be without it (HW-091); a refused request that zeroizes (a per-key limit or an error
  path, adding the zeroization's latency); zeroization, from each state that accepts it, `loading`,
  `instantiating`, `reseeding` and `generating` among them (HW-076, HW-087); the start-up
  self-test), a formula in cycles from the variant and the public lengths
  only, counting every cycle of the call from its start to its end except those in which the core
  waits for the host: on output, the cycles with valid high and ready low, and on an input (key
  loading, a nonce, a personalization string, a reseed input), the cycles with the core's ready high
  and valid low. A call starts when the core takes its command word on the command interface while
  `call_done` is high, or a zeroize while a call runs (the self-test when reset is released),
  and ends in the cycle in which `call_done` rises, the port of FR-025's
  port list that marks each call's end (FR-025 "Registers and read-back", "Flow control"; HW-086),
  so a pause by the host on any interface is excluded and a measured count matches its
  formula at any pace the host keeps; the output buffer's hand-over, its clearing and that of the
  trees when a request ends, and the start signal's fan-out latency are included (FR-025). The core
  takes a command word in the cycle in which the command interface's valid and ready are both high.
  A word taken while `call_done` is high starts a call, one the state accepts or a refused request,
  so that a refusal between calls has a call and a count of its own, to the cycle in which its error
  code is raised, when `call_done` rises, as an instantiate or a reseed refused at its length word
  has, from its command word; a word taken while a call runs, `call_done` low, starts no call of its
  own, a zeroize excepted, which ends the running call and starts its zeroization, counted from that
  word as FR-025 "Call sequence" states, and any other word is refused with its code, changes
  nothing and leaves the running call's count, outputs and `call_done` as they
  would be without it (FR-025 "Call sequence", "Flow control"; HW-091). The start-up
  self-test's formula follows from FR-059's k, the least that the model's run
  gives or one that a structure the design document names, with its reason,
  raised, as the design document's table of k states it (HW-084; REP-097), and
  counts every word it feeds, those of its key and nonce, the empty personalization string's length
  word and the reseed input's length word and words (FR-059; HW-082). A call
  made in `not-reset`, `resetting` or `failed` is never accepted and has no
  count (FR-025 "Call sequence"). Feature 008 measures the counts at the
  ports, from a call's start to the cycle in which `call_done` rises, and
  compares them with the formulas (FUT-HW-5; HW-086). For eligibility, decided
  for every candidate before selection, these cycles per call come, with
  FR-026's figures, from the candidate's `cost` records (section "Pre-registered Design Protocol"),
  the self-test's formula taking the least k that the `cost` record computes, as FR-026 states
  (FR-059; REP-097). Constant time by construction is a property of the design's sequence of
  operations and of a hardware implementation of it. For each implementation it means: in
  hardware, a cycle count fixed by public values; in feature 006's C, no branch or memory index
  that depends on secret data; in the Python model, branch-free on data as a discipline only,
  never called constant time, since Python integers are not. The C tools of this feature are
  branch-free on data where stated, and their timing is not claimed either; the claim-register test
  flags "constant time" in every scanned line, beside the model and elsewhere, and each allowed use
  needs an entry with its reason (FR-069).
- **FR-021**: Every state word MUST be full-range: no update may confine a lane to a subrange or
  keep it biased (S12, S14). A lane is biased if any of its bits fires under the bias rule at a
  round count measured, and confined if an update maps it into a subrange. The rationale's
  full-range argument is that every update `dst ← dst ⊙ F(srcs)` is a bijection of dst for
  fixed srcs (research R11), so no lane loses values; the bias rule checks the rest.
- **FR-022**: The nonlinear component MUST be designed, named and given its bounds in the
  rationale (S11). The ARX and Boolean families MUST both be explored on reduced widths with
  development keys, and one chosen by the pre-registered choice rule (D4). The ARX candidates
  MUST include at least one that treats a node's pressure and vein lanes as one 2w-bit ARX-box
  (64 bits at w = 32) under the long-trail strategy: the trail bounds of each distinct box, one pair
  per box (the K sets of rotation amounts of FR-024 make K boxes), come from a validated
  search, exhaustive where the width allows (FR-039), and the bound for Π
  follows from counting active boxes, each counted at the smallest of the K
  boxes' bounds (strategy note §5.3; D10). Only the method is reused, never a
  published box or its constants (D6). "Exhaustive" applies to box widths up to 16 bits
  (w ≤ 8), where the full difference and linear tables are computed; at w = 16 and 32 each distinct
  box's trail bounds come from the validated ARX search with the exact models of
  addition (research R12), each box within the 8 h per instance of research R7
  and the candidate's 48 h row. The rationale states the differential and
  linear branch numbers of the local linear layer between boxes, or the rule by which a
  difference must activate boxes in later rounds, and derives the active-box count from it; the
  validated active-box counting of research R12 checks it. The box uses only w-bit operations
  on the node's two lanes, never a 2w-bit addition, so every datapath word stays at w bits
  (re-decided feature-002 FR-002). For eligibility, decided for every candidate before selection,
  the bounds come from the candidate's bound records (section "Pre-registered Design Protocol").
- **FR-023**: Round constants MUST be generated by a committed script from a stated rule. They
  MUST differ in every round and at every food node within one call of Π, and their pattern MUST
  have no non-trivial translational or rotational symmetry of the grid at any supported size
  (FUT-GRID-4; S2, S7). "Differ" means that within one call of Π no two constants are equal,
  equal up to a word rotation by k = 1…w − 1, complements of one another, or rotations of
  complements; the constant script checks this beside the symmetry checks of research R13. The
  rule's reading order skips any word that is periodic (equal to its own rotation by some
  k = 1…w − 1) or related in one of these ways to a constant already taken in the same call, so
  "differ" holds by construction wherever one call needs no more constants than there are aperiodic
  classes under rotation and complement: 2 at w = 4, 16 at w = 8, 2048 at w = 16 and far more at
  w = 32, so at every claimable size. Where a call needs more, as at 2×2 with w = 4, the record of
  taken classes starts afresh each time every class has been used once, the script records every
  repeat, the size profile marks the variant, every check of research R13 still runs, and no claim
  covers it (D3). The design document MUST state whether the call types (instantiate, generate,
  forget, reseed) draw on distinct constant streams as part of domain separation (FR-014; strategy
  note §10), with the reason and, where they do, how each food node's ROM learns the call type (a
  field that travels with the start signal; FR-025), and the mode theorem's preconditions MUST still
  hold. A call type is one of these four kinds of call of Π, wherever this feature's documents use
  the term: one call on the command interface may make several calls of Π, each of its own type, and
  the start tree sends a start signal for each with that call's type, a forget call's being forget
  where its stream is distinct; t, the number of call types the start tree carries, is 4, or the
  number of distinct streams where the design shares one stream between types, as this requirement
  allows; the command word names the command, key load, instantiate, generate, reseed
  or zeroize, and a key load and a zeroize, which run no call of Π, send
  no start signal (FR-025 "Flow control"; HW-079). Where the streams are
  distinct, every kind of evidence whose result depends on the constants
  covers each call type's permutation, each record naming its call type: the symmetry check, the
  checks of the constants within a call, the refused list, the inverse tests, the invariant checks
  and the bias tests, with R the largest over the call types; the trail bounds and the dependency
  matrices read no constant value and cover one permutation for all, each record stating that reason
  (FR-037; research R13). A table holds the constants of R rounds, read in research R15's order, so
  a rise of R makes a new table, checked again, and, where the streams are distinct, changes the
  constants of every call type after the first from round 1, whose results are then measured again
  from round 1; each record names the table it read by its SHA-256, so that a result on one table is
  never cited for another (round rule). For eligibility, decided for every candidate before
  selection, the statement on the streams, with its reason, comes from the
  candidate's log entry (section "Pre-registered Design Protocol"). The rule (its
  polynomial, seed, reading order, and distinct streams per call type or not) is
  fixed by one stated rule before the first candidate is scored, and each candidate's instance of it
  before that candidate's first tool run, recorded in its log entry's `parameters`. Research R15
  fixes its values: degree 64; the polynomials x⁶⁴ + c(x) with c(0) = 1, in ascending order of the
  integer whose bits are c's coefficients; the first primitive one not on the refused list of
  re-decided feature-002 FR-007; the bits of ASCII `PhycoWeb` as the seed; and R15's reading order.
  They are recorded as `settled` entries of the protocol change log before the first candidate is
  scored. The refused list is frozen with the rule, its SHA-256 in the same entry. A match is a run
  of consecutive table words of at least 64 bits (16 words at w = 4, 8 at w = 8, 4 at w = 16 and 2
  at w = 32) equal to as many consecutive w-bit words of a listed constant set, read as one bit
  string (its words in published order, most significant bit first) from any multiple of w, under
  one of 2w transforms, each rotating every word of the run by the same k, k = 0…w − 1, with or
  without the complement of every word (k = 0 without the complement is the plain match), so that
  rotations of complements match, as in "differ" above; the check covers every table the script
  writes, one per supported (n, w), each candidate's per variant and each exploration
  table (FR-018), and each call type's stream, and its record states the
  chance of an accidental match, at most T·L·2w·2⁻⁶⁴ for T table words and L listed words, so that a
  failure shows reuse rather than chance (research R15). An addition to the list is proposed by the
  agent and approved by the owner as a stricter change in the protocol change log, with the list's
  new SHA-256, since the list only grows (FR-044); designs that the spec, the plan, research.md or
  security.md name later are added when named, as the rationale's citations are, and the check runs
  again. A match makes each candidate whose polynomial or table it touches fail this requirement, an
  eligibility item, so that candidate is `ineligible` and the rules select among the others; a match
  in the shared polynomial touches every candidate and ends in a stop. The rule is never changed to
  escape a match. A match found after the freeze is a finding recorded at once (FR-051), and
  `phyco-v3` stays as frozen; so is a failed check of an exploration table, a
  refused-list match included, which leaves that (n, w) without a table, its
  record stating why. The design document restates the values, and the script
  checks that the two agree. The script writes one table per supported (n, w), and an
  exploration table for another (n, w), before or after the freeze, with
  `--exploration N W` and every check of this requirement (FR-018), and, before the
  freeze, the table of each variant (n, w, R) a candidate uses, with each call type's stream where
  its streams are distinct, from the instance of the rule in its log entry's
  `parameters`, before the first record that reads it, a rise of R writing the new
  variant's first; a failed check or a refused-list match in a candidate's table
  makes that candidate fail this requirement, logged (research R15); every
  constant of Π and the mode, padding constants and domain separators included, has its rule and
  reason in the rationale (FR-034).
- **FR-024**: Rotation amounts and every other per-node constant MUST be generated by stated
  rules, chosen by the trail bounds they give, with no non-trivial grid symmetry and with a stated
  scaling to every word width (FUT-GRID-4). Amounts equal at every node are mapped to themselves by
  every translation, so the amounts differ between nodes: each candidate states K ≥ 2 sets of
  amounts and a placement rule from n, in its log entry before its search, whose placement has no
  symmetry of the torus that moves a node at any claimable size; one set at the three nodes (0,0),
  (0,1) and (1,2) and another elsewhere has none at 4×4, 8×8 and 16×16 (computed on 2026-11-01). At
  2×2 and 3×3, the research sizes, the placement of the amounts is checked
  together with the round constants as one pattern, as that of the food nodes
  is (FR-018; research R13). They are chosen at the D4 width, where both
  bounds are computed: each candidate's amounts are searched first at 16, the width research R6
  tries first, with the width recorded in its log entry before the search, and if R6's rule then
  fixes the D4 width at 8, every entered candidate's amounts are searched again at
  8, with the same recorded space of amounts, K and placement rule, under
  the same ranking rule, as a new log entry, and the scores at 8 use the
  amounts found at 8. No choice is involved, since R6's rule fixes the width.
  The scaling rule to every other width is stated before the search. Both searches are trail
  searches on the candidate and count against its trail row (research R7); a point whose search
  reaches its cap has no bound and ranks below every point with both bounds, and a candidate with no
  point that has both bounds at the search width has no amounts there and is not scored at that
  width (research R15). Before the search runs, the candidate's log entry records the space of
  amounts, K, the placement rule and the width. The ranking is one rule for every candidate,
  recorded as a `settled` entry of the protocol change log before the first rotation search: the
  measure is the D4 score's own, the −log₂ of the weaker of the two bounds at the search width
  (min(W_D, W_L) in research R6), at one number of rounds for every candidate, fixed in that entry;
  ties go to the smallest amounts in the order that entry states; and an assignment ranks by its
  weakest box where the K sets make distinct boxes (FR-022). A space whose projection, with the
  row's other runs, does not fit the 48 h row is refused by the Budget guard (cli contract): the
  search is inconclusive and the candidate `ineligible`, and a smaller space makes a new candidate
  within the stop rule. The amounts never change after a point's bound is seen. The full ranking is
  a committed record, and the amounts chosen are its first entry. R_bound at w = 32 is computed
  with the scaled amounts, and the rationale reports whether the ranking held after scaling,
  without choosing again. Every amount, after scaling, lies in 1 to w − 1 at every supported
  width; the constant script and the operation list refuse 0 and w, and where scaling would
  give 0 or w at w = 4 the rule names the allowed amount to use instead. Across widths and sizes
  only w, n and the values derived from them by stated rules change (FR-032). For eligibility,
  decided for every candidate before selection, the report on the ranking after scaling comes from
  the candidate's log entry (section "Pre-registered Design Protocol").
- **FR-025**: The hardware structure of Π and the mode MUST meet these rules, each stated in the
  design document (feature-002 O1; FUT-HW-1, FUT-HW-5; security.md §13; checklist group E). For
  eligibility, decided for every candidate before selection, the stages, the chains per phase and
  the key path come from the candidate's operation-list declarations, checked by code; the interface
  formats, the call sequence and the zeroization rules are properties of the mode, the same for
  every candidate, decided once in the mode chapter (section "Pre-registered Design Protocol"):
  - **Stages.** On any path, each pipeline stage of the round structure holds at most one serial
    w-bit carry chain from the round's updates, by the phase rule (FR-019), and at most one more
    from a public counter held as 32-bit words on the same path, so the same count applies at every
    width (FR-022); a candidate whose ARX-box needs an addition and a dependent operation in one
    step spreads them over two phases. A stage is one colour phase and takes
    one clock cycle, so a round of P phases takes P cycles, where P is the number of phases in one
    round of the candidate's operation list at that size: a colour visited twice counts twice, and P
    = 2 is the case of a red-black schedule that visits each colour once. FR-020's formulas,
    FR-026's rate and cost per output bit and research R5's cost model use that one P, recorded per
    candidate and size in its log entry (data-model §3), and carry chains are
    counted per phase. Within a stage every update reads the values at the
    phase's start, as the interpreters do, which the phase rule, checked by the
    loader at every supported size, makes equal to list order (FR-019). A
    subtraction counts as one chain, as an
    addition does; a public counter held as 32-bit words counts where it sits on a stage's
    path, and not when it is registered on a path of its own. The design document gives each
    stage's estimated delay against the 5 ns of 200 MHz on the XC7A100T −1, with its source, and
    the LUT levels on its longest path, as estimates that feature 008's post-route timing
    checks; they are reported, not eligibility rules.
  - **Registers and read-back.** All state is registered, and there is no read-back path for
    key or state. This covers the RTL's ports and, on the board, the configuration readback and
    any debug core (an integrated logic analyser, JTAG user registers): feature 008 builds the
    production bitstream with readback security set and no debug core, and records it. The design
    document gives the port list, with every port's direction, width, value after configuration and
    value after reset, the command interface's ports, `selftest_fail`, `call_done`, the error signal
    and the error-code output among them, the last two zero after reset (HW-078) and
    `selftest_fail` low after configuration and, after a reset, unchanged until the
    self-test ends, when a pass clears it and a failure sets it (FR-059; HW-081), and `call_done`, a
    registered output that marks each call's end, low after configuration and after reset, low from
    each call's start, its command word taken while `call_done` is high, a zeroize taken while a
    call runs or, for the self-test, reset's release, to that call's end, a word refused while a
    call runs leaving it as it would be without that word (FR-020; HW-091), and
    high from the cycle in which the call ends until the next call starts,
    `failed` included, where a failed self-test's end leaves it high (FR-020;
    HW-086), and the rule of no read-back path is checked against it (FUT-HW-5).
    Constructed states (FR-054) run on the RTL in simulation only, loaded by the testbench
    (VHDL-2008 external names or `force`), so the synthesizable RTL has no load or read-back
    port; on the board, FUT-HW-4's equivalence uses the vectors a key reaches, through the
    normal interface. In the model, `Generator` exposes no state, key or counter through any
    public method or attribute, and `pi`, `pi_inv`, `pi_trace` and `ResearchDuplex`, named
    research-only, are the only places that show state (FR-033); Python cannot make memory
    unreadable, so this rule concerns the interface.
  - **Key loading and reset.** The design document specifies the key-loading interface and the
    reset sequence: serial key loading along the registered shift chain below, the four latencies
    stated below, an output-valid signal, and the zeroization of key and state. The key-loading
    interface is generic in the key's length k, which is c at a keyed reduced variant and 256 at a
    claimable size: a k-bit key, with the k/2-bit nonce given at each instantiate on the
    absorption interface (research R14), 256 and 128 bits at every claimable
    size, both whole 32-bit words, since a keyed variant's c is a multiple of
    64; keyless variants have none, and no core is built at one: the generics refuse a keyless
    variant at elaboration, its permutation being tested alone in FUT-HW-5's simulation, and a core
    built at a keyed research variant runs the reset sequence and its self-test as at a claimable
    size, from that variant's `selftest_<variant>.json` (FR-059; HW-095); FUT-GRID-2's key loading
    "for every supported size" is met through the keyed variant each size has (FR-018). The core is
    vendor-neutral and takes its clock as an input, on the device's clock network; clock generation
    belongs to the board wrapper: a wrapper of feature 008, outside the core and the only file with
    vendor primitives, derives the clock from the 100 MHz oscillator through an MMCM, and the
    reports give the wrapper's and the core's timing apart (FUT-HW-1, FUT-HW-2). The wrapper's
    duties toward the core's reset are stated here and carried to feature 008, as the clock split
    is: it holds the core's reset while the MMCM reports no lock and for a stated number of cycles
    after lock, releases it synchronously to the derived clock through a two-stage
    synchronizer, synchronizes every asynchronous reset request (from the board,
    a button or the host) the same way, and asserts reset again whenever
    lock is lost, so that the core sees only a synchronous reset. Reset is
    synchronous and reaches the nodes through a registered
    fan-out tree whose latency is part of the reset sequence, and the round and phase counters
    are replicated, one copy at each food node or node, all started by that reset and running
    in lockstep, so that no counter value crosses the grid. The latency is given as four figures in
    cycles, fixed by public values: from reset released to the self-test done and the state
    zeroized (ready for a key), the self-test's key and nonce words fed through the chains and, for
    rate lanes, through the absorption tree into the nonce registers, and its words fed through the
    absorption interface's selector, the personalization string's length
    word and the reseed input's length word and words, included (FR-059;
    HW-082); key loading, from the last key word accepted to the key held,
    the end of `loading` ("Call sequence"; HW-087), for a finished load, a
    call of its own whose cycle count joins FR-020's list; and instantiate, as two figures: from the
    personalization string's length word accepted to the copy done, the length check and the copy
    included, with what of the nonce registers' transfer and the nonce chain's shift runs after that
    word (HW-083), and from the personalization string's last word accepted, its
    length word for the empty string, to instantiate done, its forget call complete and the core
    `instantiated` and ready for a command, the last block's absorption, the calls of Π, the trees'
    latency and forget included (FR-018). The cycles between the two, the string's earlier blocks
    and their calls of Π, and those before the first, from the command word through the nonce's
    words, are in FR-020's instantiate formula, which counts every cycle of the call but those in
    which the core waits for the host (HW-075). The copy waits for the length word, accepted and
    found within the design's maximum, so that an over-long string is refused before anything
    changes ("Flow control"); the design document states whether the nonce registers' transfer and
    the nonce chain's shift end before the core takes that word, its ready low until they do, or
    wait until it is taken, so that neither figure depends on the host's pace (FR-020), and for the
    empty string the second figure includes the first (HW-083). A generate
    request's own figure runs from its acceptance on the command interface
    ("Flow control") to its first output valid, and the figure of a zeroize from `loading`
    ("Call sequence") is stated with the other triggers' ("Zeroization").
    The key path is specified with them: a key register at each node that holds key bits, loaded
    once per key along a registered shift chain through those nodes and copied into its lanes in one
    cycle at every instantiate; this is the key path that FR-030's `mapping` entry holds among the
    candidate's own components, its nodes coming from the candidate's operation-list declarations
    (FR-034; RAT-078). The host gives the nonce on the absorption interface at each
    instantiate, in the model's word order; it reaches its lanes through the absorption tree where
    they are rate lanes, and otherwise along a nonce chain of its own beside the key's, through the
    nodes that hold nonce bits in lanes outside the rate, so the key chain shifts only at key
    loading. Each rate lane that holds nonce bits has a nonce register at its node, filled through
    the absorption tree from the nonce words' transfer on the absorption interface; at the copy the
    selector writes that register into the lane, never as an XOR, so that every lane is set in that
    one cycle and the initial state is the one FR-013 and research R10 place. Each chain
    passes, registered, through the nodes between those that hold its bits, so
    no path of neighbours is required, and its length counts those registers. At each instantiate a
    selector at every lane sets the lane, in the same cycle as the copy, from one of
    four sources: a key register, the nonce chain, the nonce register of a
    rate lane that holds nonce bits, or its stated constant (FR-013), and
    the selectors and the nonce registers are parts of FR-026's estimate.
    The key chain's length enters the key-loading figure, and the length check,
    the copy and what of the nonce chain's shift and the nonce registers'
    transfer runs after the length word the instantiate's first figure (HW-083),
    the plan's Complexity Tracking lists both chains as local data paths of
    public length, and zeroization clears both chains, the nonce registers and every copy.
  - **Call sequence.** The design document gives the call sequence as a state table, with the calls
    each state accepts, each call starting with its command word on the command interface ("Flow
    control"). `not-reset`, the state after FPGA configuration and before the first reset, accepts
    no call: every register of the core has a stated initial value of zero, given as initial values
    in the VHDL that configuration applies, the error-code register among them, where zero means no
    error and the codes start at 1, and output-valid stays low until a reset has run the self-test
    (FR-059); feature 008's board wrapper also holds reset after configuration, until the MMCM locks
    and for a stated number of cycles after lock ("Key loading and reset"). `resetting` runs from
    reset released to the self-test done and the state zeroized, and is the only state in which the
    self-test's selectors, at the key chain's input, at the nonce chain's input and at the
    absorption interface, select its constants; every other state selects the interfaces (FR-059;
    SEC-082), and FR-008's risk names each of them held on its constants,
    alone or with the others (SEC-088). At a keyed research variant `resetting`
    is the same, its self-test running from that variant's inputs, k and
    expected output (FR-059; HW-095). `failed`, after a failed self-test,
    accepts no call until the next reset, with `selftest_fail`, an output of its own, latched until
    a self-test passes: it stays set through `resetting` after that reset,
    and the end of a self-test that passes clears it (FR-059; HW-081). In `not-reset`, `resetting`,
    `zeroizing` and `failed` the core holds ready low on the command, key-loading and
    absorption interfaces and output-valid low, so no call is accepted and no error code is raised.
    The table gives each input interface's ready in every state: the key-loading
    interface's ready is high only in `loading`, until its last key word is taken
    (HW-087), the absorption interface's only in `instantiating` and `reseeding`,
    and the command interface's in each state that accepts a command word, as the table lists; a
    word offered while its interface's ready is low waits, its valid held, and is neither taken nor
    refused and raises no error, so that only a word the core takes can be refused (HW-080).
    `no-key`, after a reset with a passed self-test or after a zeroization, accepts a key load only,
    which enters `loading` at its command word; `loading` runs from that command word to the
    key held, the stretch after the last key word included, the key-loading
    interface's ready low once the last key word is taken (HW-087), and accepts
    the key words and zeroize only, the key being held only after the last
    word, so an instantiate is refused with its code and changes nothing, and a zeroize, one before
    the first key word included, clears what "Zeroization" lists, the chain among it, and
    returns to `no-key`, where a new load starts afresh, every counter of
    "Zeroization"'s list at zero (HW-085; HW-089); `key-held` accepts instantiate and
    zeroize; an instantiate is in a state of its own, `instantiating`, from
    its command word to instantiate done, and a reseed in `reseeding`, from
    its command word to its end, each accepting its input's words on the absorption interface and,
    on the command interface, zeroize only; `instantiated` accepts the calls the model's call
    sequence allows, and zeroize; a generate request is in `generating`, from its command word to
    its forget call's end, the stretch while a block waits in the buffer included, which accepts
    zeroize only on the command interface, the key-loading and absorption interfaces' ready low; and
    a zeroization by a zeroize or by a zeroizing refusal is in `zeroizing`, from the zeroize's
    command word or the refusal to `no-key`, which accepts no call, a word offered then waiting with
    its valid held (HW-087). From any call's command word to its end, `call_done` low throughout
    ("Registers and read-back"; HW-086), whether the core waits for the host or runs Π, the stretch
    while a block waits in the buffer included, the command interface accepts zeroize only, save in
    `zeroizing`, which accepts none (HW-087): every other command word is refused with the code of a
    call out of sequence, a malformed one with its own ("Flow control"; HW-090), and changes
    nothing, starting no call and leaving the running call's count, outputs and `call_done` as they
    would be without it (FR-020; HW-091), and a zeroize clears what "Zeroization" lists and goes to
    `no-key`, as a zeroize does in every state that accepts one (HW-076; HW-085). Every other call
    is refused with its error code ("Zeroization") and changes nothing, among them a key load while
    a key is held (zeroize first, so no state from the old key survives into the new key's use) and
    an instantiate with no key. The refusals match the model's `CallSequenceError` rules where the
    model has the state, and FUT-HW-5's "key zeroization on reset" is tested from the ports: after a
    reset, an instantiate is refused as `no-key`. The tests cover a call in
    each state of the table, `instantiating`, `reseeding`, `generating` and
    `zeroizing` among them (HW-087), each count measured at the ports, a word refused while a call
    runs among them (FR-020; HW-091), and a word offered on each input interface in
    each state (HW-080), and FR-020's zeroization formula covers each state that accepts a zeroize,
    `generating` among them (FR-059; HW-076, HW-087).
  - **Flow control.** Each interface (command, key loading, absorption, output) uses
    valid and ready, each input interface's ready in every state given by the call-state table
    ("Call sequence"; HW-080). A call ends in the cycle in which `call_done` rises ("Registers and
    read-back"): the host sends a command word other than zeroize once `call_done`
    and the command interface's ready are both high, and a zeroize whenever that ready is high
    (FR-020; HW-086). The core takes a command word in the cycle in which the command interface's
    valid and ready are both high; a word taken while `call_done` is high starts a call, one the
    state accepts or a refused request, and a word taken while a call runs, `call_done` low, starts
    no call of its own, a zeroize excepted, which ends the running call and starts its zeroization,
    counted from that word as "Call sequence" states, and any other word is refused with its code,
    changes nothing and leaves the running call's count, outputs and `call_done` as they
    would be without it (FR-020; HW-091). The command interface takes one
    32-bit word per call, with the command (key load, instantiate, generate,
    reseed, zeroize) in stated bits, a field named "command", since a call
    type is a kind of call of Π (FR-023; HW-079), and, for generate, the length in 32-bit words in
    the rest; the design document lists the command field's values, and a command word whose field
    names no command, or a key load, instantiate, reseed or generate word with a bit set outside its
    command field and, for generate, its length field, is refused before anything changes, in every
    state whose command interface takes words, with a code of its own among those of a refusal that
    changes nothing, a malformed command word ("Zeroization"), while a word whose command field
    names zeroize zeroizes in every state that accepts one, whatever its other bits hold, so that a
    malformed zeroize never leaves the key and the state in place, FUT-HW-5's tests covering each
    case (HW-090); a refused command is answered on the error signal and the
    error-code output, which hold its code until the next command word is accepted, the word that
    starts the next call, or a reset ("Zeroization"; HW-078), the code of a word refused while a
    call runs being held the same way unless the running call raises a code of
    its own, which replaces it (FR-020; HW-091), and the length word of a
    personalization string or a reseed input stays on the absorption interface.
    An instantiate's input on the absorption interface is the nonce's words, in the model's word
    order, then the personalization string's length word, then the string's words, if any; the empty
    string's length word is a personalization word, so every instantiate has one (HW-075). The core
    checks the length word when it takes it, before the copy that sets the lanes from the key and
    the nonce, which waits for it ("Key loading and reset"), so that a length over the design's
    maximum is a bad request refused before the lanes, the key or a counter changes: the refusal
    clears the nonce chain, the rate lanes' nonce registers and the absorption tree's registers that
    carried the nonce, which hold only that call's words, and the core returns to the state it was
    in, `key-held` or `instantiated`, the latter with the state the refused instantiate would have
    replaced, from which a new instantiate may follow, as the model's may (model contract,
    "Lengths", "Failure paths"); the tests cover a refused instantiate in
    `key-held` and in `instantiated` and the instantiate after it (HW-083).
    The core never stalls in mid-call: output leaves through a buffer of one block (r bits), and the
    next call starts only after the consumer has taken the block, so a stall, which the consumer
    sets in public, holds only the buffer and delays the next call, and no cycle count depends on
    secret data. The buffer is read out to the output interface's width i in ⌈r/i⌉ transfers per
    block, which the read-out's count of transfers within a block counts, and the read-out stops at
    a request's last word within its last block, which the request's count of remaining words gives,
    both counters on the list of "Zeroization" (FR-026; HW-094). From any
    call's command word to its end, whether the core waits for the host or
    runs Π, the command interface accepts zeroize only, save in `zeroizing`, which accepts none
    ("Call sequence"; HW-076, HW-087): while a block waits in the buffer, it accepts zeroize, which
    clears what "Zeroization" lists, the buffer, the trees and the state
    among it, and goes to `no-key` (HW-085), and no other call is accepted
    until the block is taken or a zeroize or reset clears it. Draining a
    block therefore never overlaps the next call of Π, and FR-026's rate
    counts the drain; a design that overlaps them needs a second buffer, which the design document
    may describe as a variant with its own cycle formula, size and clearing rule. The one-block
    buffer is the output register that zeroization clears, and it and the
    registers of both trees, the absorption and output trees, are cleared when a
    request ends, with or before its forget call, so the rest of a partial last
    block is discarded by clearing, not left in place; the clearing's cycles join FR-020's formulas,
    and feature 006 checks it in simulation (FR-058). The model holds no buffer. The start signal
    reaches the nodes and the replicated counters through a registered
    fan-out tree with one stated latency to every node, as reset does, no greater than
    the reset tree's ("Zeroization"; HW-088), so lockstep holds; a start
    signal goes out for each call of Π, a request's forget call included, and that call's type
    travels with it on that tree, as a field of ⌈log₂ t⌉ bits for the t call types of FR-023
    registered beside it at every node, and selects each food node's ROM stream where the call types
    draw on distinct streams (FR-023); a key load and a zeroize, which run no call of Π, send no
    start signal (HW-079). The buffer's size and the tree's latency enter the cycle formulas of
    FR-020, which count every cycle of a call except those in which the core waits for
    the host, on every interface, and the plan's Complexity Tracking. The
    design document states the interface formats: 32-bit transfers on the command, key-loading and
    absorption interfaces; a personalization string or a reseed input preceded by
    one 32-bit word giving its length in bytes, refused above the design's maximum; unused bytes of
    a last partial word that must be zero, a word with a nonzero unused byte being an error path,
    with its error code ("Zeroization"), since it can arrive after earlier blocks of the same input
    have been absorbed; each block of the absorption and output interfaces a whole number of 32-bit
    words, since a keyed variant's r is a multiple of 32 (FR-018; HW-093); and
    the key and the nonce as whole 32-bit words, since a keyed variant's c
    is a multiple of 64 (research R14), in a stated word and byte order, matching the model's. A
    generate request gives its length in 32-bit words in its command word, so a length that is
    not a whole number of words cannot be expressed, as the model refuses an `nbits` that is not a
    positive multiple of 32, and a length of zero is refused.
  - **Zeroization.** It clears, for every trigger and from every state, this one list, which
    "Call sequence" and "Flow control" cite: the lanes, every pipeline register, the registers of
    both trees, the absorption and output trees, those of the start tree with the
    call-type field at every node, so that a start signal still on its way when a
    zeroization's clear reaches a register is dropped there (HW-088), the
    key-loading buffer (the key chain's registers) and the nonce chain's registers
    beside it, the rate lanes' nonce registers, the held key and its copies, and the output
    register (the one-block buffer of "Flow control"), and it returns every counter of the core to
    zero, its value after reset, so that a call after a zeroize made in mid-call counts from zero,
    as the tests of each state expect (HW-085; HW-089). The core's counters, listed here once, each
    with the part of FR-026's estimate it falls in, are the replicated round and phase counters, a
    part of their own; the limit counters, one for each limit of FR-007 that the core counts, a part
    of their own, whose integrity FR-034's paths that carry secret data name; the read-out's
    counters, in the part of the output buffer with its read-out to the interface width: its count
    of transfers within a block, up to ⌈r/i⌉, and the request's count of remaining words, which
    stops the read-out at the request's last word ("Flow control"; HW-094); and the controller's
    counters, in the controller's part: the request's block count that FR-059's k counts, the key
    words taken in `loading`, the nonce's words, and the words of a personalization string or
    a reseed input counted against its length word (HW-089). A zeroizing
    refusal's zeroization keeps the error signal and the error-code register, so
    that its code stays readable; every other trigger clears them with the
    rest (HW-078). Its triggers are reset, an explicit zeroize, a command word whose command field
    names zeroize, whatever its other bits hold ("Flow control"; HW-090), accepted
    in `loading`, `key-held`, `instantiating`, `reseeding`, `instantiated`
    and `generating` and from any call's command word to its end, save in
    `zeroizing` ("Call sequence"; HW-076, HW-087), a failed self-test, an error
    path and a call refused at a per-key limit (the call that would pass
    the limit; nothing happens when a counter merely reaches it), each with its latency in cycles
    and output-valid low throughout, an explicit zeroize's and a zeroizing refusal's in `zeroizing`
    ("Call sequence"; HW-087). Every trigger drives the reset's registered
    fan-out tree, so a zeroization reaches every register it clears within the reset's latency,
    which each trigger's figure counts. The design document states the start tree's latency as no
    greater than the reset tree's, so that a start sent before a zeroize reaches the nodes
    before its clear, the call it starts being cleared with the lanes, and no
    call of Π starts after a zeroization's clear; the tests cover a zeroize
    taken while a start signal is on its way (HW-088). A zeroization by an
    explicit zeroize, an error path or a per-key limit runs no self-test
    and goes to `no-key`; a failed self-test's zeroization goes to `failed` and starts nothing
    further; only reset runs the self-test ("Call sequence"). The core has a
    registered error-code output beside the error signal and valid with it,
    with one code for each refusal that changes nothing (a length, a call out of sequence, a
    malformed command word ("Flow control"; HW-090), any other limit) and one for each
    zeroizing refusal (a per-key limit, an error path), listed in the design
    document, so the host can tell from the ports which refusals zeroized. The error signal and its
    code are registered and stay valid from the refusal until the next command word is accepted on
    the command interface, the word that starts the next call ("Flow control"; HW-091),
    or until a reset, which returns both to zero, their value after reset
    being in the port list; a refusal follows only a command word or an input word of the call it
    ends, or a command word refused while a call runs, so a code is replaced only once the host has
    sent another command or another input word of the running call, after as many cycles as it likes
    to read it, a running call's own code replacing that of a word refused while it runs (HW-091).
    The host reads the code from the ports with no handshake, as it reads
    `selftest_fail`, and FUT-HW-5's tests cover a code kept through its zeroization
    and a code replaced by the next command (HW-078). An error path is a
    condition the RTL detects after the state has changed: the design document lists every such
    condition, each of which zeroizes, raises the error signal with a zeroizing code and needs a new
    key, among them a last partial word with a nonzero unused byte ("Flow control"), and lists every
    other check made part-way through an input with its class; a bad request that valid and
    ready refuse before anything changes is a refusal that changes nothing, a
    personalization string's length word over the design's maximum among them, checked
    before the copy ("Flow control"; HW-083); reset and clock glitches stay
    with feature 008 (FR-008). Every path that leaves key-dependent state behind is specified and
    tested (FR-058): a refused request changes nothing, and the object or device stays usable; a
    failed self-test zeroizes and leaves it unusable, the device until the next reset, which runs
    the self-test again (FR-059), while in the model no object exists (FR-058); an exception after
    the state has changed zeroizes the whole state and ends the object, so no half-updated state
    survives; and a call refused at a per-key limit zeroizes and ends the object, since nothing more
    may be done with that key. After reset or zeroization, output-valid stays low and the output
    register holds zeros until an instantiate requested by the host completes after a passed
    self-test, and a generate request before then is refused with an error signal, as the model
    raises `CallSequenceError`. The self-test's blocks go only to its comparator, which takes each
    whole r-bit block as the output buffer receives it and each of the block's words as the read-out
    gives it at the interface width, with no consumer handshake, the read-out stepping on
    its own and the output port's valid held low, so they never reach the
    output port while the self-test reaches the read-out's counters (HW-094),
    and the zeroization after the self-test clears what it computed, both
    chains and the rate lanes' nonce registers included (FR-059). In the model, zeroization
    overwrites the object's own `bytearray` copy of the key with zeros in place and drops it, and
    drops the state's integers, which Python cannot overwrite; as with constant time, this is a
    discipline that governs what the object keeps, not what Python's memory may still hold, and the
    caller's own copy of the key is outside it (model contract). That sentence says what the model
    does not promise, so it stands through an allow-list entry of the claim test (FR-069).
  - **Usage rules across resets.** A reset or a zeroization returns the device to a state that
    accepts a new key, since a device cannot end itself as an object can. The per-key limits
    and counters then restart, so across resets they are usage rules for the caller, as across
    objects in the model (FR-007), and the design document lists them with the other usage
    rules. The behaviour on reset and clock glitches is deferred to feature 008, with its risk
    stated (FR-008).
- **FR-026**: The design document MUST state, for every supported size, the output bits per call
  of Π (r) and per round (r/R), and the throughput these imply at the 200 MHz target with the
  stated pipeline. With P phases per round (FR-025) and an output interface of i bits per cycle, the
  implied rate per call, on FR-020's formulas, is r/(P·R + ⌈r/i⌉ + t_s + t_o) bits per cycle, with
  t_s the start tree's latency, no greater than the reset tree's (FR-025 "Zeroization";
  HW-088), taken by each call of Π, a request's forget call included (FR-025
  "Flow control"; HW-079), and t_o the output tree's where it is not overlapped,
  since the next call starts only after the consumer has taken the block
  (FR-025 "Flow control"), and the cost per output bit counts P·R cycles per
  call of Π. The output interface's width is
  stated per size (for example 32 bits per cycle at every size, as the vector files list words,
  or a stated wider bus), and the implied throughput follows from that rate, with r/(P·R) and i
  reported beside it. It is stated for stated request sizes (the
  2¹⁹-bit cap, and 4,096 words as in the long-run digest), counting each request's
  output calls, its forget call, the trees' latency and the clearing at the
  request's end, with instantiate's calls spread over a stated number of requests
  per instantiation; the rate before forget calls is reported beside it as
  an upper bound. A rate below the goal of 10⁸ words per second (for example at most about
  0.48 × 10⁸ at 4×4 with R = 32, P = 2 and i = 32 before the trees' latencies are stated: 640 bits
  in 64 + 20 = 84 cycles plus t_s + t_o, about 7.6 bits per cycle at t_s = t_o = 0, before forget
  calls; the design document computes the example again once it states the trees' latencies) is
  reported and is never an eligibility rule. It MUST also give the estimated
  hardware cost per output bit, from the operation-count model of the nonlinearity choice rule,
  so that feature 008 can test it against Ascon, Trivium and Grain-128AEAD on the same board
  (strategy note §10), and, from the same model, the estimated LUTs, flip-flops and block RAM per
  claimable size, each part with its own figure: the nodes and stages (LUTs per node and round × n²
  for the stages built), the absorption and output trees, the reset and start fan-out trees with the
  call-type field at every node, the ROMs, the output buffer (r bits, 16,000 at 16×16)
  with its read-out to the interface width and the read-out's two counters
  (FR-025 "Zeroization"; HW-094), the key registers and the key-loading buffer,
  the nonce chain and the rate lanes' nonce registers, the instantiate lane
  selectors (up to 2w·n² bits, estimated per state bit and stated where merged
  with stage logic), the command interface, the controller with the call-state table and its
  counters, the error-code register, the replicated round and phase counters, the limit counters,
  each counter in the part FR-025 "Zeroization" gives it (HW-089), the self-test's sequencer,
  its comparator of r bits, its input selectors, the one at each chain's input and the one at the
  absorption interface, and its key, nonce, the empty personalization string's
  length word (RAT-079), reseed input with its length word (HW-082) and expected output, k·r bits
  per generate request with k computed as FR-059 states (HW-084), held as constants, and the 2w·n²
  state flip-flops with the pipeline registers; block RAM is expected to be
  0 unless a ROM is mapped to it. A part is stated negligible only with its figure
  and the reason. The total is compared with the XC7A100T's 63,400 LUTs and
  126,800 flip-flops, naming the largest size expected to fit, as an estimate that feature 008
  checks part by part against Vivado's hierarchical utilization report. Measured throughput belongs
  to features 006 and 008 (FUT-HW-3). For eligibility, every candidate's figures are computed before
  selection by the cost model from its operation list, P and R, as a `cost` record per size; the
  design document restates them for the selected candidate (Pre-registered Design Protocol,
  "Eligibility and selection"). The self-test's storage and cycles take there the least k that each
  `cost` record computes from a run of the candidate through the self-test's call sequence, its
  operation list interpreted by `research/004/ir.py` in the shared mode, recorded with the structure
  that set it, and the design document states the selected candidate's k equal to it or raised by a
  named structure with its reason (FR-020, FR-059; REP-097).
- **FR-027**: The design document MUST state the width, signedness, wrap rule, shift kind and
  comparison semantics of every operation, the update order, the direction of every data flow
  between neighbours, and the edge states of the model (Principle I; security.md §8 and §13), so
  that an implementation from it has nothing to guess; Π⁻¹ is written at the same level of
  detail (FR-019). Every word, counter and intermediate value MUST have an explicit bit width,
  ready for a fixed-width type such as `numeric_std` `unsigned`, and nothing MAY depend on the
  range of an integer type, which differs between VHDL-2008 and VHDL-2019 (vhdl-genai.md R4 and
  §4.3). Every state word, counter and intermediate is declared `unsigned`, and every operation
  of FR-055 is written as an unsigned operation; a signed type, resize or comparison appears
  only where FR-055 justifies it, with vectors at its sign boundary. Bit 0 is the least
  significant bit, words are `unsigned(w − 1 downto 0)`, rotl(x, k) moves bit i to bit
  (i + k) mod w, every shift and rotation amount lies in 1 to w − 1 (FR-024), and the order of
  lanes and nodes is stated. Truncation is stated too: the last output block of a request whose
  length is not a multiple of r keeps its first bits in FR-015's serialization order and
  discards the rest, and the padding rule fixes partial input blocks (FR-014). Nothing rounds,
  since nothing divides. For eligibility, every candidate's semantics are read before selection from
  its operation list's own declarations, which the framework requires for every operation (research
  R11), and the design document restates them for the selected candidate.
- **FR-028**: The round count R of every claimable size MUST be the value the pre-registered
  round rule gives at the freeze. R is computed per claimable size and may differ between sizes;
  the rationale explains its growth with n by the grid's diameter and the R_f and R_b found per
  size. Rounds MUST NOT be removed once results exist, and no round count or constant MAY be
  chosen from held-out results (feature-002 O6). A firing of the bias rule is reported as a
  distinguisher at its round count that raises R, with the family-wise rate of chance firings on
  uniform outputs for the T call types tested stated beside it, and is never measured again to
  remove it; the final report repeats the rate. The rate is 1 − 0.995^(T·N), or
  1 − 0.995^(T·N)·∏(1 − 0.005·a)^T with the avalanche test, where N is the number
  of pairs of a claimable size and a round count r at which the tests ran
  with 2r above the R then in force, the only round counts at which a firing
  raises R: with one R at every size and no rise, N = 3⌈R/2⌉, so that the
  rate is about 21% and 24% at R = 32 with one stream, T = 1, and about 62% and 66% with T = 4
  (research R4); beside a firing, N counts the pairs tested up to that firing's record, and the
  final report and FR-041's statement of the selection bias count every pair tested by the
  freeze, each size's own R and every rise included, and `round_rule.py` computes N from the records
  (ANA-075); the avalanche test's term counts its runs the same way, size by size, the product taken
  over the claimable sizes at which it ran up to that record, or by the freeze for the final report,
  a being 2 at a size where it measured at most 128 round counts and m/64 where it measured
  m above 128, research R4's chance for its two counts, so that with every size run and none
  above 128 the rate is 1 − 0.995^(T·N)·0.99^(3T), and `round_rule.py` computes a
  from the records with N (ANA-079). Before the freeze, a change is a new
  design-document version and a log entry. After the freeze, any change to a frozen file, a
  raise of R by feature 005, a new claimable size, or the correction of a disagreement between
  the frozen design document and the frozen model (edge cases), MUST be a new model ID with its
  own design-document version, model, vectors and freeze.
- **FR-029**: R_f and R_b, the numbers of rounds after which every output bit of Π (respectively
  Π⁻¹) depends on every input bit, MUST be computed exactly for every supported size. A round
  is one pass over every colour phase in order, with that round's constants (research R11); R,
  R_dist, R_bound, R_f, R_b and the r/R of FR-026 all count rounds, and a phase is one pipeline
  stage (FR-025). "Computed exactly" means the exact structural propagation of research R13,
  which over-approximates actual dependence: full structural dependence is necessary, not
  sufficient, for full diffusion, and the avalanche test of research R4, with its cross-check,
  observes the actual dependence. Instantiation MUST make every state bit depend on every key
  and nonce bit before the first output (FUT-GRID-3): the calls of Π and the rounds between key
  loading and the first output are stated, and the dependency propagation through instantiate
  as a whole shows that dependence, with the margin over R_f stated.
- **FR-030**: The design document MUST state the slime-mould mapping of every component
  (pressure and vein lanes, flux coupling, vein accumulation, food nodes, peristaltic rotations)
  and show, in a chapter of its own, "Replaced mechanisms" (FR-034), outside the mapping chapter,
  how each failed `phyco-v2` mechanism (S2, S5–S7, S11–S15) is replaced: one row for each, naming
  the rule of Π that replaces it and the evidence record, or the section and test where the answer
  is by specification. A record of FR-037, FR-038 or FR-039 is cited where a
  measurement or search shows that the failure does not recur (S2, S7, S12–S15, S8's rotational
  pairs, and S11's trail-bound records with the D4 choice record), for example S13's merging against
  bijectivity by construction and the exhaustive check, and S12 and S14 against the full-range rule
  and the bias rule. Where the answer is a property of the specification, the design document's
  section that states it and the test that checks it are cited (S1 and S3 the key and the security
  target, S4 the mode, S5 the full call's rate output, FR-015, S6 the fixed operations, FR-020, S9
  and S10 bijectivity by construction and the generic bound). The lessons table of FR-051, in
  security.md and in the final report, cites that chapter's rows with their
  records (FR-051). FR-030 covers the mechanisms of the step function. The
  others are answered elsewhere: S1 and S3 (the seed bound) by the key and
  the security target, S4 (no key, nonce or reseed) by the mode, S8 (seed words shared between
  pressures) by key loading and the rotational pairs of research R4, and S9 and S10 (bijection
  and cycles) by bijectivity by construction and the generic bound. The mapping chapter states
  what the mapping contributes (names, the structure of lanes and nodes, each component's place
  in the metaphor) and what it does not, in the fixed sentence below, answering security.md §8 both
  ways. For eligibility, every candidate's mapping is an entry of its own in the candidate log,
  written before its first score, naming each component's place in the metaphor and the attack
  class, bound or need it serves. The entry holds the candidate's own components, the five above,
  the colour schedule, the local linear layer between boxes, the round constants, its key path,
  FR-025's key register at each node that holds key bits with the key shift chain through those
  nodes, from the candidate's operation-list declarations, and any other it adds (RAT-078); the
  structures that every candidate shares, those of the mode and the core, which FR-034's component
  list names, from the padding rule, the domain separators and forget to the trees, the counters
  with their constant ROMs, the nonce chain and its registers, the selector at every lane, the
  output buffer, the call-state controller with its error-code register and the `selftest_fail`
  latch and the self-test's parts (RAT-078), take their roles from FR-025
  and FR-059, which FR-034's component list cites for them in place of the
  entry, and have no place in the metaphor, which the mapping chapter states
  once, in a sentence of its own that uses none of the words its rule lists
  (RAT-073). For the selected candidate, the mapping chapter restates each
  component's place in the metaphor only, so that it stays free of security statements, and the
  attack class, bound or need each component serves is stated in the rationale's component list
  (FR-034), which cites the `mapping` entry; the owner design review checks that the chapter and
  the rationale's list agree with the entry. In the rationale's list every
  component names at least one attack class it resists, a bound it supports or a
  functional need (bijectivity, locality, hardware cost); one with none is
  labelled "kept for the mapping only", with its cost stated, or is removed. No
  security argument MAY rest on the mapping, or on chaos, Lyapunov exponents, ergodicity, emergent
  complexity, biological unpredictability, or battery results offered as evidence of mixing or
  security (strategy note §5.1 and §10). Every security statement in the rationale cites a bound
  record, an analysis, the mode theorem or a structural proof; the mapping chapter contains no
  security statement, and none cites it. A statement whose only support is the mapping is a finding.
  Two checks find one: an item of the owner design review, "no security statement rests on the
  mapping", and a rule of the claim-register test (FR-069) that flags security wording inside the
  mapping chapter: whole words, ignoring case, from this list with its inflections: security,
  secure, securely, insecure, strength, strengths, strong, resist, resists, resisted, resisting,
  resistance, resistant, unpredictable, unpredictability, chaos, chaotic, Lyapunov, ergodic,
  ergodicity; and, for the arguments forbidden above, emergent, emergence, complexity, biological,
  mix, mixes, mixed, mixing, diffusion, diffuse, diffuses, avalanche, battery and batteries, so that
  a mixing or diffusion claim made through the mapping is flagged whatever it cites. The one
  negative statement the chapter must make is fixed text, held by one allow-list entry with that
  reason, the chapter's only entry: "The slime-mould mapping contributes no security property." Any
  other match in the chapter is flagged, so the chapter uses none of the listed words elsewhere, and
  a test runs the rule on sample sentences of both kinds. The chapter is the design document's
  section headed "Slime-mould mapping", from that heading to the next heading of the same level, and
  the test exits 1 if the heading is missing or appears twice. The mapping chapter keeps each
  component's place in the metaphor and its one fixed sentence, so that its word rule and its one
  allow-list entry stand as they are, and the claim test's other rules apply to the chapter
  "Replaced mechanisms" as to the rest of the design document (RAT-071).
- **FR-031**: The threshold-triggered disruption (Frustrated Foraging) MUST be removed from Π
  (D5); the food forcing becomes round-constant injection.

**Reduced variants**

- **FR-032**: Reduced variants MUST be defined from the same generics: word widths 8, 16 and 32,
  plus 4 for exhaustive checks; fewer rounds; the research grid sizes; and reduced capacities,
  which the research sizes need. "The same generics" means that the operation list's structure
  (its operations, their forms and order, and the colour schedule's rule) is the same at every
  width and size, and only w, n and the values derived from them by stated rules change: the
  rotation amounts by the scaling rule and the placement of their K sets by
  its rule from n (FR-024), the constants by the constant rule, the colour
  count by n's parity, the food-node positions by their rule, c, and the key and nonce lengths.
  Anything else makes a different design. Each variant MUST have its own variant ID, which
  carries its R, and its bound MUST be stated (FR-009): the size-profile row of a keyed reduced
  variant states its `bound_bits` as a bound on that variant only, with its checks made at s =
  c/2, labelled "research variant, no claim" (FR-006; SEC-096). A record
  that depends on R holds for the variant it names only, so after a rise of
  R every such record is made again at the new variant (round rule). Each
  research variant's R is stated with its reason: the round rule's value
  where its inputs exist at that variant, or a reduced count chosen for a stated test and
  marked as reduced. The D4 score (by its rule) and exhaustive bijectivity (2×2, w = 4) may rest
  on reduced variants; R_bound, R_f, R_b and the bias evidence come from the production width
  at each claimable size. A reduced-variant result transfers as evidence about the design, by
  the same generics, never as a value of the round rule. At 2×2, where a node's opposite neighbours
  coincide (FR-018), a result transfers only for properties that do not depend on the neighbourhood:
  bijectivity, which holds by construction at each size where the loader's check of the phase rule
  passes (FR-019), and the validation of tools whose behaviour does not depend on it (box and S-box
