  tables, the bijectivity bitmap, the cost model's counts). The dependency propagation is validated
  on its stated small operation list at 3×3 and 4×4, with hand-derived matrices at each, and the
  symmetry check, beside S7's result, on stated lists with planted torus symmetries (a translation,
  a rotation by 90° and a reflection) at n = 3 and 4, which it must find, and one with none, which
  it must clear (research R13); 2×2's R_f, R_b, symmetries and invariants are facts
  about 2×2 alone, never evidence about a claimable size. The reduced variants a candidate uses
  as evidence (widths, sizes, round counts, capacities) are logged in its `parameters` before
  its first tool run; one added later is logged with its reason before its first run, and
  every result on it is reported, used or not.
- **FR-033**: The production interface MUST accept only the production parameter sets (a
  claimable size, w = 32, the frozen capacity and the frozen R for that size) and MUST refuse
  every reduced variant, as the development tools refuse held-out seeds. In the model the
  production interface is `Generator`; `pi`, `pi_inv`, `pi_trace` and `ResearchDuplex`, named
  research-only in the module's docstring, are the only places that run reduced variants or
  show state (FR-025). Π⁻¹ is research-only and never part of the production interface.

**Design document, model and vectors**

- **FR-034**: A versioned design document for `phyco-v3` MUST cover every Phase 0 item of
  security.md §8, including the eight questions for a network of nodes, and a design rationale
  covering every rationale item of security.md §8 (strategy note §7.2). The rationale is its own
  file, `rationale.md`, versioned with the design document and frozen with it (FR-043). Its approval
  status, like the design document's, MUST be kept outside the frozen file (feature-002 O5), in the
  provenance log, where each review is a new entry (FR-064), and `design_freeze.py create` reads
  that status at HEAD and exits 2 while an AI-drafted section of the design document or of
  `rationale.md` has a latest entry that is not a review entry reading `accepted`, or one that names
  a row other than its review's with the highest k, the security-target review's for the design
  document's chapters "Security target" and "Mode" and the owner design review's for every other
  section, of the design document or of `rationale.md`, `rationale.md`'s chapters
  "Security target" and "Mode" among them, which the security-target review also
  reads (FR-043; RAT-082; RAT-086). The two documents MUST also meet these
  rules:
  - **Version.** The design document's header holds its title, the disclaimer and "Version: 0.<k>",
    1.0 once frozen, and nothing else, and `rationale.md`'s header holds its title, the design-form
    disclaimer and the same "Version: 0.<k>", and nothing else (RAT-087); "1.0 once
    frozen" is the name the freeze record gives the reviewed version, not a change of the line: the
    header keeps the 0.<k> that the owner design review read, and the approved freeze record names
    the design document's version 1.0, with `reviewed_version`, the header's 0.<k> that it hashes,
    so that no file changes at or after the freeze and the reviewed text is the frozen text,
    `rationale.md` keeping its 0.<k> the same way, and `protocol/evidence.py` gives a later record
    `design_version` 1.0 while the design document has the SHA-256 that the approved freeze
    record names, and the header's 0.<k> otherwise (RAT-080). Each committed change to either file
    before the freeze makes the next 0.<k> in both files' headers, in the same commit, so that a
    change to `rationale.md` alone also gives the design document its next version, which later
    records cite (RAT-087), and a revision after a decline or after the owner
    design review is logged under the candidate with its old and new version. `protocol/evidence.py`
    reads each record's `design_version` from that line, and the first record that cites a version
    stores the document as `design-versions/<sha256>.md`, as `spec-versions/` holds the spec
    (data-model §1); `design_freeze.py create` exits 2 on any other header line of either file, or
    while `rationale.md`'s version line differs from the design document's, which a test checks
    (RAT-087).
  - **Chapters.** The design document's chapters are level-2 headings with fixed names, each once
    and in this order, which a test checks: "Security target" (the properties sought, the threats,
    the parameter table, the generic bounds per size and the output limits, FR-004 to FR-012);
    "Mode" (the calls, the initial state, the theorem with each precondition and the feature that
    meets it, padding and domain separation with their constants and FR-014's proof, forget, the
    limits and counters, the interface formats, the call sequence and the zeroization rules that
    FR-025 makes properties of the mode, and its sections "Key loading", which the security-target
    review accepts, data-model §15, and "Diffusion through instantiation", FR-029's statement of the
    calls of Π and the rounds between key loading and the first output with the margin
    over R_f); "Permutation" (the operation list, the colour schedule, the
    rotation amounts, the round constants and the variant table, with its sections
    "Inverse", Π⁻¹ written out at Π's level of detail, FR-019, FR-027, and
    "Network questions", the answers to security.md §8's eight questions,
    which concern the grid and its update); "Hardware structure" (the rest of
    FR-025, FR-020's cycle formulas, FR-026's estimate and FR-059's self-test, with its section
    "Self-test"); "Replaced mechanisms" (FR-030's rows); and "Slime-mould mapping" (FR-030). Each
    edge state of FR-054, and every other Phase 0 item of security.md §8, stands in the
    chapter of what it concerns, and the chapters' test checks each named
    section (RAT-074). The security-target review covers "Security target" and "Mode", with
    `rationale.md`'s chapters of those names and the security target's files (the notes under the
    Review & Approval table), and the owner design review every chapter, with the rationale, whose
    Alternatives chapter is its own (RAT-069), and adds the review entries of every section of
    `rationale.md`, its chapters "Security target" and "Mode" among them (RAT-086). Wherever this
    feature says "the mode chapter", it means the design document's chapter "Mode", and
    `rationale.md`'s chapter of that name is named as `rationale.md`'s wherever it is meant; every
    theorem that `rationale.md`'s chapter "Mode" states the mode rests on is one the mode chapter
    uses, which `limits.json` lists and which must be `verified` before the owner design review and
    the freeze, as FR-043 and research R10 state for the mode chapter's own,
    the security-target review, which covers both chapters, checking it.
    `mode_limits_v3.py` reads three tables of the design document, each a Markdown table
    with fixed columns in a section of its own, which the chapters' test checks: the chapter
    "Permutation"'s section "Variant table", one row per variant with its ID, n, w, b, c, r, R and
    status, and the chapter "Mode"'s sections "Layout", which lanes form the rate part and which the
    capacity part at each size, and "Limits", each limit in calls of Π and in bits at every
    claimable size, with u and the two ways of choosing nonces, and exits 2 if a table is missing,
    appears twice or holds a row it cannot read, its runs comparing `design_sha256` (data-model §1);
    and the chapter "Security target"'s parameter table and output limits restate `parameters.json`
    and `limits.json`, a test comparing each with its file row by row and value by value, the key
    entropy, the reachable secret state, c, r, R and the bound among them, and failing on any
    difference, so that the security-target review reads one set of values (FR-010; RAT-085);
    `rationale.md` restates no value of either file, citing each by the design document's chapter
    and section or by the record's ID and SHA-256, and the same test reads it, failing on a line
    that writes a field of either file, or a symbol FR-010 gives one, k, s, c, r, R, u, ε or N, with
    a value outside such a citation, so that the frozen rationale states no value that its
    files do not hold and both reviews read one set of values, the three
    margins of the chapter "Margin", values of the round rule's records and of
    no field of either file, standing as its bullet states them (RAT-090).
    `gen_vectors_v3.py` reads the design document's table of k, a Markdown table with fixed columns
    in the chapter "Hardware structure"'s section "Self-test", which the chapters' test checks, with
    one row per claimable size and per keyed research variant holding k (HW-095), the
    structure that set it and, where k exceeds the least that the model's run
    gives, the structure the design document names with its reason, and it
    exits 2 if that table is missing, appears twice or holds a row it cannot read, its runs and
    checks comparing `design_sha256` (FR-059; data-model §1; REP-097).
  - **Rationale chapters.** `rationale.md`'s chapters are level-2 headings with fixed names, each
    once and in this order, which a test checks as it checks the design document's: "Security
    target", "Mode", "Components", "Nonlinearity", "Diffusion", "Constants", "Margin" and "Attack
    classes", one for each item of security.md §8's rationale list in its order, then "Network
    questions" and "Alternatives". "Security target", "Mode", "Nonlinearity" and "Diffusion" each
    state their item or cite, by chapter and section or by record ID and SHA-256, the design
    document's chapter or the evidence record that states it, a value of `parameters.json` or
    `limits.json` only by such a citation (RAT-090): the security target, the threat model
    and the claim template; the mode, its theorem and how each precondition is met; the sources of
    nonlinearity with their bound records; and R_f, R_b and FR-029's diffusion through
    instantiation. "Components" holds the component list below with what the slime-mould mapping
    does and does not contribute, "Margin" the round-count rule with the margin, and "Attack
    classes" also the known limitations, the classes left open or deferred among them; the parts
    below are these chapters, and SC-013 counts the coverage as the eight chapters present, each
    stating or citing its item (RAT-084).
  - **Components.** The rationale gives the role and the reason of every component: the two
    lanes per node, the coupling function, the vein accumulator, the colour schedule, the local
    linear layer between boxes, the food nodes and their placement, the rotation amounts, the
    round constants, the padding rule, the domain separators, forget, the absorption and output
    trees, the key shift chain, the nonce chain and the rate lanes' nonce registers,
    the selector at every lane, the start tree with its call-type field,
    the reset tree that every zeroization trigger drives, the replicated round and phase counters
    with the constant ROMs they index, the limit counters and the controller's counters, as FR-025
    "Zeroization" lists them (HW-089), the key register at each node that holds key bits, apart from
    the key shift chain, the one-block output buffer, the controller of the call-state table with
    its error-code register and the latched `selftest_fail` output, the self-test's parts (the
    selector at each chain's input and the one at the absorption interface, its r-bit
    comparator and the storage of its key, its nonce, the empty personalization
    string's length word, its reseed input with that input's length word and
    its expected output; HW-082; SEC-088; RAT-079), and every component the
    chosen candidate adds (RAT-070). Each names what it is for: the attack
    class it resists, the bound it supports or the functional need it serves, as the candidate's
    `mapping` log entry records it for the candidate's own components, which the list cites, and as
    FR-025 and FR-059 state it for the structures that every candidate shares, those of the mode and
    the core, which the list cites for them in place of the entry (RAT-073); the list names each
    component's group: the candidate's own, in its `mapping` entry with a place in the metaphor, are
    FR-030's five, the two lanes per node, the coupling function, the vein accumulator, the food
    nodes with their placement and the rotation amounts, with the colour schedule, the local linear
    layer between boxes, the round constants, its key path and any other it adds, the key path being
    FR-025's, the key register at each node that holds key bits and the key shift chain through
    those nodes, which come from the candidate's operation-list declarations; the shared structures,
    with their roles from FR-025 and FR-059 and no place in the metaphor, are the padding rule, the
    domain separators and forget, which the design document's chapter "Mode" states (RAT-086), the
    absorption and output trees, the start tree with its call-type field, whose registers a
    zeroization clears and whose latency is no greater than the reset tree's (FR-025
    "Zeroization"; HW-088), the reset tree, the replicated round and phase counters
    with the constant ROMs they index, whose contents are the candidate's
    round constants, the limit counters, the controller's counters (HW-089),
    the nonce chain and the rate lanes' nonce registers, the selector at
    every lane, the one-block output buffer, the controller of the call-state
    table with its error-code register and the `selftest_fail` latch, and the
    self-test's parts (RAT-078); a component with none is labelled "kept
    for the mapping only", with its cost stated, or is removed, and the mapping chapter restates
    only each component's place in the metaphor, stating once that the shared structures
    have none (FR-030; RAT-073). The hardware structures among them name
    their functional need, and the paths that carry secret data, every structure that holds one of
    FR-008's assets, also the attack class they meet: the key registers, which hold the key between
    instantiates, the key chain and the selectors that pass key bits, the selector at every lane and
    the one at the key chain's input (the key; RAT-079); the lanes and every pipeline register
    (the capacity part and the whole state within a request); the output tree's registers and the
    one-block output buffer (the state within a request and each output block before the caller
    takes it); and the limit counters (their integrity); each meets no read-back and zeroization
    (FR-025), the counters' integrity resting on FR-025's refusals at each limit, fault attacks on
    any of them staying with feature 008, and the self-test's selectors, the key chain's, the nonce
    chain's and the absorption interface's, also name the risk FR-008 states of each held on its
    constants, alone or with the others (SEC-082, SEC-088; RAT-076, RAT-079), while the
    self-test's stored key, nonce, length words, reseed input and expected
    output are development inputs of the frozen vectors and carry no secret;
    the rationale cites the plan's Complexity Tracking for each structure one of its rows covers,
    the key registers under the row on the key and nonce chains and the output buffer under the row
    on the clock, reset and start signal, and the design document's chapter "Hardware structure" for
    the rest (RAT-070). The chapter states once what the slime-mould mapping does and does not
    contribute, citing the mapping chapter (FR-030; RAT-084).
  - **Constants.** Every constant of Π and the mode has its rule and reason: round constants,
    rotation amounts, padding constants and domain separators, the last two in the design document's
    chapter "Mode" (RAT-086).
  - **Attack classes.** The rationale takes each attack class of security.md §12.1 in turn and
    names the design feature meant to resist it, or marks the class open for feature 005 with its
    reason, or, for a class or the part of one that another feature takes up, "deferred to feature
    <n>", citing the requirement that defers it and the risk it states: "Side-channel and fault"
    names FR-020's fixed operations as the feature that meets its timing part (T4, FR-008) and is
    marked "deferred to feature 008 (FR-008)" for power, electromagnetic and fault-injection attacks
    and the behaviour on reset and clock glitches, with FR-008's stated risk, and "open for feature
    005" stays for a class whose analysis feature 005 takes up (RAT-075). The chapter follows
    security.md §12.1 row by row, a row that joins classes being taken part by part, each part with
    an outcome and a mark as a class has (RAT-088): cube and zero-sum, which R_dist counts through
    every candidate's `degree` records (round rule), cite those records and are not marked open,
    while algebraic and integral attacks beyond them stay "open for feature 005"; the related-seed
    part of "Differential and related-seed" cites research R4's first-order related-key and
    related-nonce differences, its attacks beyond first order marked "deferred to feature
    005 (FR-049)", the requirement that defers them and keeps that analysis
    open in the final report; and "Linear and correlation" and "Invariant
    subspace and nonlinear invariant" state an outcome and a mark for each part.
    The chapter also states the known limitations, the classes left open or
    deferred among them, as security.md §8's last item asks (RAT-084). It
    names a resisting feature, never "open", for the classes this feature's
    evidence addresses: differential and linear (trail bounds), slide (round constants),
    invariant subspace (invariant checks, constants), rotational and rotational-XOR (symmetry
    checks, the rotational pairs of research R4), the inverse direction (R_b, Π⁻¹ in the bias
    rule) and the mode's generic attacks (the theorem). For every candidate (it matters most for the
    Boolean family) it cites the candidate's `degree` records, computed per candidate and claimable
    size in plan phase 5, before the round rule (research R13): the degree counts of r rounds of Π
    and Π⁻¹ from the operation list by the stated rule, w − 1 per addition, which the record marks
    as the rule's counts, not upper bounds, with an upper bound beside them, w for an addition of
    two variable words; the round counts whose count d is at most 126, for which a zero-sum over a
    cube of dimension d + 1 costs 2^(d + 1), less than 2¹²⁸; and the inside-out count, the largest
    r₁ + r₂ for which max(d_f(r₁), d_b(r₂)), the counts of r₁ rounds of Π and r₂ of Π⁻¹, is at most
    126, at a cost of 2^(max(d_f, d_b) + 1). Each such round count raises R_dist (round rule).
  - **Network questions.** The rationale cites the answers that the design
    document's chapter "Permutation" gives in its section "Network questions"
    (RAT-074): each of the eight questions is answered for the duplex as designed,
    and a question that does not fit gets the reason: for question 6, the output is the rate
    lanes themselves; for question 4, the cycle structure of Π takes the place of absorbing
    states, covered by the generic bound (research R13, choice R13.2).
  - **Alternatives.** A chapter gives the other family's representative and its score, every
    candidate not selected with its log entries and reason, and the records that decided the
    outcome, each cited by record ID or by path and SHA-256: `d4/choice.json`, `d4/tie.json` and
    `d4/tie-choice.json` where they exist, every selection record with its tie choice, each
    decline's log entry and, for each candidate not selected, the entry or record that decided it,
    so that every reason it gives traces to its record.
  - **Margin.** The chapter states the round-count rule, citing the protocol's "Round rule" (FR-032,
    FR-038), and the margin per claimable size, three numbers, R − R_dist, R − R_bound and
    R / max(R_f, R_b), with the term that set R, and the rationale explains R's growth with n
    (FR-028). It argues Δ = 2 against the single-trail limits of FR-038 as a pre-registered
    allowance, not a proof, and states the gap from clustering and hulls as a residual risk
    for feature 005 (FR-062).
- **FR-035**: A Python reference model MUST implement Π, Π⁻¹ and the mode with model ID
  `phyco-v3` and its variant IDs. It MUST be integer-only (Principle I), bit-exact and
  deterministic across platforms and supported Python versions (Principle V), and marked
  model-only, RTL-pending (Principle II). It MUST NOT import analysis-only dependencies, and it
  reads no clock, random source, environment variable or platform property: its import test
  bans `time`, `random`, `os`, `sys`, `platform` and `secrets`, as it bans numpy. The supported
  Python versions are 3.10 and 3.14, the two tested; 3.8, 3.9 and 3.11 to 3.13 are "expected,
  not tested", since the model uses only the standard library and passes the 3.8 grammar check.
  The analysis tools need Python 3.10 or later and are tested on 3.10.11 and 3.14.4. Every
  evaluator of an operation list MUST agree with the others before it counts: before any
  evidence run on a candidate, the scalar and the batch interpreters agree on reference outputs
  for the 16 Π inputs of research R8 at every variant the candidate uses, computed by the
  scalar interpreter and committed with the candidate; a C evaluator agrees on the same outputs
  before its first run; and after selection the model agrees with all of them on the committed
  vectors.
- **FR-036**: Golden vectors and known-answer tests MUST be committed for Π and Π⁻¹ at every
  supported size and every reduced variant used as evidence, and for the mode at every claimable
  size with at least two development keys and 10,000 words each (FUT-GRID-5), in generate requests
  that cycle through stated lengths, the last request taking what is left (research R8). Where the
  model's speed limits a size, the length and the measured speed MUST be stated. The vectors trace
  the state after every phase of every round for the first four inputs, so that an RTL mismatch can
  be located to one stage. Generated files hold only deterministic content, in a fully specified
  text format (data-model introduction: upper-case hexadecimal zero-padded to the word's width,
  lanes in the design document's order, a fixed header grammar, JSON with sorted keys and two-space
  indentation, a final newline, LF only), so that another implementation can write byte-identical
  files. The command, the interpreter, the model's SHA-256, the platform, the measured speed and the
  generation time go to a sidecar record per generator run, which the check of generated files
  does not compare; the measured speed is stated there. The check writes a record of its own on each
  platform, with each generated file's SHA-256 and the verdict `match` or `differs`, and each
  generator's pair of such records, one per platform, is the evidence of SC-009. The sidecar is
  frozen with the vectors, as the record of the runs that produced them, and after the freeze a
  generator runs only to check or to reproduce, and neither writes to it (data-model §11, §13).
  Every run started on a candidate or a variant writes a record, even if aborted (`inconclusive` or
  `blocked`, with the reason); a run later found hit by a tool defect keeps its record, with the
  defect noted. FUT-GRID-5's validation of an intermediate size before 16×16 is carried to features
  006 and 008: feature 006's RTL simulation runs 4×4 before 8×8 and 16×16, and feature 008's board
  runs 4×4 first, then each larger claimable size that fits the XC7A100T, in order, the largest that
  fits last (FUT-HW-2; FR-052, FR-056). FR-026's LUT and flip-flop estimate predicts the fit and
  Vivado's post-route report decides it; a size that does not fit is listed in feature 008's spec
  with its reason and stays covered by simulation. The generators run in a fixed order, each
  `--check` passing before the next: the constant tables; then the vectors, written through `pi` and
  `ResearchDuplex`, which runs no self-test, together with `phycoweb/selftest_v3.py`, the
  self-test's inputs and expected output copied from them; then `Generator`, which imports it and
  runs the self-test, in a named test of `tests/test_model_v3.py` that `gen_constructed_v3.py` and
  its `--check` run first on each platform, exiting 2 unless it passes and recording its result
  (data-model §11; REP-086); and only then the constructed-state vectors of FR-054, written by
  `gen_constructed_v3.py` through `pi`, `pi_inv` and `ResearchDuplex` only, its research path to
  chosen states included, so that every generator and module it reads has passed its check before it
  runs (FR-059; data-model §11; REP-077).

**Structural evidence and bounds**

- **FR-037**: The structural evidence MUST include:
  - Π⁻¹(Π(x)) = x and Π(Π⁻¹(x)) = x on fixed and development inputs at every supported size and
    variant: the 16 vector inputs and 2¹⁰ development test states of research R13, counts that
    may only rise;
  - exhaustive bijectivity on the smallest variant, 2×2 at 4-bit words (2³² states);
  - R_f and R_b from exact dependency matrices (FR-029);
  - the frequency of ones in every state bit after each round on development inputs, as for
    S14, and the avalanche of single-bit input differences per round (strategy note §5.4);
  - symmetry checks of every constant pattern (FR-023, FR-024), each pattern on its own: each
    round's constants, the placement of the food nodes and the placement of the K sets of rotation
    amounts, except that at 2×2 and 3×3 a placement is checked together with the round constants as
    one pattern (FR-018; research R13);
  - a check that no conserved sum or zero mode of `phyco-v2`'s transport (S13)
    survives in Π, at every supported size and every variant a candidate
    uses as evidence, after one round and after a full call, over the whole
    state and over each colour class of the size's schedule (research R13). A
    form that holds on more test states than its chance rate allows (2⁻ʷ for a w-bit sum or
    XOR), by a binomial test at the bias rule's level, 0.005 divided by the number of forms and
    round counts tested, fails the check as a probabilistic invariant and raises R_dist at that
    round count;
  - for every claimable size, R_dist, R_f, R_b and R_bound established. The frequency and
    avalanche measurements pass when they are conclusive (their controls did not fire and no
    tool defect is left unresolved), whatever R_dist they give, and inconclusive bias evidence
    at a size makes the candidate ineligible (research R4).
  - where the call types draw on distinct constant streams (FR-023), each item above whose result
    depends on the constants, for each call type's permutation, each record naming its call type,
    with R the largest over the call types; the trail bounds of FR-038 and the dependency matrices,
    which read no constant value, cover one permutation for all, each record stating that reason.
- **FR-038**: First differential and linear trail bounds MUST be computed on reduced rounds,
  either proven by the design strategy (for example by counting active boxes with exhaustively
  computed box bounds) or computed by a validated solver, for the reduced widths and for the
  production width; R_bound comes from the production width at each claimable size (FR-032).
  Each bound MUST state its rounds, width, size, kind, value, method, tool and validation
  record, and that it bounds single trails under the usual independence assumptions;
  clustering into differentials and linear hulls, and key-dependent effects, MUST be listed as
  open for feature 005 (strategy note §2.1). A solver-computed bound counts only when the
  solver proves it: an optimal or infeasible status with the proven dual bound used, or a
  search that exhausts its space within its cap. The best solution found is never a bound, and
  a search that times out gives no bound and is recorded as `inconclusive`. The solver options
  that decide a bound are fixed and recorded (a MIP gap of 0, the feasibility and integrality
  tolerances, the thread count and the 8 h limit), and a floating-point weight becomes an exact
  rational by rounding down to a multiple of 2⁻¹⁶, never up, which weakens the bound, while an
  active-box or active-S-box count, or any other objective that takes only whole values, is never
  read from a float: it stands where the status is infeasible with a limit of one less on that
  objective, or it is the sum of the counts of two shorter spans of rounds, and where a solution at
  that count holds when checked in whole numbers against the tables its model was built from; the
  record holds each status, that check and the dual bound, which no count reads, and a dual bound
  of such a model is never rounded up. The runs of one count stay within one 8 h limit. Every MILP
  bound record states that its status rests on the solver's built-in tolerances, and the design
  document and the final report say so beside R_bound. A bound that sets R_bound is confirmed where
  an exact method exists, by the exhaustive tables at w = 4 and 8; at 16 and 32, where no exact
  method exists without a further tool, and so at the production width R_bound comes from, it is
  reported as proven by the solver within its tolerances, a limitation each such record states.
  Before selection, every candidate's R_bound at each claimable size comes from a bound computed at
  that size (data-model §6), and the 48 h reservation keeps every such search; a proof in the design
  document that carries a bound to a larger size for every claimable n (for example that every
  r-round trail at n×n activates at least as many boxes as at 4×4), checked by computing it at 8×8,
  may be written for the selected candidate and serves only as a check beside the computed bounds,
  reported in the rationale. A candidate whose row cannot hold a 16×16 search has no R_bound there
  and is `ineligible`, as for any spent row. A trail is a characteristic of Π, its difference or
  mask after every round with its exact weight, exhibited by a validated tool that searches Π at the
  size and width the round rule uses; it is held in a `trail` record (data-model §6) and enters
  R_dist. A bound, a box's trail, an active-box or active-S-box count and a bound search's optimum
  are bounds, which enter R through R_bound, while an optimal characteristic that a search of Π
  returns with its weight counts as a trail.
- **FR-039**: Before any result of an analysis tool counts, positive or negative, the tool MUST
  re-find a known result of its kind (security.md §12.5): on `phyco-v2`, the FUT-SEC-8
  recovery, both merging mechanisms of S13, the vein bounds of S12 and S15, and the bias of S14,
  each re-found by the tool research R13 names for it, the FUT-SEC-8 recovery by `fut_sec8.py`,
  S13's mechanisms by the invariant checks and the others by the bias test; for trail search, a
  published optimal trail of a reference design. Every tool whose verdict
  counts has one: the symmetry check re-finds S7's rotation structure of `phyco-v2`'s constants and
  the planted torus symmetries of stated lists at n = 3 and 4, and clears a list with none; the
  dependency propagation re-finds the hand-derived matrices of a stated small operation list at 3×3
  and 4×4; exhaustive bijectivity re-finds a known permutation (2³² distinct outputs) and
  a stated map with collisions (fewer); the invariant checks re-find S13's two merging mechanisms on
  `phyco-v2`, the transport difference of S13(b) and the merged pair of S13(a), and
  the planted invariants of stated lists at 3×3 and 4×4, and clear a list
  with none; the avalanche test re-finds a planted result on each of its
  three paths, checked at every round count from 1 to its variant's R against that round count's
  expected set, the planted bits and every bit that the exact dependency matrices show independent
  of the flipped bit at that round count, each of which flips always or never: every bit of that set
  fires at every such round count, any other firing is reported with its round count, position and
  count and decides nothing below the variant's R, and at the variant's R, a round count fixed with
  the known result before the tool is drafted at which the exact dependency matrices show every bit
  outside the set dependent on the flipped bit, the result is re-found only if exactly that set
  fires, bit for bit, with the chance that a correct test fires there on a uniform bit, at most 2b
  times the threshold 0.005/(128·S_A) per flipped bit, a bit being counted twice (research R4),
  stated beside it: for the
  flip set a stated variant that leaves one lane unmixed, a lane that no
  update writes, which others may read, the difference bits of that lane being the planted bits; for
  the related-key and related-nonce set a stated instantiation variant in which a stated key bit and
  a stated nonce bit never reach a stated lane; and for the rotational pairs a stated operation list
  in which one stated lane commutes with word rotation, these two taking their sets from their own
  dependency matrices; and its validation record also shows, without new inputs, that its
  verdict function fires exactly on the counts at or beyond its two critical values and that its
  power at research R4's δ_A is at least 0.9; the cost model re-finds the hand-counted examples of
  research R5; and the degree derivation reproduces, on a stated small operation list at w = 4, the
  rule's count exactly, with the exact degree from the truth table reported beside it and the upper
  bound checked to be at least that degree, and a hand-derived inside-out count (research R13).
  The bias tool's validation also shows its detection power: on each of two families of 100 sets of
  synthetic binomial counts with a planted bias at the 90%-power values of research R4 (0.0030,
  0.0031 and 0.0033 at the three sizes), the first planting its bit as research R3's class 5 states
  and the second, a known result added, spreading the planted bits over all S tested bits, both sets
  and both directions, the rate at which its planted bit fires lies within a stated binomial
  interval around 90%, a set counting only when its planted bit fires, and every firing of an
  unplanted bit is reported with its set, position and count, and more than 3 of a family's 100 sets
  with such a firing make the validation `not re-found` (research R4). The
  synthetic sets are constructed inputs of research R3's class 5, fixed by a public
  rule before first use, hashed and never replaced: they stay local, regenerated by their
  deterministic command, and the validation record commits each set's SHA-256, the numpy version and
  a per-set summary, so that its verdict is recomputable from committed files; a later draw must
  reproduce every hash with that numpy version, a draw that differs counts for nothing, and should
  numpy's version ever differ from the one research R1 pins, which only an owner's own decision
  outside the protocol could bring (FR-044; PRO-078), the old record stays the validation in the old
  environment while a new validation, a new record, decides in the new one: outside the interval, or
  with more than 3 sets holding an unplanted firing, the tool is unvalidated there, its results
  `not evaluated`, until a defect is found and fixed (research R3, R4). Each known result
  this feature writes itself (the small operation lists with their hand-derived matrices, the lists
  with planted symmetries and the one with none, the map with collisions, the variant with a planted
  gap, the instantiation variant with a key bit and a nonce bit that never reach a
  lane, the operation list with a lane that commutes with word rotation,
  the lists with planted invariants and the one with none, research R5's
  hand-counted examples, the small operation list of known degree) is written
  with its SHA-256 and entered in the provenance log before the tool it validates is drafted; the
  validation record cites both entries, and a test checks their order and that the hashes still
  match (data-model §7, §14). Each known result of a tool at a validated width, the first and each
  further one, has a second known result of its own at that width, fixed before the tool's first
  validation, with its source, in the provenance log: a second published value of its kind at that
  width from research R19's approved papers, or else a self-written result of its kind at that
  width, written and logged before the tool is drafted. After a failed validation at any width, the
  re-validation re-finds every known result of the tool, the first and the second at each of its
  widths, so that no use at any width counts again on a fix tuned to one value; a tool with neither
  at one of its widths says so in its validation records, and after a failed
  validation it stays unvalidated, its results `not evaluated` (research R12;
  ANA-076). What must match is stated per known result (data-model §7): exact
  equality for every deterministic fact, and
  for a measured fraction a count in the binomial interval around the recorded fraction at the
  tool's sample size, with at most 0.005 in each tail; anything else is `not re-found`. Each known
  result is of the kind of its use in word width and kind of model, and the cross-checks cover each
  candidate's operation set, which no known result shares: for every r from 1 to
  the largest at which the exhaustive computation finishes within its 8 h cap, the ARX search's
  optimal single-trail weight over r rounds of each distinct ARX-box of each ARX candidate equals,
  at w = 4 (boxes of 8 bits), that of an exhaustive search over the box's internal trails, run by
  the exhaustive-tables tool, and lies, at w = 4 and 8 (boxes of 8 and 16 bits), at or above the
  best product of one-round entries of the box's exhaustive tables, a floor, since a table entry
  sums every trail through the box's internal additions, before its searches at w = 16 and 32 count;
  and the active-S-box counter's minimum over r rounds at 4×4 equals the exhaustive minimum from
  each Boolean candidate's nonlinear-layer tables at w = 4, whatever its form, over the same range
  of r. Each equality is exact, and a failure of any of these checks is a tool defect (research R7);
  they run per candidate and tool version, charged to that candidate's trail row, where they run
  first, on the first amounts of its recorded space in tie order, and again on the chosen amounts
  scaled to w = 4 and 8 before the production-width searches count (research R7, R12). A tool's
  validated kind states the word widths its known results cover, and a search's results count only
  at a width so covered: the ARX search's kind is w = 16, from SPECK32, and its searches at w = 32
  count only after it re-finds a known result at 32-bit words, fixed with its source before its
  first validation as a further known result, and otherwise its results at w = 32 are
  `not evaluated`; the cross-checks at w = 4 and 8 check each candidate's boxes and never widen a
  kind (research R12). Each other trail tool's kind, the active-S-box counter's from PRESENT, the
  long-trail counter's from SPARKLE and the exhaustive tables' from the PRESENT S-box, is stated in
  the same way, with each use this plan makes of it and the known result that covers it; a use that
  no known result covers gets a further one, fixed with its source before the tool's first
  validation, or writes `not evaluated` (research R12). A cross-check checks a tool on a candidate's
  own boxes and layers and is not a use: its values are compared, by exact equality and against the
  floor, never counted, and it writes no `not evaluated` and calls for no validation again; a use on
  a candidate's operation set counts once that candidate's cross-checks pass, in place of a
  validation again, which on the same known results could not cover it. A tool used outside its
  validated kind writes `not evaluated`. A tool is validated again after any change to its code (its
  SHA-256 differs), a dependency upgrade, or a use at a word width, operation set or kind of model
  outside its validated kind, except that at a candidate's operation set the cross-checks stand in
  for it; a use at a word width or kind of model outside the kind counts only once a further known
  result covering it, fixed with its source before the tool's first validation, is re-found, since a
  validation again on the known results the tool had never widens its kind; and each counted result
  cites the validation record of the tool's exact hash. The trail tools are a fixed set once
  the first trail search starts, named by research R12's rows (ARX search, active-S-box counting,
  long-trail active-box counting, exhaustive tables): a fixed or faster version of a named tool is
  the same tool and, re-validated, may serve searches not yet run, and a search that reached its cap
  is never run again, with another tool or another version, except as the one re-run after a
  recorded tool defect (research R7); a speed-up is not a defect. Each tool's version, licence and
  validation MUST be recorded at its first use; a self-written tool's version is the SHA-256 of its
  code. A positive result is never dropped for want of validation: a trail or distinguisher from a
  tool not yet validated is recorded as an unverified hypothesis (FR-060) and blocks the candidate's
  eligibility until a validated tool, or a direct evaluation of the trail with the validated exact
  models, settles it; if confirmed, it raises R_dist. A possible weakness can therefore only raise R
  or block, never vanish. A tool whose validation target's value cannot be obtained stays
  unvalidated, and its results are `not evaluated` (edge cases). Validation and re-validation runs,
  on published values or on any other known result, charge no candidate: they have their own cap of
  8 h per instance, and a heavy one projects first against research R7's feature-wide row and is
  refused, exit 2, once it does not fit; the D4 reduced-width searches count against their
  candidate's trail budget, and the FUT-SEC-8 vector check and sweep, one pass per target, against
  the demonstration's own budget, which holds nothing else, while a repeat, its vector check
  included, is charged to research R7's feature-wide row and runs only on the owner's request
  (Pre-registered Design Protocol).

**Protocol**

- **FR-040**: Only development keys, nonces and inputs, derived from public strings, or from
  fixed constants and development inputs, by a rule fixed in the plan, MAY be used; the closed
  class of constructed inputs (research R3; data-model §2) is the only use of the second
  kind, its class 5, the bias tool's synthetic counts in both families of its
  validation (research R4), drawn from seeds that are themselves derived from
  public strings, and no value or index is picked after any output is seen. Scripts take these
  inputs only through the loader of research R3, which checks each kind's fingerprint, the
  SHA-256 of a text that data-model §2 states exactly, refuses, exit 2, any
  (n, w), n or kind without one, the allowed pairs being FR-018's sizes with
  research R14's widths, and returns only the use's declared index range. This
  holds for the whole feature, the freeze and everything after it included: no held-out key set MAY
  be created or used in this feature at any time. Every tool of this feature that reads feature
  002's or 003's seed sets loads only feature 002's `dev` set and exits 2 on any held-out set, as
  security.md §11.3 requires of development tools, and a test checks every such call. No statistical
  battery (SP 800-22, PractRand, TestU01 or any other) runs on a candidate or on `phyco-v3` in this
  feature: the frequency and avalanche tests of the bias rule are the only statistics, and the
  statistical programme belongs to feature 007 under its own protocol. Feature 007 defines the
  held-out sets, and this requirement MUST be carried to it in a checkable form: the held-out
  keys are derived from data that does not exist until the later of `phyco-v3`'s design freeze
  and feature 007's protocol freeze, for example SHA-256 over feature 007's protocol freeze
  record with a public string; feature 007 states the derivation, and a test re-derives it.
  Varying that record to steer the keys would change the frozen protocol, visibly in its
  history. The requirement is carried in the final report's open items and in the text of
  security.md §22 row 5a.4 (FR-052).
- **FR-041**: Every candidate design MUST be logged in an append-only log with its ID,
  parameters, evidence, decision and reason, and no entry MAY be deleted (Principle V). A
  design's `proposed` entry, with its operation list's SHA-256, comes before any tool runs on
  it, and every tool refuses an operation list whose hash has no `proposed` or `correction`
  entry (exit 2). Every variant ID used, every round count measured and every fix to an
  operation list is logged under its candidate, and the log cites every record of every run,
  used or not. Before an operation list changes, its version is copied unchanged to
  `research/004/candidates/<id>/superseded/<sha256>.py`, and a committed inventory lists every
  hash and path, so that every `oplist_sha256` in the log stays checkable; the re-check
  verifies the inventory. More than three candidates of a family MUST NOT be evaluated (stop
  rule); a candidate counts as evaluated from its first entry, `proposed`, whatever happens
  after, and a fourth `proposed` entry of a family is refused. A candidate is `rejected` or
  `ineligible` only for the rules' reasons (a failed inverse or bijectivity test, a failed
  eligibility item, a spent budget); it may be withdrawn before its first tool run for any
  recorded reason and still counts, and the log writer refuses any other decision. A raised R is
  never by itself a reason to drop a candidate: the measurements it needs run in their budget rows,
  and a row that cannot hold them makes the candidate `ineligible` for a spent budget (edge cases).
  Candidates are entered before any is scored, and a further one only in place of one `rejected` or
  `ineligible`, within the stop rule, never because another's R is high; the set closes at a
  `set-closed` log entry before selection, and a decline at the owner design review reopens it once,
  for one further candidate that the rules can select (section "Pre-registered Design Protocol"):
  after a `set-closed` entry `candidate_log.py propose` exits 2 unless the
  latest `review-outcome` entry is `declined`, with no later `set-closed`
  entry, and the candidate is that decline's further candidate or one of its
  replacements, so that the set reopens once (ANA-083). The candidate frozen MUST be
  the one the pre-registered rules select; the owner design review cannot
  put another eligible candidate in its place, and throughput is never a reason to decline. After a
  decline that ends in a further candidate, the rules select again over the whole set, the declined
  candidate included, and if they select it again the outcome is a stop (FR-043). A stricter change
  of the protocol applies to every candidate, those already
  evaluated included: each one's verdicts are recomputed under the new rule from saved outputs
  where possible (FR-044). The final report MUST state the selection bias of choosing among
  candidates on development inputs (security.md §11.6): the candidates logged and evaluated per
  family, the evaluations (measurements and searches) behind the frozen one, the bias rule's
  family-wise chance rates over them, each counting every pair of a claimable size and a round count
  tested by the freeze, each size's own R and every rise included, and every avalanche run by the
  freeze, with m/64 at a size where it measured m round counts above 128 (FR-028;
  ANA-075, ANA-079), and that every result of the frozen candidate is on
  development inputs chosen among up to six candidates, so that feature 007's held-out
  evaluation is the unbiased test. The rationale's chapter on alternatives cites the log entries
  (FR-034). Each kind of entry has one writer, which the agent runs: `evidence_v3.py` appends the
  `evidence` entries; `d4_choice.py` one `scored` entry per scored candidate when it writes
  `choice.json`, citing it by path and SHA-256; `round_rule.py --decide` the `selected` and `not
  selected` decisions after each selection record, a later record getting entries of its own, so
  that no entry is edited and the latest `selected` entry is the one in force (FR-043);
  `round_rule.py --close` the `set-closed` entry; `repair_log.py` the `repair` entry; and
  `scripts/candidate_log.py`, through `protocol/candidates.py`, every other entry: `proposed`,
  `mapping`, one `entered-d4` entry per candidate of the entered set before any score, the other
  decisions, a `rejected` or `ineligible` one with the `replacement` entry it brings, `correction`,
  a `replacement` entry that closes a pending one with its reason, and the `review-outcome` entry
  that records each outcome of the owner design review (ANA-083). Every refusal of the log writer is
  the command's, exit 2, appending nothing, `candidate_log.py enter` among them once a
  `scored` entry exists, and a `proposed` entry that records the owner's approval of a replacement,
  or of a further candidate after a decline, is written only on the owner's explicit word, quoted
  with its date in it, as is a `replacement` entry that closes a pending one with the reason "the
  owner declined the proposed design", after which the agent proposes no other design for that entry
  (cli contract; RAT-077; ANA-080, ANA-081; ANA-087).
- **FR-042**: `phyco-v2` MUST be archived as byte-exact copies in `baseline/phyco-v2/`, with every
  file's SHA-256 and source in a committed manifest (Principle V): at least the model, the C
  stream, `design.md` 1.0 and the golden vectors. The live files MUST stay unchanged.
- **FR-043**: The design freeze MUST come after the owner design review (⛔ gate), which starts only
  once the security-target review reads Approved (FR-012), and after the external design review is
  recorded as done or not arranged (D9); the external review is never cited as an evaluation
  (section "Context"). A row reading done names the review package's manifest by SHA-256, with the
  design-document version and the selection record it carried, and `design_freeze.py create` exits 2
  while a done row names no manifest, or one that neither `review-package/MANIFEST.json` nor its
  `versions/` holds; where the design document or `rationale.md` changed after that review, the row
  stays done for the version it names, the freeze record and the final report state that the
  external review read that earlier version, list each version 0.<k> since, with the files each
  changed, the design document, `rationale.md` or both, so that a change to `rationale.md` after
  that review is shown (RAT-087), and cite the review for that version only (SC-013), and the owner
  may have the changes reviewed in a row of its own, "External design review
  <k>" (SEC-077). Each row of the external review's family names the selection record it applies to,
  a done row through its manifest, which carries that record, and a "not arranged" row by naming the
  latest selection record when it is written; `create` reads the highest-k row that names the latest
  selection record and exits 2 while none does, so that a new selection record needs a row of its
  own, done or "not arranged", for the candidate it selects, and a review done for another candidate
  stays done in its row and is cited, in the freeze record's "External review" and in the final
  report, for the candidate and the version it read only (SC-013; SEC-078). A
  decline at the owner design review states its reasons and ends in one of: a
  revision of the design document or rationale (same candidate, new version, logged
  under the candidate with its old and new version, review repeated); a fix
  that leaves the design unchanged, logged under the candidate; a further candidate within the stop
  rule, only one the rules can select (from the chosen family, or from the other only where the
  declined candidate was selected by the fallback or D4 was not computable, while its family has
  fewer than three evaluated candidates, the decline's log entry first stating what the rules need
  to select it; section "Pre-registered Design Protocol"): the declined candidate stays eligible
  and in the set, the further one is entered and evaluated like any other,
  and `round_rule.py --select` runs again over the whole set; if the rules
  select the further candidate it goes to the review, and if they select the declined one again the
  outcome is a stop, logged with the review's reasons, since the declined candidate is never brought
  back to the review unchanged and a revision of its document, the first outcome, stays possible
  instead; or a stop. Each is logged with its reasons in a `review-outcome` entry of the candidate
  log, holding the owner design review's row with its highest k, the outcome, `declined`, `revised`,
  `fixed` or `stopped`, its reasons, for a decline what the rules need to select a further
  candidate, and for a revision or a fix the candidate's old and new versions, which
  `candidate_log.py outcome` writes after the commit that records the row's decision,
  reading the row at HEAD and exiting 2 on an outcome the row does not record
  (data-model §3; ANA-083), and where no further candidate can be entered the decline
  ends in one of the others. Any change after the owner design review (R
  raised, a new finding, a corrected document) makes a new design-document version: the rationale is
  revised, the review is repeated for the changed parts and recorded in a row of its own, "Owner
  design review <k>" (the notes under the Review & Approval table), and the review package is
  rebuilt, its earlier manifest kept as `review-package/versions/<sha256>.json` (data-model
  introduction). The owner design review's row, and each "Owner design review <k>", names
  `rationale.md` by path and SHA-256 beside the design document's version and the selection record,
  and `design_freeze.py create` compares it with the file at HEAD and exits 2 while they differ, so
  that a change to `rationale.md` after the review's approval counts only once an "Owner design
  review <k>" naming the changed file reads Approved, as a change to the design document does
  (RAT-083). A change before the freeze that touches any candidate's eligibility or
  R at 4×4 after a selection record exists makes `round_rule.py --select` run again over the whole
  set and write the next record (section "Pre-registered Design Protocol", "Eligibility and
  selection"): if it selects another candidate, the security-target review and every later review
  are held again for that candidate, each in a numbered row of its own; if it selects the same one,
  the reviews held stand for what did not change, and the changed parts are reviewed again as above.
  A rise of the selected candidate's R at 8×8 or 16×16 after a selection record exists, one that
  leaves every candidate's eligibility and R at 4×4 as they were, also makes `round_rule.py
  --select` run again over the whole set and write the next record, citing the
  previous one, whose `selected` entry's `variant_map` carries the final R
  at every claimable size, no entry being edited (data-model §9; ANA-085).
  After each selection record, `round_rule.py --decide` appends the candidate log's `selected`
  entry, with its `variant_map`, and a `not selected` entry for each other candidate the record
  lists as eligible, each citing the record with its tie choice where there is one (FR-041); the
  latest `selected` entry is the one in force, and `design_freeze.py create` exits 2 unless it cites
  the latest selection record, with that record's tie choice where there is one, and
  names the candidate that record selects (data-model §3, §9, §13; ANA-081),
  and unless every variant ID of its `variant_map` carries the final R that
  the candidate's `round-rule.json` gives at its size (ANA-085). The freeze
  runs in this order: (1) the owner commits the feature files (Principle
  IX): from the first commit that records an owner design review's approval to the feature's end,
  plan phase 9 included, the agent commits no file of this feature and leaves its
  changes in the working tree (SEC-084), and step (1) is the owner's next
  commit after the one that records the owner design review's approval, in its
  row with the highest k, of every feature file changed since, or an empty
  commit of the owner's where none changed, so that the external review's row, where it is set after
  that approval, is set in that commit (PRO-070); where the owner must commit a row on its own
  between that approval and step (1), "Environment record <n>" after a Windows or WSL update or "Log
  repair <n>", the owner design review is held again in "Owner design review <k>", naming the
  same versions where nothing else changed, and step (1) is the owner's next
  commit after the commit that records that row's approval, so that `create`'s
  check stands as written (PRO-072); (2) `design_freeze.py create` runs with
  `git_dirty` false, records as `owner_commit` the commit that follows the one recording that
  approval, step (1)'s, exiting 2 unless it is HEAD, and also while `check_claims.py` reports
  a finding at HEAD (SEC-084), and writes the record with `freeze_status` `draft`, its common
  `status` computed by code as every record's is, `pass` once each of `create`'s checks holds
  (data-model §1, §13; REP-093); (3) the owner's approval is written into it by `design_freeze.py
  approve`, run by the owner, which changes `freeze_status` to `approved` and adds who, when and the
  wording, and nothing else, the record keeping the `record_id` that `create` gave it (⛔ gate;
  REP-092); (4) the owner commits the record; (5) `design_freeze.py validate` exits 0,
  having checked against the draft's SHA-256, `draft_sha256`, taken without that field and the
  approval fields, `freeze_status`, `approved_by`, `approved_utc`, `approval_wording` and
  `approval_confirmed` (data-model §13; REP-084, REP-093), that nothing but
  the approval changed. The window of step (1) runs to the feature's end, plan phase 9 included, as
  the Assumptions' "from the owner design review's approval on" reads, and ends with the owner's
  commit that records the decision of the Review & Approval table's last row, "Final result review",
  a stop that the final report recommends (FR-052) ending it at the same commit, the standing
  authorization covering the files of this feature again from the next commit, for the features that
  follow (PRO-080); until then each later commit of a file of this feature is the owner's, a later
  row "Owner design review <k>" and the change it reviews included (PRO-072): a
  file of this feature is every file under the paths that the plan's project structure
  lists and every other file that this feature's requirements change, security.md, README,
  `.gitignore`, `.gitattributes` and `requirements.txt` among them, so that `protocol/claims.json`
  and security.md are files of this feature; the agent writes the owner design review's review
  entries with `provenance.py review`, on the owner's word, right after the commit that records that
  review's approval, before the owner sets any row in the working tree for step (1), the external
  review's row among them, leaving them in the working tree, since `provenance.py review` exits 2
  while the working tree's table differs from HEAD's, and the owner sets step (1)'s rows only once
  those entries stand, so that the owner's commit of step (1) holds both
  (PRO-075) and the frozen provenance log carries the status FR-034 keeps there,
  and `design_freeze.py create` reads the provenance log at HEAD and exits 2 while an AI-drafted
  section of the design document or of `rationale.md` has a latest entry that is not a review entry
  reading `accepted`, or one that names a row other than its review's with the highest k, the
  security-target review's for the design document's chapters "Security target" and "Mode" and the
  owner design review's for every other section, `rationale.md`'s chapters of those names among them
  (FR-034, FR-064; RAT-082; RAT-086); inside the window the agent writes
  each change of `phyco-v3`'s entry and of its line in security.md §15 that SEC-076 and
  SEC-079 name in the working tree, once the owner has set the row or made the
  change there and before the owner's commit, which holds it, so that at every
  commit of the window the register gives the rows' level (FR-012; SEC-084).
  `design_freeze.py create` exits 2 unless every theorem the mode chapter uses is `verified`
  in `limits.json`, with each precondition marked met beside the section
  that meets it, one of the mode chapter's sections in the version of the
  design document it reads, naming the theorem otherwise, and reads those
  marks as `unverified` while the security-target review row does not read Approved (FR-012;
  research R10); a theorem whose paper cannot be obtained or read is replaced by another of research
  R10's list, in a new version reviewed again, or the feature stops (Edge Cases).
  Steps (1), (3) and (4) are the owner's own acts: like a constitution amendment, they fall outside
  the standing authorization for local commits (constitution 1.3.0, Principle IX), and the agent
  performs them only on the owner's written approval (FR-044). The tie choices are owner's own acts of the
  same kind: running `d4_choice.py --choose` or `round_rule.py --choose`, each of which prints the
  tie record's SHA-256 and refuses unless standard input is an interactive terminal and the owner
  types its first 16 hex digits, kept in the choice record as `choice_confirmed` (data-model §8,
  §9), and setting the "D4 tie" and "Selection tie" rows of Review & Approval; the owner's review at
  each ⛔ gate lists them. `design_freeze.py approve` prints `draft_sha256` and refuses unless
  standard input is an interactive terminal and the owner types its first 16 hex digits, and the
  record keeps that confirmation (data-model §13). Dirty means a tracked file modified or an
  untracked file not ignored; ignored files (the locks, the local-only outputs under `data/local/`)
  do not count, and local-only outputs are in the record by SHA-256 anyway. The freeze record MUST
  hold the SHA-256 of the design document, with its version named 1.0 and `reviewed_version`, the
  header's 0.<k> that the owner design review read and the record hashes, the
  files keeping that line, so that nothing changes at or after the freeze
  (FR-034; RAT-080), `rationale.md`, the model, the vectors (constructed-state
  vectors included), the scripts that generate the constants and the vectors, `gen_constants_v3.py`,
  `gen_vectors_v3.py` and `gen_constructed_v3.py` (REP-077), the selected operation list,
  `research/004/ir.py` with its tools, and the scripts that compute verdicts
  (`evidence_v3.py`, `round_rule.py`, `d4_choice.py`, `mode_limits_v3.py`,
  `validate_tool.py`, `fut_sec8.py`, `recheck_004.py`, `protocol/devinputs_v3.py`,
  `protocol/evidence.py`), the freeze's code `protocol/design_freeze.py` and its command
  `scripts/design_freeze.py`; every module of the repository that a frozen script imports, directly
  or through another module, `protocol/candidates.py` among them and, where a frozen script imports
  them, the log writers `protocol/changelog.py` and `protocol/provenance.py`, which
  `design_freeze.py create` lists from the scripts' imports, so that a change after the freeze to a
  module on the path of a verdict fails the freeze's validation and the re-check runs only code
  whose hashes match the freeze (REP-091); the security target's files `parameters.json`
  and `limits.json`, with each theorem's status; R with every input of the round rule and every
  evidence record behind R, by ID and by SHA-256; the last `seq` and line SHA-256 of the candidate
  log, the provenance log and the protocol change log; the tool versions; `git_head` and
  `git_dirty`; the external design review's row, with the manifest's SHA-256 and
  the design-document version it names where the review is done; and the
  owner's approval. After the freeze, the frozen files MUST NOT change: a fix
  to a verdict script is a new file, so that the re-check (FR-065) runs the code that produced the
  verdicts, and the frozen model and vectors stay the expected values (FR-056) even when they
  disagree with the frozen design document (edge cases; FR-028). `protocol/evidence.py` refuses,
  exit 2, to write over a fixed-name file that the freeze record pins, `parameters.json`,
  `limits.json` and the selected candidate's `round-rule.json` among them, as the generators refuse,
  and after the freeze `mode_limits_v3.py` runs only with `--reproduce`, whose record goes under
  `$REP/reproductions/`.
- **FR-044**: After spec approval the pre-registered design protocol MAY only become stricter,
  and every change MUST be recorded with its reason before any result it affects, with the
  owner's recorded approval: the agent may propose a stricter change, and it carries one out only on
  the owner's written approval, as below. Settling a
  case the protocol leaves undefined is a change of the same kind: allowed only before any
  result it affects exists, with the owner's approval, recorded in the change log, and never
  looser than any reading of the existing text. The plan's rules for an exact D4 tie, for no
  scored family and for a representative found ineligible first were settled that way, at plan
  approval. Every change is recorded in the protocol change log,
  `research/004/protocol-changes.jsonl`, chained and locked like the candidate log and written only
  through `protocol/changelog.py`, by `scripts/protocol_change.py` (cli contract) and, for the one
  append to a broken chain, by `scripts/repair_log.py`, whose `repair` entry changes no
  rule, holds the repair's fields in place of the rule's and goes through
  the same approval step, while its row "Log repair <n>" reads Approved at
  HEAD (research R16; data-model §18; PRO-076), with per entry the date,
  the rule, the old and new text, the reason, the direction (below), the readings compared for a
  settled case, a reading that the new text sets aside, admitting what that reading refuses, marked
  `set_aside` with the case under which the old text fails and the owner's decision on it
  (PRO-079), the approver, the last record ID that existed before it, so that
  "before any result it affects" can be checked, and the results known at the time of the change;
  the writer refuses, exit 2, an entry that changes a rule without its direction, a
  settled one without its readings, any entry without the last record ID
  or the results known, and a `repair` entry without its fields (PRO-076).
  Its first entries, written when it is created, are the answered rows of every checklist round
  answered before then, by the per-round lists "Rows that change the protocol" of
  checklist-choices.md, in round order, each holding, per rule its row is listed under, that rule's
  text before and after its round was applied (data-model §18). A row is listed under every rule
  whose text its Changes cell names or its applied change altered. A change made in the texts of a
  rule's places alone is a change of that rule: the places of "Records and their hashes" are
  research R1 and R16, data-model's introduction and §1, §13, §14, §16 and §17, the cli contract and
  the notes under the Review & Approval table, and those of "Protocol changes and their directions"
  this requirement, data-model §18 and the cli contract's `protocol_change.py`, as the directions
  below name them; the comparison below reads them over every pair and span, each rule's `old_text`
  and `new_text` taken from them, and each round's list names under those headings
  every row of its round whose Changes cell names one of their places or
  whose applied change altered one (PRO-067). Each list names its round's two
  commits by hash, the commit before the round's first applied row and the commit that completed its
  alignment, so that a round's alignment commits fall inside its pair, the first two rounds' pairs
  being the commits that hold their rows with their other edits, named as such. Before the tasks
  approval the agent makes the comparison with `research/004/place_check.py`, committed for it,
  standard library only, which reads the named commits with `git show` and writes
  `research/004/place-check.json`: per round, its list's heading, its two commits and each place
  whose text changed between them, with the rows of that round listed under its rule; per span
  outside the pairs, from the plan-approval commit on, each commit that changed a place, with the
  place; each place changed by an edit that no row made, with its commit; and `compared_to`, the
  commit it compared up to. A place's text, as committed with CR LF read as LF, runs for an FR item
  from its line `- **FR-0xx**` to the next line that starts an FR item, a bold group name or a
  heading; for a research section from its heading `## R<k>.` to the next heading of that level; for
  a paragraph of the protocol from its bold name to the next bold name or to the section's end; for
  the plan's table from its heading to the next heading; for a numbered section of the data model,
  or a script's section of the cli contract, from its heading to the next heading of the same or a
  higher level; for the data model's introduction from the file's start to its first numbered
  section; for the cli contract, the whole file; and for the notes under the Review & Approval table
  from the line after the table to the next heading; in the data model, the cli contract and those
  notes, a line in fenced code is never a heading (PRO-067). A round with no alignment commit takes
  as its second commit the commit of its last applied row, named as such. The results beside each
  list restate that file. A place changed by an edit that no row made, in a round's commits or
  between two rounds, is recorded beside a list with its commit, for the owner to confirm at the
  tasks approval as an entry of its own or as no change of rule, with the reason, in a Review &
  Approval row of its own, "Place check", which names the file's SHA-256 and each such place with
  its confirmation, a row the owner sets and commits (PRO-063): an edit inside a round's pair is
  recorded beside that round's list, and its entry follows the round's row entries; an edit between
  two rounds is recorded beside the list of the round before it, as ad2c195's is beside the
  re-run's, and its entry follows that round's entries, so that the log keeps the order of the
  commits. Such an entry holds `rules`, one object per rule whose place the edit changed, its
  `old_text` and `new_text` at the edit's parent and at its commit, each named by hash, with
  `direction` and `readings` per rule; `row` none and `granularity` `commit`, with `commit`, the
  edit's hash; `reason`, the owner's confirmation with its reason, naming the commit; and
  `place_check`, the comparison of the pair or span that holds the commit (PRO-064).
  `scripts/protocol_change.py check`, which writes nothing, makes the same comparison, and a test
  checks that it gives that file's result over its commits. Before the log is created its writer,
  `create`, reads the row "Place check" at HEAD and exits 2, writing nothing, while a place whose
  text changed has no row of that round listed under its rule and no such confirmation, while its
  result up to `compared_to` differs from the file the row names, or while a place changed after
  `compared_to`, until the comparison is made again over the later commits and its file confirmed in
  "Place check <k>", read with the highest k; the comparison's result is recorded with the first
  entries (PRO-063). `create` also exits 2, writing nothing, while a first entry holds a reading
  marked `set_aside` that the owner has not decided, entry by entry, in the Review & Approval row
  "Readings set aside", set at the tasks approval and read at HEAD: the change kept, with the
  owner's reason, as a change these words do not allow, or the old text restored; it records each
  decision in its reading, a later entry holding such a reading shows it at the approval step, which
  records the owner's decision and reason, and the guard "Protocol log" exits 2 while such a reading
  holds no decision (data-model §18; PRO-079). The owner's answer to a round approves its list.
  The log is created, and no evidence run starts, only after the owner has
  confirmed every list, and a row the owner takes out of a list is recorded beside it with the
  owner's reason; the shared guard "Protocol log" enforces it: `protocol/evidence.py` exits 2,
  writing nothing, while the log does not exist, its chain does not check, or its first entries, one
  per listed row of every round answered before it was created and one per place
  the owner confirmed as an entry of its own, do not all hold the owner's `approval_confirmed`, or
  while any entry's `approval_confirmed`, the first entries' and each later one's, the last line's
  included, which the chain does not protect, differs from the first 16 hex digits of the approval
  hash that the guard recomputes (data-model §18; PRO-077), so that every
  record cites the log's last entry (cli contract; data-model §1), with two
  exceptions, the two kinds that `protocol/evidence.py` writes while the
  log does not exist, each with `protocol_seq` and `protocol_line_sha256` `none`: a `lock-cleared`
  record, since it records an event and clearing a stale lock is what lets the log be created, and
  an environment record written by `owner_record.py environment`, which records the platform and
  holds no result, so that each platform's first record comes before the log, in plan phase 1's
  order, and the log's writer runs under it; every other kind stays refused, and once the log exists
  every `lock-cleared` record and every environment record, after a Windows or WSL update among
  others, cites its last entry as every record does, `tests/test_protocol_004.py` covering both
  kinds and the refusal of every other (PRO-071). Before the log exists only
  `data/log-append.lock` can be held, since every run that takes the heavy-run lock writes evidence,
  and after the approval step `create` writes the first entries to a temporary file beside the log's
  path and renames it to that path once all are written, so that a writer that
  dies leaves no partial log, only the lock, which `scripts/clear_lock.py`
  removes (PRO-065). A round answered after the log exists adds entries the
  usual way, proposed by the agent and approved by the owner. Approving a
  change is the owner's own decision, outside the standing authorization like the freeze's steps (1),
  (3) and (4), the tie choices, the environment records and the values the owner provides, each
  written by `scripts/owner_record.py`, with a Review & Approval row of its own
  (research R1, R19), and every Review & Approval decision that opens a guard (FR-043; section
  "Review & Approval"). The agent never makes that decision, and never takes it from a general
  request, a question, a notification or its own recommendation: only the owner's written approval,
  given before the act and naming what it approves, decides it. After that approval the agent
  may carry out the act's steps for the owner, running its script, entering its hex digits and
  writing its row, as the owner instructed on 2026-11-05, and what the act writes says so: a row in
  its notes, a change-log entry and a record of `scripts/owner_record.py` in `written_approval`, the
  owner's words with their date and `entered_by` `agent` (data-model §18). Each approval step prints
  the SHA-256 of what is approved and refuses unless standard input is an interactive terminal and
  the owner types its first 16 hex digits, or what it approves holds `written_approval`, where the
  writer takes those digits itself, and the entry records that confirmation (data-model §18). Each
  push, tag, release and publication still needs the owner's word for that action, and each download
  the owner's permission for that file. Every commit carries the owner's git
  identity and no AI co-author line, so the history cannot show who acted, and the owner's review at
  each ⛔ gate lists these acts since the last gate. A stricter change applies to every candidate,
  those already evaluated included. It is applied first by recomputing verdicts from saved outputs;
  a new measurement it needs, such as more inputs, is a new measurement of the same kind under the
  changed rule, not a re-run: the old records stay, and the new hours count against the same budget
  row. Every firing already recorded stands, so a stricter change can only raise R or make a
  candidate ineligible, never undo a raise; before the freeze, one that touches any candidate's
  eligibility or R at 4×4 after a selection record exists makes selection run again
  over the whole set (section "Pre-registered Design Protocol", "Eligibility
  and selection"). Every record cites the change log's last entry when its
  run started, and the re-check recomputes each verdict under the rules in force at that entry and,
  where a later stricter change applies, also under the current rules, listing both, and applies
  only entries whose `approval_confirmed` matches the first 16 hex digits
  of their recomputed approval hash (FR-065; data-model §1; PRO-077). If
  the row cannot hold the new run, that evidence is inconclusive and the
  candidate ineligible at that size, as for any spent budget; a budget lowered below the hours
  already used leaves zero hours, results so far stand, and no further run of that row starts. After
  a stop, a later feature may evaluate further candidates only under its own pre-registered protocol
  and new model IDs, and it counts this feature's candidates, results and stop in its selection bias
  (FR-041). "Stricter" has one direction per rule, the one that makes the conclusion more
  conservative, and each rule of section "Pre-registered Design Protocol" repeats its own. Each
  heading of checklist-choices.md's lists "Rows that change the protocol" takes its direction from
  this list, and each listed row's rules take `stricter` in these directions or `settled`, with its
  readings (data-model §18; PRO-066):
  - eligibility: items only added; selection and the nonlinearity choice rule (D4): no change;
  - tool validation: known results and re-validation triggers only added;
  - the FUT-SEC-8 demonstration: no change;
  - the round rule: Δ only rises, and sources of R_dist are only added;
  - the bias rule: only a change that can add firings and never remove one. Every firing
    recorded under the rule then in force stands, and a larger N, N_a or F, or a new set, is
    tested under its new S or S_A in addition, so R can only rise;
