  - the stop rule: budgets and the candidate limit only fall;
  - the constant rule (FR-023): fixed, with its refused list, before the first candidate is scored,
    no change after; the refused list only grows, each addition proposed by the agent and approved
    by the owner as a stricter change with the list's new SHA-256, and a later addition that matches
    makes each candidate it touches fail FR-023;
  - the security-target parameters: s, the key and nonce lengths and c only rise; ε only falls
    and N only rises; u, every output limit and the per-request cap only fall;
  - the values the plan fixes: as the plan's table "Values this plan fixes" states for each;
  - the development inputs (research R3): no change;
  - the structural checks (research R13): counts only rise, and symmetries, constant relations,
    forms and sources of R_dist are only added;
  - the vector lengths (research R8): lengths only grow;
  - records and their hashes (research R1, R16; data-model's introduction and §1,
    §13, §14, §16 and §17; the cli contract; the notes under the Review &
    Approval table): what a record holds, which files are hashed and kept,
    and the writers' and guards' checks and refusals are only added, and no file's hash kind changes
    once a record holds it, nor any version that research R1 pins, its pinned versions being of this
    heading's text (PRO-078);
  - protocol changes and their directions (this requirement; data-model §18; the cli
    contract's `protocol_change.py`): an entry's fields, the comparison and
    the guard's refusals are only added, and no rule's direction is loosened.
- **FR-045**: Heavy runs MUST NOT overlap one another or any battery run (security.md §12.5). A
  heavy run is any run that is of a kind research R7 budgets, uses more than one worker process or
  thread, or is projected to take, or last took, more than 10 minutes; the FUT-SEC-8 sweep,
  exhaustive bijectivity and solver runs are examples. Every other kind is light, as research R7
  lists them, and FR-053's test with two worker counts is a heavy run that charges no candidate.
  Every heavy run that charges no candidate, the FUT-SEC-8 row's one pass per target with its vector
  check excepted, is charged to research R7's feature-wide 66 h row and refused, exit 2, once its
  projection does not fit the hours left there, and a heavy reproduction, like a FUT-SEC-8 repeat,
  runs only on the owner's request, quoted with its date in its record.
  Every heavy run takes the heavy-run lock,
  projects its hours first and records `heavy_run` true, and a test checks that every budgeted kind
  and every script with a worker option takes the lock. A stale lock, left by a crash or a reboot,
  is removed only by `scripts/clear_lock.py`, once its holder is shown gone, and the removal is
  recorded (cli contract).
- **FR-046**: Every download MUST have the owner's permission for that file (name, source,
  size), and its licence MUST allow use in a GPL-3.0 project. Downloaded files MUST be treated as
  untrusted. Code reused from a library, or generated code that closely matches published code,
  MUST have that code's licence checked against GPL-3.0 and recorded (vhdl-genai.md R9). A tool's
  second known result comes from a file approved before its first validation or from this feature's
  own work, never from a download requested after a failed validation (FR-039).

**Carried-over requirements and the redesign checklist**

- **FR-047**: Each of feature-002 FR-001 to FR-009 MUST be re-decided as in the table
  "Re-decided feature-002 requirements" below; none is inherited silently.
- **FR-048**: Every item of the redesign checklist
  ([`seed-entropy-bound.md`](../../research/security/seed-entropy-bound.md) §5.3, groups A–G)
  MUST have a disposition, as in the table "Redesign checklist" below, and the final report MUST
  cite the evidence for each item this feature covers.

**Traceability and reporting**

- **FR-049**: FUT-SEC-1 to FUT-SEC-9 MUST be carried as requirements traced to tasks and
  evidence, as in the table "Security Policy traceability" below. FUT-SEC-7's statistical
  programme MUST stay open in the final report, and no battery runs in this feature (FR-040).
  FUT-SEC-5 is split: this feature specifies key loading, shows diffusion through instantiation
  (FR-029) and measures the first-order related-key and related-nonce differences of research
  R4, reported as "a single-difference avalanche measurement, not a related-key analysis";
  feature 005 analyses related-key and related-IV differential and linear attacks beyond first
  order, weak-key classes and key-dependent effects, and that analysis MUST stay open in the final
  report, the rationale's chapter "Attack classes" marking those attacks, the related-seed part of
  security.md §12.1's row "Differential and related-seed" beyond first order, "deferred to feature
  005 (FR-049)" and citing research R4's first-order differences for the rest (FR-034; RAT-088). The
  boundary keys of FR-058 are tests of the model, not a weak-key analysis. No
  claim, evaluation or certification may follow from this feature alone (FR-052).
- **FR-050**: Every report and document of this feature, each checklist and tasks.md
  included, MUST carry the design-form disclaimer, the block directly under this
  spec's title (FR-068), MUST use only the vocabulary of security.md §15
  ("proven bound", "best known attack", "no attack found within budget", "not
  evaluated"), and MUST label every target as a target. No artifact MAY describe `phyco-v3`, or
  any model, as secure, proven secure, unbreakable, random, truly random or high-entropy, or credit
  it with randomness, or state a security strength as achieved: Principle IV names "random" and
  "high-entropy" beside "cryptographically secure", and the uses that stay allowed are those of
  FR-069's standing allow-list entries. "Proven" may appear only with its object named in the same
  sentence or table row (a trail bound, a theorem's bound on an advantage) and its record cited;
  "proven" or "provably" never takes the design, the generator or a security property as its object.
  Frequency, avalanche and battery results are reported only as inputs to R_dist or as statistics,
  never as evidence of security or of mixing beyond what they measured, and no text implies
  prediction resistance (D7). The claim-register test (FR-069) treats every other use as a finding.
  Wherever the use of `phyco-v3` is described, the restriction of security.md §4 MUST be stated.
- **FR-051**: A new version of security.md, with a history entry, MUST give `phyco-v3` its own
  section (a table of structural facts, best-attack register rows for its first bounds, and a
  lessons table that links all fifteen of S1–S15 to the rules of Π or the parts of the design
  that answer them (FR-030), citing for each the evidence record, or the
  section and test where the answer is by specification, as the final report's
  lessons table does, and for S2, S5–S7 and S11–S15 the rows of the design
  document's chapter "Replaced mechanisms" with their records), a row in
  §1.1, which plan phase 1 adds with its line in §15 and this version keeps
  (SEC-095), a validation-phase table like §1.3, the §20.1 decision (recorded in
  2.11 on 2026-11-01; FR-017), the claim-register entry as it then stands
  (its line for `phyco-v3` at L0 is added in plan phase 1, in a security.md version of its own,
  which also gives security.md's opening and closing blocks FR-069's fixed
  sentence; FR-069; SEC-081, SEC-092), the status of roadmap stage 5a.1 and
  a mention in §23. security.md keeps policy, status and registers, and derivations and other
  per-model detail go to research notes or reports (strategy note G16 and §7.1). A finding that
  breaks the target on the full rounds MUST be recorded at once, with a history entry and a
  README update (strategy note §7.1, rule 5), as MUST a match of FR-023's refused list found after
  the freeze, with `phyco-v3` staying as frozen, and the standards rows of security.md §6 MUST be
  re-checked and dated (rule 8). README MUST state the status of `phyco-v3`, and the documentation
  drift listed in checklist group F MUST be corrected; README's status block, written for the models
  with a 64-bit seed, gains in that update a sentence on `phyco-v3`, a design at the register's
  level, with no claim and not for secrets, which the claim test checks against its
  `claim_level` and `claim` (FR-069; SEC-086). The new security.md version and
  README's update keep the fixed sentence that FR-069 has the three blocks
  hold from plan phase 1 (SEC-092). The new security.md version keeps the
  constitution version that 2.13 names, 1.3.0, in its header, §4, §18's tree and §22's stage 1.
- **FR-052**: The final phase report MUST follow the constitution's Phase Completion Reporting,
  MUST state which items of the constitution's change-completion list remain open (the
  statistical evaluation in feature 007; the RTL), MUST state the risks of strategy note §11 as
  they stand at the end of the feature, and MUST recommend proceed, revise or stop; a stop is
  reported with what was reached (security.md §21, option C). It MUST state that, with
  FUT-SEC-7 (feature 007) and FUT-SEC-5's analysis (feature 005) open and L2 to L5 not reached
  (features 005 to 009), no claim, evaluation or certification may follow from this feature
  alone, and list each gap with its feature, as security.md §22 rows 5a.2 to 5a.6 name them.
  Its open items carry FR-040's requirement on held-out keys in its checkable form, and
  FUT-GRID-5's validation of 4×4 first to features 006 and 008: in simulation before 8×8 and 16×16,
  and on the board before each larger claimable size that fits the XC7A100T (FR-036). The report
  reads 2×2 evidence only for properties that do not depend on the neighbourhood, such as
  bijectivity and the validation of tools whose behaviour does not depend on it (box and S-box
  tables, the bijectivity bitmap, the cost model's counts), and states 2×2's R_f, R_b, symmetries
  and invariants as facts about 2×2 alone, never as evidence about a claimable size (FR-032). Its
  open items for feature 008 also name each measurement FUT-HW-3 asks for: words per second by
  on-chip counters at the achieved clock, per claimable size that fits, compared with the C stream,
  and power through the board's VCCINT current sensor and the XADC, beside Vivado's estimate; this
  feature supplies only the implied throughput (FR-026).

**Reproducibility**

- **FR-053**: Every evidence record MUST be reproducible from a committed script with fixed inputs
  and the recorded command, with the model hash and tool versions recorded (Principle V), the kinds
  that FR-061 leaves out excepted, each with its reason: the records of an owner's act, an
  outside fact or an event, which a later run cannot regenerate, and the
  reproduction records, which are not reproduced, and a record that holds `ended_outside`, whose
  run gave no result (research R7; data-model §1; REP-085).
  `requirements.txt` MUST be corrected and its versions pinned (checklist group F), the Hypothesis
  pins entering it once their wheels are installed and checked (research R18). These rules
  make that checkable:
  - **Reproducible, per kind.** Deterministic kinds (vectors, constants, dependency matrices,
    counts, digests) reproduce byte for byte; searches and solver runs reproduce their values
    and verdicts. A reproduction is run by the script's `--reproduce RECORD` mode, the re-check's,
    the generator checks' and the cross-checks' included, from a separate worktree of the
    original's `git_head` whose blobs its code is checked against, and writes a
    `reproduction` record of its own, naming that commit; a generator's reproduction
    compares each generated file in memory with the SHA-256 its `generator-run`
    record holds (data-model §11); it appends nothing to the candidate log,
    charges no candidate's budget row (a heavy one, run only on the owner's
    request, charges research R7's feature-wide row) and is not a re-run
    under research R7, so it can neither replace evidence nor add any, and a
    difference is a finding: the original stands, a difference in a field that enters a verdict
    blocks the candidate's eligibility, `blocked`, until it is resolved (research R7, R16), and a
    difference in a field that decides no verdict, such as a p-value, is reported and blocks
    nothing. It compares the fields data-model §1 lists per kind (`kind`,
    `model_id`, `variant_id`, each source file's entry in `code_sha256`, `inputs`, each
    deterministic output's `sha256` and `bytes`, `exit_status`, `assertions`, `status`,
    `classification` and the fields of its kind, and `design_sha256` only for the kinds whose code
    reads the design document) and reports every other field only, timings and raw solver logs among
    them; a different source hash makes it a new run, not a reproduction. A `p_values` output is
    marked not deterministic and compared by its values, within the tolerance data-model §1 defines;
    a value outside makes the outcome `differs`, a finding about the run's
    reproducibility that blocks no candidate, since a p-value decides nothing. The verdict is
    recomputed under the spec version, the research version, the design version and the protocol
    change-log entry the original cites, the design document being read from the worktree of
    `git_head`, where the record's `design_sha256` is not `none`, and the run exiting 2 unless it
    matches `design-versions/<sha256>.md` and that hash (REP-099), and a reproduction runs in the
    environment that made the original, the environment record the original cites, a `recheck`
    record's reproduction excepted, which runs anywhere, as the re-check does (research R1):
    `--reproduce` exits 2, writing nothing, with the reason "environment record <id> is not in
    force", while that record is not the platform's record in force, so that no difference is ever
    charged to a tool that a changed environment could explain, and a record made under an earlier
    record stays the evidence of its environment (REP-100).
  - **Code by hash.** Every record holds the SHA-256 of every code file the run used (FR-061).
    The model hash is that of the candidate's operation list before selection and that of
    `model_v3.py` after it. Code is committed before every evidence run; `git_dirty` may be true
    for data and reports only, never for a file a record cites, the spec,
    research.md and the design document among them, every run that writes evidence
    exiting 2 while one is modified in the working tree, so that the original's
    `git_head` holds each version its record cites (data-model §1; REP-099), and
    the record lists the dirty paths: a run that writes evidence exits 2
    unless every file in its `code_sha256`, each C tool's binary among them, matches HEAD,
    committed and unmodified, which constitution 1.3.0's standing authorization to commit makes
    routine. Each C tool's binary is built from its committed source and committed after it is
    built and before the run that uses it (research R18), so that each record's code, its
    binaries among it, is then found at its `git_head`, and a test checks each `code_sha256`
    entry against the blob at that commit. Its make file builds into it the SHA-256 of that
    source, which the binary prints with `--source-sha256`, and the guard also exits 2 unless
    the printed hash equals the source's entry in `code_sha256`, so that the binary that runs
    was built from its committed source (research R16). A reproduction runs the original's code
    at its `git_head`, its committed binaries included, the guard applying to that commit. It also
    removes each C tool's committed binary from a second worktree of that commit, so that make
    cannot take it as up to date, rebuilds it there from that commit's source with its make file
    and reports the rebuild's hash only; where the rebuild's SHA-256 differs from the committed
    binary's, it runs the recorded configuration again there with the rebuild and compares that
    run with the original too, so that both the binary that ran and one built from its committed
    source reproduce the result. A rebuild that differs is a new version of the tool (FR-039):
    where the guards Tool validation and Trail tools do not admit it, no such run is made and the
    reproduction counts a difference in every compared field, as for a rebuild that fails, so that
    a binary that its committed source does not give is a finding. Each make file compiles with
    relative paths and no debug information, so that a correct tool rebuilds to the same bytes in
    any worktree, which a test checks (research R16).
  - **Canonical order.** Every list in a record or vector file is sorted by a stated key, so
    that nothing depends on set iteration, hash randomization, thread completion or worker
    count. Records hold `PYTHONHASHSEED` if it is set, and the worker and thread counts; a test runs
    a stated test operation list with two worker counts and compares the outputs. That test is a
    heavy run (research R7): it takes the lock, projects first, charges no candidate, only research
    R7's feature-wide row, and is not evidence.
  - **Hashes and line endings.** Each recorded hash says beside it whether it is over the raw
    bytes or over LF-normalized content (`raw` or `lf-normalized`). Every new file whose hash
    is recorded is hashed over its raw bytes and MUST have a pinned line ending in
    `.gitattributes` (LF, or `binary`), so that its raw and normalized content agree on every
    checkout: `reports/004-crypto-architecture-v3/**`, `research/004/**` and `protocol/*.json` are
    pinned to LF, and `*.gz` and every file under a `build/` directory, where each C tool's
    binary is built (research R18), to `binary`. Files committed under
    `reports/004-crypto-architecture-v3/` are limited to `.json`, `.jsonl`, `.md`, `.csv`, `.txt`
    and `.gz`, and `protocol/evidence.py` refuses any other suffix there, as research R16 and the
    data-model introduction state; `research/004/**` holds the plan's Python code beside files of
    those suffixes, and `protocol/*.json` JSON only, each pinned to LF as above. Every `.gz` file
    this feature writes is written by `gzip.compress(data, compresslevel=9, mtime=0)`, and its
    record also holds the SHA-256 of the uncompressed content, which a reproduction compares for
    every `.gz` file but a `p_values` file, whose values it compares instead (data-model §1). A test
    checks the header of every committed `.gz` file under this feature's paths
    (`reports/004-crypto-architecture-v3/`, `research/004/`, `vectors/phyco-v3/` and
    `baseline/phyco-v2/`); feature 003's `.gz` files, listed with their hashes in its `SHA256SUMS`,
    are outside its scope and stay unchanged. An existing `text=auto` file that a new record hashes,
    such as `security.md` or `README.md`, is hashed over its LF-normalized content, and no existing
    pin changes. The CRLF-pinned freeze records of features 002 and 003 keep their recorded hashes
    over CRLF bytes. A test lists every path whose hash a committed record, manifest or freeze
    record holds and checks with `git check-attr text eol` that each has an explicit rule or
    `binary`, or that its record marks the hash `lf-normalized`; it replaces the one-off check of
    2026-10-31 and runs from plan phase 1 on, so that a path missed later fails it as soon as the
    first record that cites it is committed.
  - **Encoding.** Every new text file is UTF-8 without a byte-order mark, with LF line endings.
    Scripts write with `encoding="utf-8"` and `newline="\n"`, the line-ending test also looks
    for a byte-order mark, and no hashed file is written by Windows PowerShell 5.1, whose
    `Out-File -Encoding utf8` adds one.

**Verification and translation readiness (security.md §8, §10 and §13; vhdl-genai.md §5)**

- **FR-054**: The design document MUST list the edge states of Π and the mode, and a committed
  script MUST generate a constructed-state vector for each, from chosen states as well as from
  development keys: at least all-zero and all-one lanes, states that drive every addition to a
  carry out of its top bit, every shift and rotation boundary, the first and last value of every
  counter, every output limit reached and one request beyond it, and every domain separator.
  These vectors are part of the frozen vectors (FR-043; vhdl-genai.md R3 and §5.3, step 1). On
  the RTL they run in simulation only, loaded by the testbench (FR-025), and the record of an
  edge state that no key reaches says that it is covered in simulation only. The design document's
  list of edge states marks each as reached by the normal calls from development keys within a
  stated number of calls of Π, or covered only from a chosen state, which the research interface's
  `ResearchDuplex.from_state` sets (model contract), and `gen_constructed_v3.py` writes each vector
  the way the list names, the vector saying which (REP-078).
- **FR-055**: Every operation on state words in Π and the mode MUST give the same bits whether
  its operands are read as signed or unsigned: addition and subtraction modulo 2^w, XOR, AND,
  OR, NOT, rotations and logical shifts. An arithmetic shift, a signed comparison or a sign
  extension MAY appear only with a justification in the rationale and constructed-state vectors
  at its sign boundary (FR-054), because those are the corners where translations of `phyco-v2`
  go wrong (vhdl-genai.md §5.2). Comparisons of public counters MUST be unsigned. Every shift
  and rotation amount lies in 1 to w − 1 at every supported width (FR-024), and every operation
  is written as an unsigned operation on `unsigned` words (FR-027).
- **FR-056**: The frozen model, vectors, known-answer tests and constructed-state vectors MUST be
  the only source of expected values for every later implementation, and none MAY come from a
  drafting tool or be edited to make a test pass (vhdl-genai.md R1). This feature writes no RTL,
  testbench or mutant catalogue. Where the frozen design document and the frozen model
  disagree, the frozen model and vectors are the expected values, and the correction is a new
  model ID (FR-028). The final report MUST map each of the hardware note's rules R1–R12, and the
  AI-assisted RTL rules of security.md §13, to the requirement of this feature that applies it
  or to feature 006 or 008, which carries it. The mapping carries three rules of this feature:
  constructed-state vectors run on the RTL in simulation only, loaded by the testbench, while
  the board's equivalence check (FUT-HW-4) uses the vectors a key reaches; 4×4 first, in feature
  006's simulation before 8×8 and 16×16 and on feature 008's board before each larger claimable size
  that fits the XC7A100T (FUT-GRID-5, FUT-HW-2; FR-036); and the self-test's `selftest_fail` output,
  latched until a self-test passes, which feature 008 tests (FR-059; HW-081).
- **FR-057**: A second transcription of Π, Π⁻¹ and the mode MUST be written from the design
  document alone, in a session separate from the one that wrote the model and importing no code
  from it, and MUST agree with the model step by step on the vectors before the freeze, as
  `tests/v2_reference.py` does for `phyco-v2` (security.md §8 acceptance criteria, §10.2). That
  session MUST start only after the owner's recorded go-ahead, the Review & Approval row "Second
  transcription go-ahead", set by the owner and committed, and the provenance records of the
  model and the transcription (FR-067) MUST show that they come from different sessions. The
  agreement MUST be reported with the common-mode caveat of security.md §10.2; an
  implementation from outside the project belongs to feature 006.
- **FR-058**: The tests of the model MUST cover the items of security.md §10.1 as they apply to a
  keyed design: minimum, maximum and boundary values; all-zero and all-one keys and nonces;
  single-bit keys; overflow and truncation; shift and rotation boundaries; reset, zeroization and
  repeated instantiation; long runs; invalid parameters, invalid states and invalid call
  sequences such as generate before instantiate; and every supported configuration. Every
  data-flow direction stated in the design document (FR-027) MUST be tested (checklist group
  F). The tests also show the stated behaviour on a repeated nonce in each case the
  model can run, each beside the same calls under the same key and nonce:
  another personalization string, other request lengths, another order of
  calls and a reseed with another input (edge case "Nonce reuse"; SEC-083),
  every zeroization path of FR-025 (a refused request, a failed self-test, an exception after
  the state has changed, a call refused at a per-key limit), the refusal of an `instantiate` whose
  personalization string is over its maximum, before the first instantiate and after one, nothing
  changing and the earlier instantiation's output continuing, with the `instantiate`
  after it (FR-025 "Flow control"; HW-083), the refusal of a generate request
  after reset or zeroization until an instantiate completes, and that the key, given once at
  construction, is never compared with another (FR-013). The refusals at a per-key or
  per-instantiation limit, and quickstart M5's refusals beyond every limit, are tested through
  `ResearchDuplex.from_state`, research-only, which starts the mode from a chosen state and chosen
  counter values, each within its range, and every other path through the normal calls;
  `Generator` has no such path, and a test checks that it has none and that
  `Generator` and `ResearchDuplex` enforce every limit through one shared
  function (model contract; REP-078). A failed construction, by a refused
  variant, a wrong key length or a failed self-test, leaves no object: the constructor checks in
  that order, and each refusal is tested with an input that would also fail every later check, so
  the order shows. After `zeroize`, the key buffer the object held reads all zeros and the object no
  longer refers to it (model contract). Property-based tests (for example with
  Hypothesis) MUST be added if the owner approves the download; otherwise their absence MUST be
  recorded as an open item. Until the approved wheels are installed and checked, the property tests
  skip with a reason naming that open item and none fails, and the final report lists the skipped
  tests. Each platform's first environment record, of the interpreter and the packages that
  `requirements.txt` pins before the wheels, comes before every other write through
  `protocol/evidence.py` or a log writer: it is the one write the guard "Environment" admits while
  no record is in force, checked against research R1 and those pins instead (cli contract,
  "Environment"; PRO-068); once the wheels are installed and the pins take effect, each platform's
  new record is written and approved before any later write, in plan phase 1's order, and the
  property tests run before any later record; a wheel installed after a record needs a new
  environment record, approved before any later record (research R1, R17, R18; REP-081,
  REP-082). They run under a settings profile registered by the test package,
  with `derandomize=True` and `database=None`, so that every run tries the same examples and a
  failure replays by running again.
- **FR-059**: The design document MUST define a known-answer self-test for start-up as a step of
  the reset sequence of FR-025, whose stated latency includes it: its inputs, taken from the
  frozen vectors (which the vector script copies, in the model, into `phycoweb/selftest_v3.py`,
  imported only in `Generator`'s constructor, so that a `Generator` built before that file exists
  fails its self-test; FR-036), its expected output, its behaviour on failure, and the zeroization
  of the whole state after it, before the first instantiate. The self-test runs instantiate with key
  and nonce 0 and the empty personalization string, its length word fed through the absorption
  interface's selector as the host would; then one generate request of k whole
  blocks, k·r bits, k stated per claimable size and per keyed research variant
  (HW-095) in the design document as the least for which every node, every lane,
  every constant ROM entry of the calls run, both trees and the counters take part; then a
  reseed with reseed input 0 of research R3 and a second generate request of
  k whole blocks, so that the reseed's effect is compared; the self-test holds that reseed input and
  its length word among its constants and feeds them through the absorption interface's selector,
  word by word as the host would, their storage in FR-026's estimate and their cycles in FR-020's
  self-test formula and the reset figure (FR-025; HW-082). Its stored constants are its key, its
  nonce, the empty personalization string's length word, its reseed input with that input's length
  word and its expected output, and its parts are these with the selector at each chain's input, the
  one at the absorption interface and its comparator, as FR-034's list and FR-026's
  estimate name them (RAT-079). A structure takes part as the model's run
  shows it: a lane, and the node that holds it, once its value is computed in a call of Π of the
  run, each constant ROM entry once a call that reads it runs, the absorption tree once each kind of
  the self-test's input words has entered it, the output tree once a whole block has left it, and
  each counter once it has counted, the round and phase counters through a whole call, the request's
  block count, one of the controller's counters (FR-025 "Zeroization"; HW-089), to its last
  block, the read-out's count of transfers within a block through a whole
  block and the request's count of remaining words to the request's last word
  (HW-094), and each limit counter by one step; k is the least number of whole
  blocks for which the run meets them all, which may be 1, unless the design document names a
  structure that needs more, with its reason, in its table of k (FR-034 "Chapters"), one row per
  claimable size and per keyed research variant with k, the structure that set it
  and, where k exceeds the least that the model's run gives, the structure
  the design document names with its reason; `gen_vectors_v3.py` reads k
  from that table, computes the least k from its self-test run, exits 1 if the table's k is below
  the least k of its run, or above it with no named structure and reason, and writes k, the
  structure that set it and the run's least k into the self-test's vector file, and a test computes
  the least k again from the model's run and checks the file's k against it and against the table,
  equal unless a named structure with its reason raised it (HW-084; REP-097). Before selection, the
  self-test's figures take the least k that each `cost` record computes from a run of the candidate
  through the self-test's call sequence, its operation list interpreted by `research/004/ir.py` in
  the shared mode, recorded with the structure that set it, the design document stating the
  selected candidate's k equal to it or raised by a named structure with its
  reason (FR-020, FR-026; REP-097). `gen_vectors_v3.py` writes these inputs
  and every expected block, whole, in the same generator run as the vectors, from a `ResearchDuplex`
  object of its own run through the self-test's call sequence alone, instantiate with key and nonce
  0 and the empty personalization string, one generate request of k whole blocks with its forget
  call and the reseed with reseed input 0 of research R3 and the second request of k whole blocks
  with its forget call, so that its expected blocks are those that the model's `Generator` and
  the hardware's self-test compute (REP-096), as a vector file of its own per claimable
  size and per keyed research variant (HW-095), `selftest_<variant>.json`,
  frozen with the vectors, from which `phycoweb/selftest_v3.py` is copied, so
  that the frozen vectors hold every value the comparator compares; FR-020's
  self-test formula, the reset figure and FR-026's storage count follow from k
  (HW-077). It feeds the key and nonce chains from its constants through a
  selector at each chain's input, word by word as the host would, the nonce's words for rate lanes
  through the absorption tree's input in the same way, with their cycles in the reset figure
  (FR-025) and no parallel load path; afterwards the selectors return to the interfaces. The
  selectors' control is a function of FR-025's call states: the constants are selected in
  `resetting` only, and every other state selects the interfaces, so that no call accepted from the
  host runs on the self-test's constants (SEC-082). These are three selectors, at the key chain's
  input, at the nonce chain's input and at the absorption interface, and FR-008's risk and feature
  008's test cover each held on its constants, alone or with the others, the test matching the
  output, block by block, with the model's after a passed self-test, a key load and an instantiate
  with the host's key, nonce and personalization string, each other than the
  self-test's, and after a reseed with an input other than the self-test's
  and a second request, so that each selector held alone, as well as all of
  them, gives an output that differs from the model's (FR-008; SEC-088). Its
  comparator, r bits wide, takes each whole r-bit block as the output buffer receives it and each of
  the block's words as the read-out gives it at the interface width, the read-out stepping with no
  consumer handshake and the output port's valid held low (HW-094), compares each with the
  matching block of the expected output, or that block's matching word, stated
  per claimable size and per keyed research variant from the frozen vectors
  (HW-095), and holds the result until the last block; the comparator and the
  expected output's storage are parts of FR-026's estimate. The self-test then zeroizes, both chains
  and the rate lanes' nonce registers included; on failure it zeroizes, blocks every call and
  sets the `selftest_fail` output, an output of its own, latched until a self-test passes (state
  `failed`, FR-025 "Call sequence"): it stays set through every later reset, `resetting` included, a
  pass clearing it at the self-test's end and a second failure leaving it set,
  so that a host never reads a failure as cleared before a pass (HW-081), the host reading
  `selftest_fail` once `call_done` rises after a reset, a second failure included (FR-025 "Registers
  and read-back"; HW-086); every reset runs the self-test again, as a new `Generator` runs it again
  in the model. The design document lists the output in the port list (FR-025
  "Registers and read-back"), and feature 008 tests it. Its cycle count is stated (FR-020), the
  read-out's ⌈r/i⌉ transfers of each of its blocks among its cycles (HW-094), and the
  reset latency of FR-025 runs from reset released to the self-test done
  and the state zeroized. Features 006 and 008 implement it in the RTL (strategy note §10; D10). It
  is not a FIPS 140-3 self-test and MUST NOT be described as one (FR-011). Its blocks go only to its
  comparator and never to the output port, with output-valid low throughout (FR-025 "Zeroization"),
  and its tests cover a call made in each state of FR-025's call-state table, among them one before
  the first reset and one while the self-test runs, which the core does not accept in `not-reset`
  and `resetting` ("Call sequence").

**Findings, evidence and review (security.md §9.1, §14, §16 and §17)**

- **FR-060**: Every finding of this feature, about `phyco-v3` or `phyco-v2`, MUST be classified
  as in security.md §9.1: confirmed vulnerability, strong theoretical concern, unverified
  hypothesis, or no weakness identified within the analysis performed. A negative result MUST be
  worded as limited to its scope, and a proposed attack MUST NOT be reported as a vulnerability
  until it is proved or reproduced (security.md §12.4). The classification is computed by code from
  data-model §1's table by kind and outcome (FR-065), and a reviewer may add only a note. The table
  gives every kind and outcome its class: a record without a finding holds `none`, a fired control
  among them, with `status` `inconclusive` for that test and size; a firing, a trail found by a
  validated tool or a `dependency` record showing incomplete diffusion, raising R or not, is a
  confirmed vulnerability of the reduced rounds it covers, worded with its round count and, for a
  firing, the stated family-wise chance of a false firing; a failed inverse, bijectivity or symmetry
  check, or a kept invariant, is a confirmed vulnerability of the candidate, worded as the failed
  property, with its decision; a `degree` record that raises R_dist is a strong theoretical concern,
  since an argument shows it and no run demonstrates it; a proven bound, or a test that does not
  fire, is "no weakness identified within the analysis performed", with its scope; a reproduction
  that differs on a field that enters a verdict, a record whose verdict the re-check, under
  the rules in force at that record's entry, does not recompute (FR-065),
  and a finding of a tool not yet validated, stay unverified hypotheses,
  `blocked`; and the demonstration record is a confirmed vulnerability of
  `phyco-v2`'s seed bound once every target is recovered.
- **FR-061**: Every evidence record MUST hold the fields of security.md §17.2: a unique
  identifier, which names the record's file by one rule (data-model §1), and
  the date and time; the model ID, design-document version, read from the
  document's version line (FR-034), `git_head` and `git_dirty`; tool and
  dependency versions, operating system and hardware; the exact command and
  configuration, runnable as written (the interpreter by its full path: on Windows the
  `python.exe` of the Store distribution's versioned prefix, `sys.executable` with the
  alias resolved, and in WSL `~/.venvs/phycoweb/bin/python`, as research R1
  states; the working directory, which is the repository root; paths relative
  to it), with a test that reproduces exactly as recorded, through the script's `--reproduce` mode
  (FR-053), per kind, a run that was light by research R7's test, light being a property of each
  run: the kind's latest such run made under the platform's environment record in force and, where
  one exists under it, one made before the last change to its code, the test listing each kind whose
  run before that change was made under an earlier record, with that record (REP-100); the test
  leaves out, each with its reason, the claim test, which writes no record and runs
  as a test, a `lock-cleared` record, which records an event, a tie choice, an owner's own act, the
  reproduction records, which are not reproduced, and the `environment`, `downloads` and `freeze`
  records, whose writers have no `--reproduce`, each recording an owner's act or an outside fact
  that a later run cannot regenerate (data-model §1; REP-085); the SHA-256
  of every code file the run used (`code_sha256`: the script, the
  `protocol/` modules, the candidate's operation list, `research/004/ir.py` and its tools, a C
  source and its binary), each committed at HEAD before the run and a binary built from committed
  source (FR-053); the inputs or their derivation (the namespace, kind, index range
  and fingerprint of each input taken through the loader; data-model §2); output paths with SHA-256,
  and for each whether it is deterministic (a raw solver log is not, and a reproduction reports its
  hash only); the exit status and assertions; the classification (FR-060); the analyst and the
  reviewer, with the review status; known limitations; and the disclaimer, which feature 003's
  provenance records lacked (feature-003 phase report, open issue 3). A statistical measurement,
  such as the bias rule's, MUST also record the items of security.md §11.6. Raw tool output MUST be
  kept, not only summaries. A generator run writes a `generator-run` record with these fields and
  each written file's path and SHA-256, the sidecar left out, its entry citing the record
  (data-model §11; REP-092). The four kinds kept under a name of their own, the environment records,
  the downloads manifest, the freeze record and the review package's manifest, hold these fields for
  the run of the script that wrote each or its latest change, `heavy_run` false and `inputs` and
  `outputs` `none`, the files each names being held in its own fields (data-model §1; REP-093).
- **FR-062**: The final report and the design rationale MUST keep apart demonstrated facts,
  mathematical arguments, experimental observations, unverified hypotheses, unsuccessful
  attacks, untested areas and residual risks (security.md §17.3). In the rationale each
  statement carries one of seven labels (fact, argument, observation, hypothesis, unsuccessful
  attack, untested, residual risk), by section heading where a section holds one kind and
  inline otherwise, the label fixed by the statement's support. The gap between single-trail
  bounds and bounds on differentials and linear hulls is stated as a residual risk for feature
  005 (FR-034). The final report MUST name which of the nine reports of security.md §17.3 this
  feature contributes to, and which are not started.
- **FR-063**: A review package MUST be assembled for the external design review, whether or not
  a reviewer is arranged: the spec, the design document and rationale with exact revisions, the
  model source and vectors, the evidence records, the threat model, the security target marked
  as targets, and the known limitations and unresolved findings (security.md §14). Sending it is
  the owner's outward action (Principle IX). The package carries the design-form disclaimer and
  states that the review asks for comments on a design that makes no claim and is not an
  evaluation intended to support one (section "Context"). Where its manifest hashes an existing
  `text=auto` file, such as security.md or README.md, the hash is over LF-normalized content
  and marked so (FR-053). Once the external design review is done, its Review & Approval row names
  the manifest it read by SHA-256, with the design-document version, which `rationale.md`'s header
  holds too (FR-034; RAT-087), and the selection record it carried, so that a
  package rebuilt later leaves that review tied to the version it read (FR-043;
  SEC-077). `scripts/review_package.py` assembles the package and writes its manifest, a record of
  kind `review-package` (data-model §1, §16), whose `--check` rebuilds the manifest from the files
  it lists and compares it (REP-085). The manifest groups its entries by security.md §14's six
  items, each listing by path and SHA-256 the files that stand for it: the specification, the spec,
  the design document and `rationale.md`, each at its revision; the source code and test vectors,
  the model source and the vectors; the statistical, attack and hardware reports, the evidence
  records and reports that exist, with the code of every tool that wrote an attack or search record,
  and for a kind of report this feature makes none of, the feature that does, feature 007 for the
  statistical programme and feature 006 for the hardware; the threat model, the design document's
  chapter "Security target"; the claims and assumptions, the register `protocol/claims.json`,
  `parameters.json` and `limits.json` with each theorem's preconditions, and the security target
  marked as targets; and the known limitations and unresolved findings, from the rationale's chapter
  "Attack classes", every record's `limitations` and the manifest's field `unresolved`, a list that
  `review_package.py` writes of every finding that no later record, decision or entry closes, a
  `confirmed vulnerability` or `unverified hypothesis`, a re-check disagreement, a claim-test
  finding and an item the final report leaves open. `--check` rebuilds that list from the records,
  and the test of User Story 5's scenario 6 fails a §14 item with no file (data-model §16; RAT-089).
- **FR-064**: No AI session MAY be recorded as an independent or external reviewer: a second AI
  review is not independent cryptanalysis (security.md §14), and a security argument drafted by
  an AI tool is a draft for review, not evidence (security.md §2.1 and §16.3; vhdl-genai.md
  §2.5). The final report MUST record who took each role of security.md §16.2 in this feature,
  and mark every role that was not staffed, computed from the provenance log's review entries, whose
  `reviewer` names who reviewed (RAT-077). Each AI-drafted section of the rationale is recorded in
  the provenance log with its review status, the owner design review adding its review entry, the
  chapters "Security target" and "Mode" included, which the security-target review also
  reads (FR-034; RAT-086); the agent writes the owner design review's review
  entries after the commit that records its approval and before the freeze's step (1), in the
  working tree, which that step's commit holds, and `design_freeze.py create` exits 2 while an
  AI-drafted section of the design document or of `rationale.md` lacks, as its latest entry, an
  `accepted` review entry naming its review's row with the highest k (FR-034,
  FR-043; RAT-082). A review is a new entry for the same path and section,
  holding the `seq` of the entry it follows and the SHA-256 of the section text reviewed, with
  `reviewer`, the role and name of who reviewed, the owner for the security-target review and the
  owner design review and an outside reviewer by name where one took part, never an AI tool or
  session, `row`, the Review & Approval row by name and k, and `reviewed_sha256`, the SHA-256 that
  row names; the agent runs `provenance.py review` only on the owner's explicit word for that entry,
  quoted with its date in it, as for a row's approval (RAT-077); and the latest entry for that path
  and section gives the status, so that no entry changes; the frozen text
  carries no status (FR-034, FR-067).
- **FR-065**: Every verdict of this feature (the demonstration; the tool validations and
  re-validations and the cross-checks between kinds; the inverse, bijectivity, dependency, bias,
  uniform-control, symmetry, invariant, degree and bound checks; the `trail` records'
  weights and classifications; the D4 choice; the check of generated files on
  each platform; the size profile's generic-bound checks and the bound at the limits;
  eligibility and selection, with every decision and `replacement` state of the
  candidate log) and every record's classification (FR-060) and `status`,
  the freeze record's included, whose approval state is a field of its own,
  `freeze_status` (data-model §1, §13; REP-093), MUST be computed by code from saved outputs
  and be recomputable by a re-check script, never taken from a narrative (security.md §16.1). For a
  `trail` record the re-check recomputes each trail's exact weight, as a rational, from its
  committed characteristic and the committed operation list that the candidate's `oplist_sha256`
  names, by exact integer arithmetic, and checks that the characteristic holds round by round and
  that the record's classification follows from that weight, the 2⁻¹²⁸ threshold included (ANA-086),
  and it recomputes every decision of the candidate log and its `replacement` state with eligibility
  and selection (ANA-088), a disagreement blocking the candidate as for any record. No
  phase, test or gate MAY be recorded as passed because its documents exist; where evidence is
  missing, the status MUST be "not evaluated", "inconclusive" or "blocked" (security.md §16.3). The
  re-check needs only committed files, so every verdict is recomputable on a fresh clone: the
  committed per-round summaries suffice (for example, R_f from the per-round flag "every output bit
  depends on every input bit", and the bias tool's validation from each synthetic set's committed
  summary), and local-only outputs serve only to re-derive a committed summary, which the re-check
  also does when they are present; it lists both checks per kind. A record that `clear_lock.py`
  wrote for a run that died holding the lock holds no saved output and no verdict: the re-check
  checks its `status`, its classification and its hours used, or a `projection` record's time,
  against it and the `lock-cleared` record that names it (data-model §1). The re-check is the
  routine
  reproduction test (User Story 4), and it runs the frozen verdict scripts (FR-043): the verdict
  functions it applies are those of `protocol/evidence.py`, which the scripts that wrote the records
  call: for the rules in force at a record's entry, those at that record's `git_head`, from a
  separate worktree of that commit, named per record, so that a function changed before the freeze
  is never applied to records it did not compute, and for the current rules those at HEAD. It
  recomputes each verdict under the rules in force at the protocol change-log entry its
  record cites and, where a later stricter change applies (FR-044), also under the current rules,
  both listed. A disagreement under the rules in force at a record's entry blocks the candidate as a
  differing reproduction does: the record's result becomes an unverified hypothesis, `blocked`
  (FR-060), the `recheck` record is a finding, and the disagreement is a tool defect (edge cases),
  whose one re-run follows the fix; a disagreement under a later stricter change is that change's
  recomputed verdict, applied as a stricter change (FR-044) and recorded in the change log's
  `results_known`, not a defect; selection waits while a disagreement of the first kind is
  unresolved for a record it cites (data-model §9); and a `recheck` record's reproduction that
  differs is a tool defect of the re-check wherever it ran, since its verdicts and counts
  depend on no environment. It is exempt from the environment guard, since
  it recomputes verdicts from committed counts by exact integer arithmetic (data-model §5) and
  imports only the standard library and those `protocol/` modules that do the same, which a test
  checks; its `recheck` record holds the interpreter, platform and full `pip freeze` it ran with,
  and says when that environment is not one of research R1's.
- **FR-066**: This feature MUST prepare what feature 005's copy of the cryptanalysis
  instructions needs (security.md §16.4; strategy note G13): the model ID and design-document
  version for its placeholders, `phyco-v3`'s threat-model rows (FR-008), its table of structural
  facts and its best-attack register rows (FR-051).
- **FR-067**: Every file of this feature drafted or repaired with an AI tool (design document,
  model, transcription, scripts, tests and reports) MUST be recorded with the tool and model,
  the date, the task ID and its review status, in the feature's evidence (vhdl-genai.md R7;
  security.md §17.2). A file drafted in parts, such as the rationale, is recorded per section, a
  section running from its heading, an ATX heading outside fenced code blocks as CommonMark reads
  it, to the line before the next one of the same or a higher level, or to the file's end,
  and named by its heading's text, preceded, where that text occurs more
  than once in the file, by the texts of the headings that enclose it, from
  the highest level down, joined by " > " (data-model §14; REP-088). The
  review status is `pending`, `accepted` or `changes-requested`, and a review is a new provenance
  entry, never a change to an entry or a record (FR-064; data-model §1, §14). Its one writer is
  `protocol/provenance.py`, run as `scripts/provenance.py`, whose `add` writes an
  entry and `review` a review, and, for the one append to a broken chain, as
  `scripts/repair_log.py`, whose `repair` entry it admits while its row "Log repair <n>"
  reads Approved at HEAD (research R16; data-model §14; PRO-076), each under
  the log-append lock; it refuses, exit 2, an entry with a field missing,
  a `section` that names no section or more than one (REP-088) or a `sha256` that is not
  that of the named file, section or record, or a review entry whose `row`
  does not read at HEAD, with its `reviewed_sha256`, the decision the entry records, Approved
  for `accepted` and Declined for `changes-requested`, or whose `reviewer` names an AI tool or
  session (FR-064; RAT-077; cli contract), or a review entry while the named file, or, where the row
  names a chapter by its own SHA-256, the chapter, does not have at that moment the SHA-256 that the
  row names for it, while its `follows` is not the latest entry for that path and section, or while
  the section's text differs from the text whose `sha256` that entry holds, so that the status the
  latest entry gives is that of the text the reviewer read, a section changed after its last
  entry getting an `add` entry before its review (RAT-081). Files drafted
  or repaired before the log exists, the planning documents, the checklists and checklist-choices.md
  among them, and the files of plan phase 1 written before it, are in this requirement's scope, with
  every file outside the feature directory that this feature's sessions changed, as `git log` from
  the spec's first commit, 801c6d7, shows, the log read on 2026-11-02 listing
  `research/004/place_check.py` and `.gitattributes`, entered whole, and security.md, the
  constitution, `.specify/memory/constitution.md`, whose amendment to 1.3.0 an AI session drafted
  for the owner's approval, and the strategy note `research/security/crypto-redesign-strategy.md`,
  entered per section changed, as a file drafted in parts is (REP-089): right after the log's
  creation, before any other entry, `provenance.py add` writes one entry per such file, or per
  section changed of a file entered per section, the sections and the commits that
  changed them as data-model §14 states (REP-095), holding `sha256` and its
  kind (REP-079) of the file or section as committed then, `date_utc` as
  the writer adds it, `drafted_from` and `drafted_to`, the dates of the first and last commits from
  801c6d7 on that changed the file or the section, never an earlier feature's (REP-089),
  `session` "several, not recorded" unless one is known, `tool` and `model` as
  known and otherwise "not recorded", `task_id` `none` for a file drafted before tasks.md existed
  and the task's ID for a file of plan phase 1, and `review_status` `pending` until the owner
  reviews it, a review entry following then. The entries, not the history, state that an AI tool
  drafted them, and a later change to such a file gets an entry as any file does (data-model §14;
  REP-080).

**Claims discipline (security.md §1 and §15; strategy note §3.1 and §10)**

- **FR-068**: The design-form disclaimer, the block directly under this spec's title, MUST be

  and ends with the owner's commit that records the decision of the row "Final

  the register that FR-012 names, so that the test reports no finding on
  the level at any commit of the window, and `design_freeze.py create` exits 2 while it reports
  any finding at HEAD (SEC-084). In its rules "near" and "beside" mean the
  same sentence or table row, as they do for "target", and every rule matches whole words, ignoring

  and randomization, with their spellings in -ise; high-entropy; secure and
  securely; unbreakable; constant time and constant-time; and FR-030's list
  with its own. A word is a run of letters and digits, so "random-nonce",
  "random_state" and `numpy.random` hold "random" and `os.urandom` does not. A flagged line, a
  rule's own text included, passes only through an allow-list entry in the register, each with its
  reason, so "outside the rules that forbid it" is met by one entry per rule line; standing entries,
  each with its reason (data-model §15), let stand security.md §6's rows, which state a standard's
  own status, FR-025's sentence on zeroization in the model, which says what the model does not
  promise, the negative statements of FR-011, FR-049, FR-059 and the section "Context", which say
  what no artifact may call a model, a test or a review, and, in the checklists and

  `history` against the rows and the files, so that the blocks and the register stand only as the
  rules give them; outside the blocks entries pass the flags of the wording rules and of the level
  rule, as above; the test exits 1 on an entry that names a line of a block or a finding of the
  register's checks, and `tests/test_claims.py` covers each case. security.md's two blocks, which
  state the restriction with "an established cryptographic random-number generator",
  a phrase that passes only through its standing entry, word it from plan
  phase 1, in the version that gives them the fixed sentence, as README's
  block does, with "an established cryptographic RNG" (SEC-094). The agent
  drafts each entry, standing phrase or line entry, with its path and text, its reason, its writer
  and the date; the test counts an entry from the commit that adds it, so that it runs from plan
  phase 1 on, and prints on every run each entry added since the last ⛔ gate's approval commit, and
  the owner's review at each ⛔ gate lists those entries with their reasons, as it lists the approval

  standing phrase on sample sentences that must and must not be flagged, with
  one that must be flagged for each form listed above and "phyco-v3 is unbreakable" among them,
  "phyco-v3 is at L2" and "the design reaches L3" among those that must be flagged and
  security.md §15's ladder rows among those that must pass (SEC-085), as research R18's test does
  for the mapping chapter's rule. The rules flag:
  - a strength not labelled as a target: a number of bits beside "security", "strength" or
    "secure", or a cost such as 2¹²⁸ beside "security", unless the same sentence or row carries
    "target" or quotes a rule;
  - "proven" or "provably", with the other forms of "prove", without its object and its record
    (FR-050);
  - frequency, avalanche or battery results offered as evidence of security or of mixing, and
    any text that implies prediction resistance (FR-050);
  - "compliant", "conforms" and "approved", with their inflections, near a standard's name, outside
    the design document's one statement that `phyco-v3` conforms to no standard (FR-011);
  - "DRBG" beside a model ID, "the model", "the generator" or "the design" (FR-011);
  - a claim level above the register's `claim_level` in any document: every name of a level above
    it, matched as a whole word in every scanned line, as "random" is, `claim_level` being, for each
    line, the lowest level the register gives the models the line names by ID, and L0 where it names
    none, every other model staying at L0 in this feature, so that L1 to L6 are flagged in every
    line while `phyco-v3` is at L0, and, while it is at L1, L2 to L6 in a line that names it alone
    and L1 to L6 in every other line, and a level stated for a model in any words is caught;
    standing entries, each with its reason (data-model §15), let stand the lines that name a level
    without stating one for a model, as security.md §6's rows stand: security.md §15's ladder and
    gate rows, which state each level's requirements, the rows' names "L1 recorded" and "L1 recorded
    <k>", `in_progress`'s "not reached" and data-model §17's sentence that L2 is never `claim_level`
    in this feature; the rule lines that move a model between levels, in FR-012, FR-069 and
    data-model §15 and §17, pass through one entry per rule line, as every rule's own text does; in
    the checklists and checklist-choices.md a level name inside a quotation passes as the other
    flagged words do, and any other flagged line needs a line entry with its reason (FR-012;
    SEC-085);
  - security wording and the arguments FR-030 forbids, whole words from FR-030's list with their
    inflections, ignoring case, inside the mapping chapter, the design document's section headed
