    negative statement passes, through its allow-list entry, the chapter's only entry; the test
    exits 1 if the heading is missing or appears twice (FR-030);
  - "constant time" and "constant-time" in every scanned line, not only beside the model, so that
    the statements on the C tools and on the RTL need entries with their reasons (FR-020);
  - "FIPS 140" beside "self-test" (FR-059);
  - "evaluation", "evaluated" and "certified" beside "external review" or "design review" (section
    "Context"; FR-043);
  - "related-key analysis" and "weak-key analysis" in every scanned line (FR-049);
  - "random" with its inflections, "truly random" among its uses, and "high-entropy", in every
    scanned line (Principle IV; FR-050). A standing entry is one fixed phrase with its reason and
    passes only a line that holds that phrase. The allowed uses stand through such phrases: "random
    permutation" and "ideal permutation" in a bound's statement; random nonces as a usage rule; the
    random baseline and the uniform controls of the tests; and "an established cryptographic
    random-number generator" in the disclaimer. Each other form of an allowed use, such as "a random
    nonce", "random-nonce limit" or "hash randomization", gets a standing phrase of its own, listed
    with its reason in data-model §15, or a line entry. The project's name holds no flagged word and
    needs no entry. Any other flagged line needs an entry of its own (data-model §15).
  - "secure", "securely" and "unbreakable", "cryptographically secure" and "proven secure" among
    their uses, in every scanned line, as "random" is (FR-050; SC-015). Standing phrases, each with
    its reason, let stand the rule lines of FR-050 and SC-015, which name the words they forbid, and
    the negative statements, such as README's "**not** cryptographically secure" and security.md's
    "is not thereby cryptographically secure"; in the checklists and checklist-choices.md a flagged
    word inside a quotation passes as above, and any other flagged line needs an entry of its own.

### Pre-registered Design Protocol

Fixed at spec approval; afterwards it may only become stricter (FR-044). Each rule states what
stricter means for it, and every change is recorded in the protocol change log with its reason
and the owner's approval (FR-044).

**Development inputs.** Keys, nonces, personalization strings and test states are development
inputs, derived from public strings by a rule the plan fixes before their first use, as feature
002 derived its seeds. No held-out key set exists in this feature. Beside them, a closed class
of constructed inputs is allowed, each fixed by a public rule before its first use (research R3;
data-model §2): (1) the fixed patterns of FR-058 and research R8 (all-zero, all-one,
alternating, counting; the single-bit keys and nonces e_i for every i); (2) the edge states of
FR-054, from the design document's list; (3) the avalanche flips x ⊕ e_j, the key and nonce bit
flips and the word rotations of development inputs, for every j, bit and k the rule names; (4)
feature 002's `dev` seeds for FUT-SEC-8, the one external development set; and (5) synthetic counts
for the bias test's validation only, drawn by a public rule from seeds hashed from public strings
(research R3). No value or index is picked after any output is seen. A run that is not evidence,
such as the 60 s projection before a heavy run or any timing probe, writes a record of kind
`projection` with its inputs (the first inputs of the run it projects); its outputs other than
timing are discarded unread, the record says so, and its time counts against the budget row of the
run it projects. *Stricter means*: the derivation does not change.

**FUT-SEC-8 demonstration.**

| Item | Rule |
|------|------|
| Targets | The first three seeds of feature 002's `dev` set |
| Attacker's observations | The first two seed words of the target, and the first three output words after the 256-step warm-up |
| Search | Every pair (s₂, s₃) in 0…65535², once per target; a candidate is dropped at its first mismatching word |
| Success | The target is among the candidates that match all three words |
| Random baseline | False matches expected per target: about 1 after one word (2³² candidates, 32-bit words), about 2⁻³² after two, about 2⁻⁶⁴ after three. Agreement is tested on the one-word count of each target against a Poisson distribution of mean (2³² − 1)/2³², and of the three pooled against three times that mean, two-sided at level 0.01; any false match after two or three words is reported as a deviation. The baseline assumes each candidate's output words uniform and independent of the target's; `phyco-v2`'s biases (S12, S14) could make false matches more frequent, so a count above the baseline is an observation about `phyco-v2`, reported as such, and does not change the success criterion. A disagreement is a finding (FR-060), never a reason to sweep again |
| Cost | Steps and time per candidate, measured; the total; the extrapolation to 2⁶⁴ candidates. Expected: about 2³² × 257 steps per target, about 3.6 core-hours at 8.5 × 10⁷ steps/s |
| Check before the sweep | The search tool reproduces the `phyco-v2` golden vectors |
| Budget | One pass over the 2³² candidates per target, for three targets, with its vector check, and nothing else. A repeat, only after a tool defect is fixed (Edge Cases) or a pass ended from outside (research R7), its vector check included, is a heavy run charged to research R7's feature-wide row: it projects first, is refused, exit 2, once its projection does not fit the hours left there, and runs only on the owner's request, quoted with its date in its record as `owner_request`, so that the agent never starts one; each repeat follows a defect or an ended pass that no earlier record names, and the feature-wide row's hours bound their number. Checking a record's listed matches and recovered seeds runs no sweep and is not a repeat. Nor is a reproduction after the FUT-SEC-8 result review: a heavy run that writes its own record, compared field by field with the original, which can neither replace the demonstration record nor change the review; a difference is a finding |
| Report | security.md §12.4, with T1 as the attacker, who knows the position of the observed words |

*Stricter means*: no change.

**Round rule.** For every claimable size,

```text
R = max(2 · R_dist, R_bound + Δ, 2 · max(R_f, R_b))
```

Every term counts rounds, a round being one pass over every colour phase (FR-029), and every term
comes from the production width at that size; a reduced-variant result is evidence about the
design, never a value of the rule (FR-032).

- R_f, R_b: from the exact dependency matrices (FR-029).
- R_bound: the smallest number of rounds after which the proven bound on the best differential
  trail probability and the proven bound on the best linear trail's squared correlation are both
  below 2⁻¹²⁸, from bounds (FR-038) that hold at the production width and that size. If it is
  not established, the size cannot be frozen. Before selection, R_bound at each claimable size comes
  from a bound computed at that size; a proof that carries a bound to a larger size is written for
  the selected candidate only and serves as a check beside the computed bounds (FR-038). The
  searches at the production width climb as research R6's do at the reduced widths, so that nobody
  picks where they stop: at each claimable size each candidate's bounds are searched at r = 2, 3, 4
  and so on, one search per kind and r, each a search instance of research R7 under its 8 h cap, an
  ARX candidate's box bounds over one call of each box, a chain of t calls costing t times one
  call. The climb at a size ends at the first r at which both bounds lie below 2⁻¹²⁸, which is
  R_bound there, or at the first search of either kind that reaches its cap or that its trail row
  refuses, the refusal written in that search's `projection` record, which leaves the candidate
  without R_bound at that size.
- R_dist: the largest number of rounds of Π or Π⁻¹ for which this feature's evidence shows a
  distinguisher costing less than 2¹²⁸ evaluations of Π or Π⁻¹ (FR-004): incomplete diffusion,
  a bias that meets the bias rule below, a probabilistic invariant (FR-037), a trail of Π found with
  probability or squared correlation above 2⁻¹²⁸ (a characteristic, its difference or mask after
  every round with its exact weight, exhibited by a validated tool that searches Π at the production
  width and that size and held in a `trail` record, while a bound of any kind enters R only
  through R_bound; FR-038), or a degree-based distinguisher (zero-sum, cube,
  or an inside-out zero-sum over r₁ rounds of Π and r₂ of Π⁻¹ from a middle state) that the
  candidate's `degree` records admit by the stated rule's counts, w − 1 per addition, which are kept
  where they are not upper bounds, computed per candidate and claimable size before
  this rule is applied (FR-034; research R13). Feature 005's campaign may
  raise R_dist; a higher R is then a new model ID (FR-028).
- Δ = 2. It may only be raised.

At each size the kinds of evidence run in this order: trail bounds (R_bound), then the structural
checks (R_f, R_b, symmetries, invariants and the `degree` records), then the frequency test, then
the avalanche test (plan phase 5; research R4). The bias tests start at the R the earlier kinds
give, max(2 · R_dist, R_bound + Δ, 2 · max(R_f, R_b)), and R rises as research R4 says. Any later
rise, from either bias test, a re-run after a reproduction's finding or a stricter change, makes
every record that depends on R again at the new variant, each a new measurement of its kind, charged
to its budget row where research R7 budgets the kind, in research R7's order: first the constant
table for the new R with its checks (research R15); then the inverse and invariant checks; then the
`cost` records and the reference outputs (research R5, R11); then every bias test already run at
that size, extended to the new R and charged to the 12 h row. The propagation through instantiate
and its margin over R_f are computed again (FR-029). A check made again that fails or fires raises R
again or makes the candidate `ineligible`, as its kind says, and the sequence repeats until R no
longer rises; if a row cannot hold a measurement it needs, that evidence is inconclusive and the
candidate `ineligible`. Where the call types draw on distinct constant streams, a rise changes the
constants of every call type after the first in research R15's reading order from round 1, so their
results are measured again from round 1, and every record names the constant table it read by its
SHA-256 (FR-023).

*Stricter means*: Δ only rises, and sources of R_dist are only added.

**Bias rule.** At each size and round count, a state bit's frequency of ones counts as a
distinguisher if its two-sided binomial p-value is at most 0.005/S, where S is the number of bits
tested at that size and round count on one call type's permutation, S = 4b
(research R4); where the call types draw on distinct constant streams (FR-023),
each call type's permutation is tested at its own threshold, and a firing in
any of them raises R. The number of development inputs per measurement is
fixed in the plan before any measurement. By the Bonferroni inequality, the rule
fires on uniform states with probability at most 0.005 for one permutation.
Before its first use at each size, it is applied once to the same number of
uniform states, derived from public strings by SHA-256 as the development
inputs are; if it fires there, the bias evidence of that size is inconclusive (security.md §11.6);
a control ended from outside gives no result and runs again on the same inputs (research R7).
The hours of this uniform control are charged to every entered candidate's 12 h row, whether the
control ran for it or before it (research R7), and the control runs once every row it counts can
hold them, each charged them in full: the 12 h row of every entered candidate, except a candidate
`withdrawn`, `rejected` or `ineligible`, whose row is charged no control that runs after that
decision, and a candidate whose row cannot hold the hours, which takes no test of that kind at that
size, its evidence there inconclusive, and is charged nothing for it (research R4,
R7; ANA-074); a decision that this brings is classed as "Eligibility and
selection" states, shared where the control's hours exceed the row's value and
the candidate's own where its earlier charges left too little (ANA-078).
A firing is reported as a distinguisher at its round count that raises R,
with the family-wise rate of chance firings for the call types tested stated beside it,
over the pairs of a size and a round count tested up to that firing's record and the avalanche runs
made up to it, size by size, with research R4's m/64 at a size where more than 128 round counts are
measured (FR-028; ANA-075, ANA-079), and it is never measured again to remove it. At each size
it runs after the trail bounds and the structural checks, from the R they
give, and a later rise of R extends it to the new R (round rule). *Stricter means*: only a change
that can add firings and never remove one. Every
firing recorded under the rule then in force stands, and a larger N, N_a or F, or a new set, is
tested under its new S or S_A in addition, so R can only rise.

**Nonlinearity choice rule (D4).** Each family is represented by its best candidate on reduced
widths. The family chosen is the one with the larger proven weight per round (−log₂ of the
weaker of the differential and linear bounds, per round, over the rounds computed) per unit of
estimated hardware cost (LUTs per node and round, from an operation-count model the plan fixes
before any candidate is scored). The width and r* are research R6's: the widest of 16 and 8 at which
at least one entered candidate of each family has both a differential and a linear trail bound for a
common number of rounds of at least 2, r* being the largest such common number, "common" meaning
common to the two bounds of each scored candidate and to the scored candidates of both families, so
that at a width where both families qualify each has a scored candidate at r*. Where no width has a
number of rounds common to both families, D4 chooses by default the family whose candidate has both
bounds at the same number of rounds, at least 2, at the widest width at which any candidate has
them, 16 before 8, and, where both families have such a candidate at that width, the family whose
candidate has them at the larger number of rounds there, the two numbers never being equal, since an
equal number would be common; that family is scored at that width and at r*, the largest such number
of rounds it has there, its candidates with both bounds at r* rounds scored, the highest being its
representative, with the other family's outcomes listed (research R6, "One family scored"): a family
that has one only at 16 is chosen over a family that has one only at 8, and where at 16 and at 8
only one family has one, that family is chosen. This is the conservative reading, as ANA-082's is:
it sends one family on wherever either has reduced-width evidence, never both. D4 is
not computable only where no candidate of either family has both bounds at
the same number of rounds, at least 2, at 16 or at 8 (research R6; ANA-082;
ANA-084). The rotation amounts each candidate is scored with are ranked by
the score's own measure, the −log₂ of the weaker of the two bounds at the search width, at one
number of rounds for every candidate, fixed in the protocol change log before the first rotation
search (FR-024). Only bound records of a tool that has passed its cross-check on
that candidate count for the choice, the cross-checks running first in the
candidate's trail row, on the first amounts of its recorded space (FR-039;
research R7, R12). Ties go to the smaller R from the round rule, then to fewer
serial carry chains per round, then to the owner's recorded choice. That choice is made only after
the tie record (`d4/tie.json`) exists, never changes, and is recorded beside it, in
`d4/tie-choice.json`, which `d4_choice.py --choose` writes against the SHA-256 of the record the
owner saw, and in its own row of the Review & Approval table ("D4 tie"), with the date and both
records' SHA-256; the tie record stays as the script wrote it. The production-width analysis then
runs on the chosen family. The other family is analysed at the production width only if the chosen
family ends with no eligible candidate, once every candidate of the chosen family has its final
decision (Eligibility and selection), in research R7's order, its rows reserved meanwhile; if the
chosen family has an eligible candidate, each candidate of the other family not analysed there gets
`ineligible`, with the reason "not analysed at the production width: D4 chose the other family",
which the final report states. Where D4 is not computable both families are analysed. An exact tie
sends both representatives on (research R6): while it waits, the two alone are analysed at the
production width, at 4×4 and only as far as R at 4×4 needs, and every other candidate of both
families waits; once the tie-breaks choose, the chosen family goes on as after any D4 choice, and
the losing representative stops, its records standing: it is its family's fallback with the family's
other candidates, and at the set's close it takes `ineligible`, with the reason "D4 chose the other
family by its tie-breaks", unless the chosen family ends with no eligible candidate, when its family
is analysed as research R7 states (RAT-072). *Stricter means*: no change.

**Tool validation.** A tool's result, positive or negative, counts only after its validation
record exists (FR-039). *Stricter means*: known results and re-validation triggers are only
added.

**Eligibility and selection.** A candidate is eligible for the freeze only if it passes every
structural test (FR-037), has an established R_bound at every claimable size, from a bound
computed at that size (FR-038), and meets FR-018 to FR-031, each item decided
for every candidate before selection by `round_rule.py` from its operation
list, log entries and records (data-model §9): FR-018's folding rule, longest link, food nodes with
their reason and tree figures from its operation list and `parameters` entry; FR-020's cycles per
call, with FR-026's figures, from its `cost` records per size; FR-022's bounds from its bound
records; FR-023's statement on the streams, with its reason, and FR-024's report on the ranking
after scaling from its log entries; FR-025's stages, chains per phase and key path from its
operation list's declarations, checked by code; FR-027's semantics from its operation list's
declarations; and FR-030's mapping from its log entry. FR-025's interface formats, call sequence and
zeroization rules are properties of the mode, the same for every candidate, decided once in the mode
chapter, and the self-test's expected output (FR-018) is generated from the selected candidate's
vectors and cannot fail. A missing item fails eligibility. The design document restates the items
for the selected candidate, and the owner design review checks that the two agree. "Passes"
means that every measurement is conclusive (its controls did not fire, no tool defect is left
unresolved and no reproduction that differs from its original on a field that enters a verdict is
unresolved; FR-053), whatever R_dist it gives, and that R_dist, R_f, R_b and
R_bound are established at every claimable size; inconclusive bias evidence at a size makes the
candidate ineligible. Of the eligible candidates of the chosen family, or of the other family if the
chosen one has none, or, where the D4 record's outcome is `not computable`, of both
families, the one with the smallest R at 4×4 is frozen; a tie goes to the owner's
recorded choice, made only after the selection record (`selection.json`, or a
later `selection-<k>.json`) exists, never changed, and recorded beside it, in
`freeze/selection-tie-choice.json` (`freeze/selection-<k>-tie-choice.json`), which `round_rule.py
--choose` writes against the SHA-256 of the record the owner saw, and in its own row of the Review &
Approval table ("Selection tie", or "Selection tie <k>"), with the date and both records' SHA-256; a
choice binds only the record whose hash it names, so a new tie needs a new choice, and every
selection record stays as the script wrote it. Any change before the freeze that touches any
candidate's eligibility or R at 4×4 after a selection record exists makes `round_rule.py --select`
run again over the whole set, writing `selection-<k>.json`, k = 2, 3, …, which cites the previous
record; earlier records stay, and a decline's re-selection is one such record. If another candidate
is selected, the security-target review and every later review are held again for it, each in a row
of its own; if the same one is, the reviews held stand for what did not change (FR-043). Every
candidate stays in the log (FR-041). After each selection record, `round_rule.py --decide` appends
one `decision` entry `selected` for the candidate the record selects, with its `variant_map` and the
operation list's SHA-256, and one `not selected` entry for each other candidate the record lists as
eligible, each citing the record by path and SHA-256 and its tie choice where there is one; it exits
2, appending nothing, while the record's outcome is a stop, while the record has a tie and no choice
whose row, "Selection tie" or "Selection tie <k>", reads Approved at HEAD with the choice record's
SHA-256, or once the record has its entries; and a later selection record gets entries of its own,
whether it selects another candidate or the same one, so that no entry is edited, the latest
`selected` entry being the one in force and an earlier one standing as a decision that a later
record superseded (FR-043; ANA-081). Candidates are entered before any is scored
(the entered set of research R6), and a further candidate is entered later only in place of one that
became `rejected` or `ineligible`, never because another's R is high: one is entered for every such
candidate while its family has fewer than three evaluated candidates and the budget allows, proposed
by the agent, its entry approved by the owner, and the candidate log's `replacement` entry,
`pending` from the decision until the replacement's `proposed` entry or a
stated reason (the family full, the budget spent, or "the owner declined the proposed
design") closes it, records each case (data-model §3), `candidate_log.py`
appending the decision with its `replacement` entry, the replacement's `proposed`
entry, written on the owner's explicit word quoted with its date in it, and a `replacement` entry
that closes a pending one with its reason (FR-041; ANA-080). A proposal the owner does not approve
is recorded, the owner's word quoted with its date, and `candidate_log.py replace` closes the
pending entry with the reason "the owner declined the proposed design", a reason
of its own beside the family full and the budget spent; the agent proposes
no other design for that entry, so that the set closes on the candidates
entered and `round_rule.py --close` runs as for any closed entry (ANA-087). The
entry is logged `pending` only with a decision whose cause lies in the candidate itself, its own
evidence or properties. A decision whose cause its family or the whole set shares calls for no
replacement, and its `replacement` entry is logged closed at once, with the reason "cause shared":
`ineligible` because D4 chose the other family, by its score or its tie-breaks, and the decisions
that a fired uniform control, the lack of a validated trail route or a match in the shared
polynomial bring, which end in the stop rule's recommendation for every candidate; a
candidate of the family D4 did not choose that is analysed after all takes a replacement only for a
decision of its own (research R7, "The other family"; ANA-073). An `ineligible` decision for a row
that cannot hold a run the rules require of every candidate at that size, a uniform control's hours,
a stricter change's new measurement or a trail search at 16×16, is classed by that run's recorded
need: shared where the need exceeds the row's value of research R7, at its current value after any
lowering, which every replacement's row of that value shares, the decision's `replacement` entry
then being logged closed at once with the reason "cause shared", and the
candidate's own where the candidate's earlier charges to that row left too little
(ANA-078). The set closes at a `set-closed` entry of the candidate log, written by
`round_rule.py --close` once every candidate has a final decision and no replacement is pending, and
selection runs only after it. A final decision is `withdrawn`, `rejected` or `ineligible`, or, for
an eligible candidate, its round-rule record at every claimable size; a candidate of
the family D4 did not choose, the representative that lost an exact D4 tie
among them, gets its final decision as the nonlinearity choice rule says
(RAT-072). A decline at the owner design review reopens the set once, for one
further candidate that the rules can select (FR-043): it comes from the chosen family, or from the
other family only where the declined candidate was selected by the fallback or D4 was not
computable; it is entered only while its family has fewer than three evaluated candidates;
and before its first entry the decline's log entry states what the rules
need to select it, an R at 4×4 below the declined one's or a tie. Where no further candidate can be
entered, the decline ends in one of FR-043's other outcomes, and a further candidate that
becomes `rejected` or `ineligible` is replaced the same way, within the one
reopening. A stricter change applies to every candidate, those already evaluated
included, and the verdicts are recomputed under it (FR-044). *Stricter means*: eligibility items are
only added; the selection rule does not change.

**Stop rule.** At most three candidates of each family (D4) are evaluated. A candidate is a
distinct design of Π with its own entry in the candidate log (FR-041), eligible or not, and it
counts as evaluated from its first entry, `proposed`, which comes before any tool runs
on it; a fourth `proposed` entry of a family is refused, `candidate_log.py
propose` exiting 2 and appending nothing (ANA-080). A later candidate is entered
only in place of one `rejected` or `ineligible`, one for each such candidate
while its family has fewer than three evaluated candidates and the budget
allows, or once after a decline at the owner design review where the rules can
select it, never because another's R is high, and none after the set closes (Eligibility and
selection). The budget allows a replacement while each of its own rows of research R7, at its
current value after any lowering, holds more than zero hours and at least the recorded need of each
run that the rules require of every candidate at that size and that would be charged to it, a
uniform control's hours, a stricter change's new measurement or a trail search at 16×16, so that a
replacement is not entered only to end as the candidate it replaces did (ANA-078); "the budget
spent", the reason that closes a `replacement` entry otherwise, means that a row of the further
candidate, at its current value, is at zero after a lowering or below the recorded need of such a
run, which the log writer, `candidate_log.py replace`, accepts only when R7's
rows, the record of that need and, for a lowered row, the protocol change
log's entry that lowered the budget show it, exit 2 otherwise (data-model §3;
ANA-078). A decision whose cause its family or the whole set shares calls for no
replacement: its `replacement` entry is logged closed at once, with the reason "cause shared"
(Eligibility and selection; ANA-073). A change makes a new candidate if it touches the
operation list's structure: the nonlinear component, the round structure
(its operations, their forms or order), the colour schedule, or the rule that generates the
constants or the rotation amounts. It is a variant or a fix if it changes only values the
candidate's logged rules derive (w, n, R, c), or corrects a value to what those rules give; so a
reduced variant, a round count, or a fix to the model that leaves the design unchanged is not a new
candidate. When in doubt, it is a new candidate. The analysis budget per candidate, such as the
solver time for trail bounds, is fixed in the plan before the first candidate is scored. The feature
recommends stopping (security.md §21, option C) if none of the evaluated candidates is
eligible, or if no published theorem's preconditions can be met by the mode,
or shown met, as when no theorem of research R10's list that covers a call
has its paper on disk and read (Edge Cases). No candidate is eligible, for
example, when an attack below the target reaches the full rounds of every candidate, or when R_bound
is not established at the production width for any of them (strategy note §10; D10). The budget may
only be lowered (FR-044): a budget lowered below the hours already used leaves zero hours, and a
new measurement under a stricter rule counts against the same budget row. A stop is a valid,
reportable outcome, reported with what was reached. After a stop, a later feature may evaluate
further candidates only under its own pre-registered protocol and new model IDs, and it counts
this feature's candidates, results and stop in its selection bias. *Stricter means*: budgets
and the candidate limit only fall.

### Re-decided feature-002 requirements

| Feature-002 requirement | Rule in feature 002 | Decision for `phyco-v3` | Reason |
|-------------------------|---------------------|-------------------------|--------|
| FR-001 | Integer operations only: add, subtract, XOR, AND, OR, NOT, constant shifts and rotations, comparisons, table lookups, conditional selection; no multiplication, division, modulo or power | Kept and made stricter: in Π and the mode's datapath only add, subtract, XOR, AND, OR, NOT, constant shifts and constant rotations; no multiplication, division, modulo or power; no table lookup, comparison or selection on secret data; comparisons only on public counters such as the output limits; no arithmetic shift, signed comparison or sign extension without the justification of FR-055 | Fixed operations and constant time (FR-020); no signedness corners (FR-055); S6, S12 |
| FR-002 | Every value at most 32 bits, with signedness, wrap and comparison semantics stated | Kept for every datapath word (w = 32; reduced widths 4, 8 and 16). A public counter wider than 32 bits, such as an output-limit counter, is held as 32-bit words with a stated carry rule; it counts as a carry chain where it sits on a stage's path, and not when it is registered on a path of its own (FR-025) | Principle I; checklist group E |
| FR-003 | Keep the `lut-v1` table, phase increments and quarter-period offset | Dropped: round constants by a stated rule, never repeated within a call wherever the aperiodic classes allow, as at every claimable size (FR-023), replace the public 128-step forcing | S2; slide attacks (group F) |
| FR-004 | Keep the bio-inspired structure, including a threshold-triggered disruption | Revised: the structure stays as a documented mapping (FR-030), without the threshold-triggered disruption | D5; FR-020 |
| FR-005 | Disruption on 1–50% of steps | Withdrawn with the disruption | D5 |
| FR-006 | 32-bit unsigned output words from the generator's own state | Revised: the output is the rate part after a full call of Π, in one canonical serialization | FR-015; S5 |
| FR-007 | No cryptographic primitive, standard PRNG or value derived from one | Re-decided (D6). Allowed: a standard mode with a published proof, with its padding and domain separation; published design and analysis methods; a plain counter, which is not a PRNG. Forbidden in Π, the mode and key loading: any published permutation, cipher, hash, MAC or standard PRNG, any component taken from one, and their constants. Constants come from a stated rule in a committed script that reproduces no published primitive's constants. The script holds a committed list of the polynomials and constant sets it refuses, each with its source, frozen with the constant rule before the first candidate is scored: those of the reference designs of research R12 and of every design the strategy note, the spec, the plan, research.md and security.md name, and the standard polynomials of degree 64 and 128 (CMAC's R₆₄ and R₁₂₈, GCM's, and the CRC-64 polynomials of ISO 3309 and ECMA-182). A design that the rationale, the spec, the plan, research.md or security.md names later is added when named, as a stricter change the owner approves (FR-044). The check fails if the chosen polynomial is listed, or if a table contains a listed sequence: a run of consecutive table words of at least 64 bits equal to as many consecutive w-bit words of a listed constant set, read as one bit string from any multiple of w, under one of 2w transforms, each rotating every word of the run by the same k, k = 0…w − 1, with or without the complement of every word, so that rotations of complements match, over every table and each call type's stream (FR-023). A match makes each candidate it touches ineligible, one in the shared polynomial ends in a stop, a match after the freeze is a finding (FR-051), and the rule is never changed to escape it | Checklist group C; feature-002 O2 |
| FR-008 | Bit-exact model; golden vectors for at least 2 development seeds × 10,000 words; an RTL-consumable description; RTL out of scope | Kept and extended to every size (FR-035, FR-036); RTL out of scope | Principle II; FUT-GRID-5 |
| FR-009 | Seed domain of four integers in 0…65535 | Replaced by a 256-bit key and a 128-bit nonce (FR-006); `phyco-v2` keeps its seed domain | FUT-SEC-1; S1 |

### Security Policy traceability

In the last column, **met by specification** means that a cited section of the design document
(version and section) states how the requirement is met, that nothing remains to measure, and
that the owner has accepted that section, at the security-target review that records L1 for
FUT-SEC-1 to FUT-SEC-6 and FUT-SEC-9 (FR-012) or else at the owner design review. **Met by
evidence** means that a record of FR-061 shows it, computed by code (FR-065). **Met by
statement** means that the requirement applies only under a condition, and a cited decision
states that the condition does not hold. The Evidence column cites the section or the record.
**Done**, used for FUT-SEC-8 alone, means that the demonstration record the FUT-SEC-8 result
review names (data-model §4) exists as the search tool's validation record
(FR-003), that every development target is recovered as SC-001 states, that
security.md's best-attack register row for exhaustive seed search cites the record, and that the
"FUT-SEC-8 result review" row reads Approved at HEAD; SC-002 and security.md §15 use it in that
sense. **Partly met** means that the requirement's other clauses are met in one of the three terms
above and that each clause not met is named in the same cell as open, with the feature or open item
that takes it, as the FUT-SEC-7 and FUT-GRID-1 cells do.

| Requirement | Met in this feature by | Evidence | Expected status at the end |
|-------------|------------------------|----------|----------------------------|
| FUT-SEC-1 Key entropy | FR-005, FR-006, FR-010 | Security target; parameter table. FR-010's "key plus nonce at least 2s" and "reachable secret state at least 2s" answer the time–memory–data trade-offs on the key and on the state, the attack security.md applies to `phyco-v2`'s seed (S3, §5) | Met by specification |
| FUT-SEC-2 Reachable secret state | FR-009, FR-010, FR-015, FR-017 | Parameter table per size; the 2×2 decision | Met by specification at every claimable size |
| FUT-SEC-3 Stream separation | FR-006, FR-014 | Nonce rule; domain separation | Met by specification |
| FUT-SEC-4 Reseeding | FR-004, FR-016 | Prediction resistance not sought (D7) | Met by statement: it applies only if prediction resistance is claimed, and D7 does not seek it |
| FUT-SEC-5 Initialization analysis | FR-013, FR-014, FR-029, FR-049 | Key loading specified; diffusion through instantiation; the first-order related-key and related-nonce differences of research R4, reported as "a single-difference avalanche measurement, not a related-key analysis" | Met by specification for key loading, and by evidence for diffusion through instantiation (FR-029) and research R4's first-order related-key and related-nonce differences; related-key and related-IV attacks beyond first order, weak-key classes and key-dependent effects open for feature 005 (FR-049) |

This feature supplies the implied throughput only |

Design-level criteria: the cycle formulas (FR-020), the port list with every port's direction, width and values after configuration and after reset, against which no read-back path is checked, and the command interface's format, the zeroization list with its error codes, and the call-state table with its `not-reset`, `resetting`, `loading`, `generating`, `zeroizing` and `failed` states and the malformed command words (FR-025; HW-087, HW-090); the counting rule on every interface, every cycle of a call but those in which the core waits for the host, measured at the ports from a call's start to the cycle in which `call_done` rises (FR-020; HW-086), a call starting at a command word taken while `call_done` is high, and a word refused while a call runs, which starts none and leaves the running call's count as it would be without it, among the tests (FR-020; HW-091) | Open for feature 008, whose spec lists the board tests, among them key zeroization on reset, tested from the ports (after a reset, an instantiate is refused as `no-key`), and says whether side-channel measurement equipment exists; if not, side channels are recorded as untested |

### Key Entities

- **Security target**: properties sought, s, key and nonce sizes, output limits, u, threats
  adopted or excluded, the mode's theorem and its preconditions, the generic-bound table, the
  certification route.
- **Size profile**: n, w, lanes per node, b, c, r, R, key entropy, reachable secret state, bound,
  status (claimable or research variant), variant ID.
- **Permutation Π**: design version and hash, round count, constants, and its inverse Π⁻¹.
- **Mode**: calls, padding, domain separators, output limits, output serialization.
- **Candidate design**: ID, family, parameters, evidence, decision, reason, date.
- **Variant**: variant ID, parameters, and whether the production interface accepts it.
- **Vector file**: size, variant, inputs, outputs, SHA-256.
- **Structural-evidence record**: kind (inverse, bijectivity, dependency, bias, symmetry,
  invariant), parameters, result, command, hashes.
- **Trail-bound record**: rounds, width, size, kind (differential or linear), bound, method,
  tool, validation reference.
- **Tool validation record**: tool, version, licence, the known result re-found, outcome, date.
- **Demonstration record**: targets, revealed words, matches after one, two and three words, cost
  per candidate, total, extrapolation, machine, commands, hashes.
- **Design freeze record**: hashes of the design document, `rationale.md`, model, vectors
  (constructed-state vectors included) with the self-test data copied from them, the constant and
  vector scripts, the selected operation list, `research/004/ir.py` and the verdict scripts; R and
  its inputs; the last `seq` and line hash of each log; tool versions; `git_head`, `git_dirty`; the
  draft's SHA-256; `freeze_status` (`draft`, then `approved`) and the owner's approval, written by
  `design_freeze.py approve`, and the common `status`, computed by code (FR-043; REP-093).
- **Baseline archive**: the `phyco-v2` files, with SHA-256 and source, in a manifest.
- **Constructed-state vector**: the edge state, how it was constructed, whether any key reaches
  it, inputs, outputs, SHA-256.
- **Evidence record** (every record above): the fields of security.md §17.2 and the disclaimer
  (FR-061).
- **Provenance record**: file, tool and model, date, task ID, review status.
- **Claim and evidence register**: model ID, claim level, claim or "no claim", bound, evidence
  records, and the allow-list of flagged lines, each with its reason (FR-069).
- **Protocol change log**: one chained, append-only entry per change of a pre-registered rule:
  date, rule, old and new text, reason, direction, the readings compared for a settled case,
  approver, the last record ID before it and the results known then (FR-044).
- **Review package**: the items of security.md §14, with exact revisions.

## Success Criteria *(mandatory)*

### Measurable Outcomes

- **SC-001**: For each of the 3 targets, the FUT-SEC-8 sweep recovers the target seed within one
  pass over 2³² candidates, the matches after one, two and three words are reported against the
  baseline, and the owner's review is recorded before any `phyco-v3` instantiation code exists.
- **SC-002**: 9 of 9 FUT-SEC requirements are traced to requirements, tasks and evidence, with
  the statuses of table "Security Policy traceability" in the terms it defines: FUT-SEC-1,
  FUT-SEC-2, FUT-SEC-3, FUT-SEC-6 and FUT-SEC-9 are met by specification; FUT-SEC-4 is met by
  statement (D7); FUT-SEC-5 is met by specification for key loading and by evidence for diffusion
  through instantiation and the first-order related-key and related-nonce differences, with its
  analysis open for feature 005 (FR-049); FUT-SEC-8 is done; and FUT-SEC-7 is listed as open.
- **SC-003**: Every claimable size satisfies every generic bound of security.md §12.2, with 0
  violations, every check of `parameters.json` that can fail passes (each output limit at most
  what the theorem allows at ε, k/2 ≥ s, and the stated nonce-reuse behaviour with its test),
  and every supported size states its key entropy, reachable secret state and bound.
- **SC-004**: Π⁻¹(Π(x)) = x and Π(Π⁻¹(x)) = x hold on 100% of the test inputs of research R13
  (the 16 vector inputs and 2¹⁰ development test states, counts that may only rise) at every
  supported size and variant, and the smallest variant maps its 2³² inputs to 2³² distinct
  outputs.
- **SC-005**: R_f and R_b are reported for 100% of supported sizes, and R at every claimable size
  equals the round rule's value, with every input cited.
- **SC-006**: The committed script reproduces 100% of the constants on both platforms of
  SC-009; within one call no two constants are equal, equal up to a word rotation, complements
  of one another, or rotations of complements, wherever the aperiodic classes cover the call, as at
  every claimable size, and every repeat elsewhere is recorded (FR-023); and 0 non-trivial grid
  translations or rotations preserve any constant pattern at any supported size.
- **SC-007**: 100% of counted tool results have a validation record older than they are: its
  `created_utc` is earlier, and the counted record cites its ID and SHA-256. Every counted tool has
  a known result (FR-039): S12, S13, S14, S15 and the FUT-SEC-8 recovery are re-found on `phyco-v2`,
  each by the tool research R13 names for it; each trail tool re-finds its published value (the ARX
  search, the active-S-box counter, the long-trail counter and the exhaustive tables); the symmetry
  check, the dependency propagation, exhaustive bijectivity, the invariant checks, the avalanche
  test, the cost model and the degree derivation re-find their stated known results; and the bias
  test's planted bit fires in each of its two synthetic families at the rate FR-039 states; each by
  the comparison data-model §7 states for it.
- **SC-008**: Every claimable size has an established R_bound from recorded trail bounds, each
  citing its method, tool and validation.
- **SC-009**: 100% of the generated files (the vectors, known-answer tests, constants and
  constructed-state vectors) are reproduced on both Windows and Linux, the latter in WSL, as each
  generator's pair of check records, one per platform, shows, `gen_constructed_v3.py`'s pair also
  showing that `Generator`'s self-test passed on each platform (FR-036, FR-065; REP-086).
- **SC-010**: 100% of reduced-variant requests and 100% of requests beyond any limit of FR-007
  (output volume, calls of Π, instantiations per key, reseeds per instantiation) are refused by
  the production interface.
- **SC-011**: 0 candidate designs are missing from the candidate log, whether or not they
  produced evidence, 0 log entries are deleted, 0 held-out keys are created or used, and no
  family has more than three candidates in the log.
- **SC-012**: Every archived `phyco-v2` file matches its manifest hash and its live source, and
  every frozen file of features 002 and 003 still hashes as recorded.
- **SC-013**: The design rationale covers 100% of the items of security.md §8's rationale list,
  counted as the eight chapters of `rationale.md` that FR-034 fixes for them, each present and
  stating or citing its item (RAT-084), and the owner design review, the external
  review record and the design freeze are recorded; where the design changed
  after the external review, the freeze record and the final report state the version that review
  read and the versions since, and cite it for that version only (FR-043).
- **SC-014**: 9 of 9 carried-over feature-002 requirements and every item of the §5.3 checklist
  have a recorded disposition.
- **SC-015**: No report, document, code comment or docstring (in the files FR-069 scans)
  describes `phyco-v3`, or any model, as cryptographically secure, proven secure, unbreakable,
  random, truly random or high-entropy (FR-050), words the claim test flags in every
  line it scans (FR-069), or states a security strength as achieved, and
  every report carries the disclaimer.
- **SC-016**: 100% of the edge states the design document lists have a constructed-state
  vector, reproduced on both platforms of SC-009, and the second transcription agrees with the
  model at 100% of the steps of the committed vectors.
- **SC-017**: 100% of evidence records hold every field of security.md §17.2 and the disclaimer,
  100% of findings carry a classification of security.md §9.1 and every other record `none`, each
  computed by code (FR-060), and the re-check reproduces 100% of the verdicts on a fresh clone from
  committed files alone, on any machine, since it is exempt from the environment guard (FR-065); a
  reproduction that re-runs evidence stays in the environment that made its original (research R1).
- **SC-018**: The claim-register test reports 0 disagreements between the register, README,
  security.md and the disclaimers, 0 uses of forbidden wording outside the register's allow-list,
  and 0 strengths stated without "target" or a quoted rule outside it (FR-069).
- **SC-019**: 100% of the sections and items of the four input documents have a requirement or a
  recorded disposition (section "Input coverage"), and the final report maps 12 of 12 rules
  R1–R12 of the hardware note.
- **SC-020**: 100% of the files drafted or repaired with an AI tool have a provenance record, the
  review package holds 100% of the items of security.md §14, and 0 AI sessions are recorded as
  independent or external reviewers.

## Assumptions

- **Decisions**: the owner confirmed D1–D10 on 2026-11-01, with the stop rule of D10 changed to
  a budget of three candidates per family (section "Clarifications").
- **Machine and run times**: the 32-thread machine with WSL2 used in feature 003. The FUT-SEC-8
  sweep costs about 3.6 core-hours per target at the 8.5 × 10⁷ steps/s measured for the C stream
  (`reports/003-extended-batteries-calibration/dev/timing.json`): about 11 core-hours for three
  targets, under an hour of wall time on 32 threads. Exhaustive bijectivity over 2³² states and
  solver runs are heavy runs too (FR-045).
- **Search tool**: built from the existing C stream, so it needs no download, and checked
  against the golden vectors first (FR-002).
- **Analysis tools**: the solvers and trail-search tools of strategy note §6.4 are optional
  downloads, each needing permission. If none is approved, bounds come from self-written
  exhaustive or branch-and-bound searches, validated in the same way (FR-039). Analysis tools
  are never imported by the core model.
- **AIS 20/31 version 3.0** (D8) is among the downloads the owner allowed (research R19), and
  none has been made yet. Until it is read, the route stays named with "class unconfirmed"
  beside it, an open item closed at the security-target review once the class is confirmed or
  changed in the design document (FR-011).
- **Key source**: the caller supplies a 256-bit key with full entropy, uniform and secret, from
  an established cryptographic random-number generator, never from a PhycoWeb-PRNG model. The
  generator cannot check this, and a key with less entropy caps the strength at that entropy
  (FR-006, FR-010).
- **Python model speed**: about 2 × 10⁵ words/s for `phyco-v2` at 2×2. A permutation of a large
  grid will be much slower, so the plan sets vector lengths at large sizes (FR-036).
- **Square grids only**; rectangular grids are out of scope.
- **No git branch** is created; the feature directory names the feature. The agent commits locally
  under the owner's standing authorization (Principle IX, constitution 1.3.0), at consistent
  checkpoints and with no AI co-author line, and no file of this feature from the first commit that
  records an owner design review's approval to the feature's end, plan phase 9 included, each later
  commit of such a file being the owner's (FR-043; SEC-084); only the owner pushes, tags, releases
  or publishes.
- **External review**: constitution 1.3.0 does not require an external design review, and
  security.md §7 recommends one. Arranging it is the owner's outward action (Principle IX).
- **Constitution**: 1.3.0 applies. The optional amendment of strategy note §9.1 and the
  Principle I wording update (checklist group E) are not part of this feature.
- **Change completion**: `phyco-v3` remains a design under evaluation. Of the constitution's
  change-completion list, the statistical evaluation (feature 007) and the RTL (features 006 and
  008) are still open when this feature ends (FR-052).
- **AI assistance**: as in features 001–003 (security.md §16.1), except that the agent commits
  locally under the standing authorization above: an AI coding agent drafts the documents and code
  and runs the tools, bound by the constitution as a human contributor is. Tools
  judge its drafts and the owner reviews them (FR-064, FR-065, FR-067).
- **Roles**: mathematics, cryptanalysis, hardware and independent review are not staffed
  (security.md §16.2) unless the owner arranges otherwise.
- **Repository layout**: new files follow security.md §18, and the plan names them. No
  `hardware/` directory is created, because this feature writes no RTL.
- **Tool versions**: those of security.md §19 are the baseline. A new tool is used only after its
  download is approved (FR-046), and its version is recorded with its first result (FR-061).
- **Out of scope**: RTL; the held-out statistical programme (feature 007); the full cryptanalysis
  campaign (feature 005); the board benchmark (feature 008); external review for L5, public
  reduced-round challenges, publication and any security claim (feature 009); a formal
  specification in Cryptol; an entropy source (D7); experiment E1; any change to `phyco-v2`,
  `lut-v1`, harness `sp80022`, the feature-002 and feature-003 protocols or their seed sets.
